# 계약 문구 대비 구현 점검 — 남은 두 묶음 (2026-11-13)

`docs/review-2026-11-05/contract-sweep-findings.md`의 '점검하지 못한 묶음'(`datasets·sharing·deid·assets`,
`reports·records·contracts·uploads`, 작업 72개)을 이어서 점검한 결과다. 작업 묶음 11개를 발견 담당 에이전트가 하나씩 맡아
`contracts/openapi.yaml`의 작업별 문구와 구현을 비교했고, 발견은 임시 DB에서 **직접 재현한 것만** 올리게 했다.
발견마다 서로 다른 검증 담당 둘(계약·코드를 읽어 반증 / 독립 재현)이 반증을 시도했다.
발견 47건 가운데 둘 다 반증하지 못한 것이 46건, 의견이 갈린 것이 1건이다.
발견 문구는 원문(영어)을 그대로 둔다. 심각도와 고칠 쪽은 검증 담당의 정정을 반영했다.

## 구현 반영

- **S01 큰 데이터셋 버전의 데이터 카드** — 작업 큐로 가는 큰 버전(항목이 `DATASET_SYNC_MAX_ITEMS` 초과)도 BUILDING 버전과 `dataset_build` 작업을 만들기 전에 데이터 카드를 매니페스트 계약의 `data_card`로 검사한다. 틀린 카드는 요청 안에서 만드는 경로와 같은 422 `RULE_VIOLATION`(`/data_card…` 포인터)을 받고 버전·작업이 남지 않아, 운영자의 `GET /v1/datasets/{id}`·`GET /v1/datasets`가 계약을 지킨다. ADR-0072.
- **S03 데이터셋 목록 쪽 나눔** — `limit`(기본 50, 최대 500)과 `cursor`대로 `dataset_id` 순으로 나누고 다음 쪽이 있으면 `next_cursor`를 준다. 이용기관에게는 보이는 버전(게시·L3 이상·READY)이 있는 데이터셋으로만 쪽을 채워 쪽이 짧거나 다음 쪽이 비지 않는다. 웹 운영 콘솔 데이터셋과 이용기관 카탈로그는 `?limit=500`으로 부른다. ADR-0072.
- **S04 계보 409** — 동작은 그대로 둔다(READY만 200, BUILDING·FAILED는 409 `CONFLICT`, 볼 수 없는 버전은 상태와 관계없이 404). 계약 `getDatasetVersionLineage`에 409 응답과 설명을 적었다.
- **S05 item_asset_ids 형식** — 항목이 `asset_id` 형식이 아니면 400 `VALIDATION_ERROR`와 `/item_asset_ids/<i>` 오류를 반환한다. 같은 ID가 다시 나오면 그 자리마다 400(`code: unique`)이다. 중복 ID를 조용히 합쳐 201을 주지 않고 멱등 기록도 남기지 않는다. 형식이 맞지만 없는 자산은 지금처럼 422 `RULE_VIOLATION`이다. ADR-0072.
- **S02 배포의 다른 기관 이용 권한** — 다른 이용기관의 이용 권한 ID로 `POST /v1/distributions`를 보내면 없는 ID와 같은 404를 반환하고 `CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT`(대상 `Grant`, 기관은 권한을 가진 이용기관)를 기록한다. 없는 ID에는 감사를 남기지 않는다. 주인 이용기관의 발급(201)·만료 권한 410은 그대로다.
- **S06 이용 권한 철회·파기 확인·이용 신청 결정의 다른 기관 요청** — 역할 범위 밖인 다른 기관의 권한·신청을 철회·파기 확인·결정하려 하면 같은 404와 `CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT`(대상 `Grant`·`AccessRequest`)를 기록하고 아무것도 바꾸지 않는다. 같은 기관인데 역할 범위 밖이면 교차 기관으로 보지 않는다(Tenant_Guard와 같은 규칙). 역할별 조회는 그대로다. ADR-0073.
- **S07 이용 신청 기간 날짜** — 달력에 없는 날짜(2월 30일·13월·0일)와 끝 날짜 9999-12-31은 500 대신 400 `VALIDATION_ERROR`와 `/period/start_date`·`/period/end_date` 오류를 반환하고 신청·감사를 남기지 않는다. 9999-12-30은 받는다. 계약 설명에 명시했다. ADR-0073.
- **S09 운영자 승인의 L3 미달** — 제공기관 승인 뒤 자산 등급이 내려가 버전이 L3 아래가 되면 운영자 승인이 422 `RULE_VIOLATION`인 것을 `decideAccessRequest` 계약(응답 목록·설명)에 명시했다. 결정과 이용 권한은 남지 않고 신청은 `PROVIDER_APPROVED`로 남는다.
- **S10 응답 기한 뒤 결정** — 기한(`deadline_at`)이 지난 신청의 제공기관·운영자 승인은 409 `INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION`(응답 기한 초과)으로 거부하고 결정·이용 권한을 만들지 않는다. 비교는 정비 주기와 같다(`deadline_at < 지금`). 거절은 받고, `DENIED` 전이는 정비 주기가 한다. 계약 설명에 명시했다. ADR-0073.
- **S11 분류 변경 뒤 공유 등급** — 운영자가 분류를 지정하면 그 자산과 파생본(원 자산의 가장 엄격한 sensitivity_class를 물려받음)을 다시 본다. 분류가 정하는 조건(sensitivity_class ≠ SECURITY·=INTERNAL, privacy_status 두 조건)을 못 맞추거나, 이번 변경으로 다른 조건을 새로 못 맞추게 되면 조건을 충족하는 가장 높은 등급으로 함께 내린다(L2 이상에서 SECURITY → L1, L4에서 RESTRICTED → L3 또는 L2). 수정 전 코드가 남긴 'L2인데 SECURITY' 행도 다시 지정하면 바로잡힌다. 분류와 무관한 조건(약정 만료 등)이 전부터 어긋났다는 것만으로는 내리지 않는다. 그 자산이 든 데이터셋 버전의 이용 권한은 즉시 철회한다. 내린 자산과 끊은 권한은 `ASSET_CLASSIFIED` details와 권한마다 `GRANT_REVOKED`로 남기고, 이 감사는 요청 트랜잭션에 쓴다. 자신·파생본은 계보 단계 순으로 잠그고, 비식별 검수 결정도 원본 → 비식별본으로 잠근다. 그래서 둘이 겹쳐도 교착하지 않는다. 계약 `classifyAsset` 설명에 적었다(ADR-0074). 권한 처리를 즉시 철회로 고정한 것은 소유자 결정 대상이다.
- **S12 재신청(RESUBMIT)** — REJECTED에서만 받는다. NONE 등에서는 역할과 상관없이 409 `INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION`이다. 심사 처리로 CANDIDATE가 되려면 data_role=BYPRODUCT여야 하고, 아니면 422(`data_role=BYPRODUCT`)다. 공유 후보 지정에서 제외된 CORE 원본은 심사 처리로도 후보가 되지 않는다.
- **S13 검수자의 공유 심사 접근** — 고치지 않음: 검증 의견이 갈렸다(한쪽은 계약 위반이 아니라고 봄). 고치는 방향도 소유자가 정할 일이다(검수자에게 배정 기반 자산 범위를 줄지, share:read·share:review에서 REVIEWER를 뺄지). 검수자의 QUALITY_REVIEW → REPROCESS는 비식별 검수 결정 경로로 이미 된다.
- **S14 심사 처리의 전이 주체 403** — share:review 권한은 있지만 그 전이의 주체가 아닌 역할이 받는 403에 `PERMISSION_DENIED` 감사(대상 자산, 동작, 전·후 상태)를 남긴다. 감사는 요청과 따로 확정한다. 표에 없는 전이(409)는 권한 거부로 남기지 않는다.
- **S15 등급 변경 신청의 409** — `createShareRequest` 계약에 두 409를 명시한다. 하나는 목표 등급이 지금 등급과 같을 때의 `INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION`, 다른 하나는 멱등 키 충돌(`IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT`, 처리 중이면 `CONFLICT`)이다. 동작은 그대로다.
- **S16 공유 후보 지정 본문** — 빈 `asset_ids`(session_id와 함께여도)와 중복 `asset_ids`는 400 `VALIDATION_ERROR`(`/asset_ids`, `too_short`·`unique`)다. 이때 어떤 자산도 후보가 되지 않는다.
- **S17 운영자 원본 프레임 거부 감사** — 긴급 열람 승인이 없는 운영자가 다른 기관 원본 프레임을 요청하면 403과 함께 `PERMISSION_DENIED`(대상 원본, `reason: no_break_glass_grant`, 검수 ID)를 남긴다. 감사는 요청과 따로 확정돼, 거절된 요청 ID로 대조할 수 있다.
- **S19 운영자 비식별본 프레임 범위** — 배정 검수자가 아닌 운영자는 원본 `share_status`가 PROCESSING·QUALITY_REVIEW·REPROCESS일 때만 비식별본 프레임을 받는다. 공유 철회·반려·승인 뒤에는 403과 `PERMISSION_DENIED`(`reason: not_under_review`)를 남긴다. 배정 검수자는 그대로 본다. 승인(APPROVED) 뒤에도 허용할지는 주인 결정 대기다(ADR-0075).
- **S20 같은 회차 재검수 중복** — 열린 검수가 있는 비식별본에는 재검수를 새로 만들지 않고, 그 검수 ID를 `recheck_review_ids`로 돌려준다. 결정한 비식별본 자신과 불합격(FAILED) 비식별본은 옛 PASSED 검수가 있어도 다시 열지 않는다. 같은 회차의 미탐 결정이 겹치면 비식별본 행을 잠근 뒤 다시 확인해 하나만 연다. 두 세션 동시 결정 시험으로 고정했다(ADR-0075).
- **S21 사람 태그 추가** — 모델이 단 자산 전체 태그를 사람이 추가하면 `source=HUMAN`(model_ref·confidence 없음)으로 바뀌어 날씨 보정이 지우지 않는다. 지운 것이 없는 REMOVE의 이력 기록은 계약이 정하지 않아 그대로 둔다.
- **S22 검증에서 실패한 자산의 재처리** — 무결성 검증이 재시도를 다 써 FAILED(failed_from=VALIDATING)가 된 자산을 재처리하면 검증 작업부터 다시 넣는다(이번 실행의 중복 키, 요청 트랜잭션). 검증이 이어서 끝나 등록·예약 정산·무결성 로그를 한 번 하고, 등록 뒤 보통 처리 순서가 돈다. 재처리는 상태를 보기 전에 자산 행을 잠그고 다시 읽는다. 그래서 겹친 늦은 요청이 그사이 등록된 자산을 VALIDATING으로 덮어쓰지 않는다(등록됐으면 보통 재처리, 검증 중이면 409). 응답 job_ids는 검증 작업이며 계약 reprocessAsset 설명에 적었다(ADR-0076).
- **S23 실패한 수정의 data_role 감사** — `DATA_ROLE_CHANGED`는 모든 검사와 마지막 flush를 통과한 뒤 요청 트랜잭션에 쓴다. 같은 요청의 회차 지정이 404·409·422로 실패하면 data_role 변경도 감사도 남지 않는다.
- **S24 data_role 변경 권한 거부** — ORG_ADMIN이 아닌 사용자의 data_role 변경 403은 `PERMISSION_DENIED`(action `asset:data_role`, from·to, 대상 자산)를 남긴다. 요청이 되돌아가도 남는다. 지금 값과 같은 data_role은 거부도 감사도 없다.
- **S25 CORE 변경의 하향·철회 감사** — 공유 등급을 내린 자산마다 `SHARE_SCOPE_DOWNGRADED`(from·to·`REVOKE_NOW`·원 자산·끊긴 권한 ID)를, 끊긴 이용 권한마다 `GRANT_REVOKED`(대상 기관은 이용 기관)를 같은 트랜잭션에 남긴다(ADR-0076). 공유 심사 WITHDRAW와 공유 등급 신청의 하향에는 권한별 `GRANT_REVOKED`가 아직 없다.
- **S26 다른 실증사업 회차 지정** — 422 RULE_VIOLATION(condition `session.project_id=asset.project_id`)을 계약 patchAsset의 응답과 설명에 적었다. 구현은 그대로다.
- **S27 삭제 증명 조회** — 다른 기관 ORG_ADMIN이 삭제 증명을 조회하면 404를 주고 `CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT` 감사(대상 `DeletionCertificate`, 소유 기관, 경로)를 남긴다. 소유 기관 ORG_ADMIN과 운영자는 그대로 200이고, 없는 증명은 감사 없이 404다. 증명의 `deleted_at`(삭제한 시각)을 Tenant_Guard가 삭제 표시로 보므로 `load_scoped` 대신 처리기에서 감사한다.
- **S28 운영자 다운로드 URL** — 유효한 긴급 열람 승인이 없는 운영자 요청(승인 없음·승인 전·만료·승인자가 남의 승인 사용)은 403을 주고 `PERMISSION_DENIED` 감사(`action=asset:download`, `reason=no_active_break_glass`, 대상 자산·소유 기관)를 남긴다. 감사의 요청 ID로 거절 응답을 대조할 수 있다. 같은 빈틈이 있던 비식별 검수 원본 프레임(`deid.py`)은 S17에서 고쳤다.
- **S29 클립 작업 목록** — `limit`(기본 100, 최대 500)·`cursor`와 응답 `next_cursor`로 100건 이후의 클립 작업도 이어서 본다. 순서는 요청 시각 역순이고, 같으면 작업 ID 역순이다. 형식이 틀린 `limit`·`cursor`는 400이다. 시간대가 없거나 UTC로 바꾸면 1~9999년 밖인 커서 시각도 질의 전에 400이며, 500이나 연결 오염이 나지 않는다(ADR-0077). 포털의 더 보기 버튼은 남았다.
- **S39 회차 기록 수정의 `evidence_refs: null`** — 500 대신 400 `VALIDATION_ERROR`(`/evidence_refs`, `not_nullable`)를 반환하고 새 버전을 만들지 않는다. 항목을 빼고 보내면 지금 근거를 그대로 잇는다.
- **S40 관측 구간 근거의 없는 시각** — 형식은 맞지만 없는 날짜·시각(2월 30일, 25시, 13월, 윤초)을 `from`·`to`에 넣으면 등록·수정 모두 DB 작업 전에 400 `VALIDATION_ERROR`를 반환한다. 없는 장비와 함께 와도 같다. 오류 위치는 같은 항목의 형식 오류와 같다(`/evidence_refs/{i}/OBSERVATION_RANGE/from`).
- **S41 근거의 다른 기관 ID** — 응답은 없는 ID와 같은 400 `not_found`로 그대로 둔다. 행이 있고 소유 기관이 다른 자산·이벤트·장비이면 대상마다 `CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT` 한 행(소유 기관·대상 종류·ID·경로)을 요청 트랜잭션과 따로 커밋해 남긴다. 없는 ID와 같은 기관의 삭제된 자산은 감사하지 않는다(ADR-0078).
- **S42 회차 기록 수정의 `kind: null` 오류 위치** — `/title`이 아니라 `/kind`를 가리킨다. `title`·`kind`·`evidence_refs`의 null은 모두 그 항목 위치와 `not_nullable`로 답한다(회차 수정 `PATCH /v1/sessions/{id}`와 같은 모양).
- **S30 기간 보고서 관측값 범위** — 기간 보고서의 관측값 지표는 세 가지 관측값만 센다. 그 사업의 회차로 보낸 관측값, 지금 그 사업에 배정된 장비의 관측값, 그 사업 회차의 장비가 그 회차 시간 안에 보낸 관측값(회차 표시 없어도)이다. 같은 기관의 다른 사업 장비나 사업 없는 장비의 관측값은 이 셋에 들지 않으면 값과 원천 목록(`observation_ranges`)에 들어가지 않는다. 그래서 제출본을 보는 관리기관 열람자에게 관계없는 사업의 장비 ID와 값이 보이지 않는다. 그 사업 회차 보고서에 드는 관측값은 같은 기간의 기간 보고서에서 빠지지 않는다. '데이터 없음'과 0건의 구분도 같은 범위로 한다. 이미 저장된 보고서 버전은 그대로다(ADR-0079).
- **S31 9999년 끝 기간 보고서** — `from`·`to`가 9999-12-31T15:00:00Z 이후(한국 시각으로 쓸 수 없는 시각)이면 500이 아니라 400 `VALIDATION_ERROR`를 반환하고, 넘는 값마다 `/from`·`/to` 오류를 단다. 보고서와 생성 감사는 남기지 않는다. 고치지 않은 것: 회차 시각이 9999-12-31T15:00:00Z 이후인 회차(회차 등록이 그 시각을 받는다)의 회차 보고서는 아직 500이다(ADR-0079 감수할 점).
- **S32 HWPX 양식 버전 0** — 경로의 `version`이 0이나 음수이면 400 `VALIDATION_ERROR`(`/path/version`)를 반환한다. 어느 버전에도 파일을 붙이지 않고 감사도 남기지 않는다. 전에는 0이 최신 버전에 붙었고 음수는 404였다.
- **S33 HWPX 양식 크기 초과** — 10MiB를 넘는 본문의 413 `PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE`를 계약에 적었다. 구현은 그대로다.
- **S34 새 버전이 앞 버전의 기간을 바꿈** — 보고서 데이터 기간(`period_from`·`period_to`)을 버전마다 저장한다(마이그레이션 0025). 새 버전(`POST …/versions`)은 자기 기간만 갖는다. 승인·제출된 앞 버전은 `?version=` 조회와 관리기관 열람자 조회에서 승인 스냅숏과 같은 기간을 보여 준다. 기존 버전은 보고서 행의 기간으로 채웠다(ADR-0080).
- **S35 초안 HWPX의 '초안' 표시** — 초안(DRAFT) 버전은 양식의 XML 항목에 `{{report.status}}` 자리가 없으면(조각으로 나뉜 경우 포함) 표시 없는 파일을 만들지 않는다. 대신 409 `CONFLICT`(양식을 고쳐 다시 올리라는 안내)를 반환하고 내보내기 감사를 남기지 않는다. 승인·제출·대체된 버전과 양식 올리기는 그대로다. 생성 시각·데이터 기준 시각 자리는 요구하지 않는다(사람 결정, ADR-0080).
- **S36 내보내기 확인 순서** — 보고서·버전을 먼저 불러온다. 그래서 형식이 꺼져 있어도 다른 기관 보고서는 404와 `CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT` 감사, 없는 보고서·버전은 404를 반환한다. 형식 409는 볼 수 있는 보고서에만 적용된다.
- **S37 기능 상태의 PDF** — `/v1/system/capabilities`의 `report_formats`를 내보내기와 같은 기준(`enabled_formats`)으로 만든다. 그래서 PDF 렌더러를 불러올 수 없으면 PDF를 알리지 않고, PDF 내보내기 409 안내는 렌더러를 이유로 든다. 렌더러를 불러올 수 있는지는 프로세스마다 한 번만 확인한다. 조회마다 실패한 import를 다시 하지 않으므로 WeasyPrint 안내도 한 번만 찍힌다.
- **S38 열람자의 latest_version** — 관리기관 열람자의 보고서 조회·목록에서 `latest_version`은 제출된 적 있는 버전 중 최신이다. 제출되지 않은 초안의 존재와 수를 드러내지 않는다. 소유 기관·운영자는 그대로다.
- **S43 사전 검증의 코드표 검사** — 사전 검증이 운영 경로와 같은 함수로 세 가지를 검사한다: 이벤트 코드의 공통 상위분류, 호출자 기관의 하위코드 등록, 관측 항목 코드. 상위분류에 없는 코드는 `/event_code`의 `unknown_event_category`, 등록하지 않은 하위코드는 `unregistered_event_subcode`로 `valid:false`를 답한다. 이벤트 등록은 같은 함수로 지금과 같은 422 `RULE_VIOLATION`을 낸다. 코드표에 없는 관측 항목은 `/observations/{i}/metric`의 `unknown_metric`이고, 관측값 API는 항목별 `UNKNOWN_METRIC`이다. 규칙 문제는 스키마·항목 문제 뒤에 붙고 100개 상한과 안내 하나를 지킨다. 판정은 목록에서 잘린 문제까지 본다. 공개 예시 `event.manual-safety.json`은 `SAFETY_INCIDENT.near_miss`를 등록한 기관에서만 유효하다(ADR-0081).
- **S44 사전 검증의 시각 순서 검사** — 이벤트 `ended_at`이 `started_at`보다 이르면 `/ended_at` `before_start`를 답한다. 근거 출처 매니페스트의 `capture_end_at`이 `capture_start_at`보다 이르면 `/capture_start_at` `time_rule`을 답한다. 둘 다 운영 경로의 400과 같은 문제다. 두 시각이 모두 읽힐 때만 UTC 값으로 비교하고, 출처가 `UNKNOWN`이면 오류가 아니다. 운영 경로의 응답과 검사 순서는 그대로다(ADR-0081).

## datasets (버전·배포·카탈로그) — 발견 5건

### S01. [medium] POST /v1/datasets/{dataset_id}/versions — missing-behavior

**Large (queued) version path skips the data_card check, returns 202, and GET /v1/datasets/{id} then breaks the Dataset schema**

- **계약:** createDatasetVersion description (contracts/openapi.yaml:4172-4174): '더 많으면 조건을 먼저 모두 검사한 뒤 BUILDING 버전을 만들고 작업 큐에서 사본·매니페스트를 만든다(202) ... 그사이 조건이 바뀌면 FAILED(build_error)'. The request body's data_card is $ref dataset-version-manifest data_card (openapi.yaml:6741). Dataset.data_card in the getDataset/listDatasets 200 bodies uses the same schema (openapi.yaml:6725).
- **구현:** The sync path checks the data card. _materialize validates the manifest, so a card with a required section missing gets 422 RULE_VIOLATION. The queued path (items > DATASET_SYNC_MAX_ITEMS) calls start_build, which runs only _validate (asset conditions) and never checks data_card. The same invalid card is accepted with 202 BUILDING. The worker later marks the version FAILED with build_error '/data_card (privacy is a required property)', so a condition that existed at request time is reported as if it had 'changed meanwhile'. Until then and afterwards, dataset_view reports the latest version's data_card, including a BUILDING or FAILED version. GET /v1/datasets/{id} (and the operator's GET /v1/datasets) therefore returns 200 with a data_card that violates the Dataset schema.
- **근거:** backend/app/api/datasets.py:133-139 (queued branch), 140 (sync branch); backend/app/services/datasets.py:240-254 (start_build: no data_card check), 172-175 (_materialize: the only manifest/data_card check), 270-275 (FAILED at build time), 285-293 (dataset_view takes data_card from versions[-1] regardless of status)
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_dsa_probe.py::test_dsa_versions_async_and_lineage — OBS sync bad card: 422 RULE_VIOLATION | OBS async bad card: 202 BUILDING | OBS GET dataset after async bad card: 200 CONTRACT-FAIL: GET /v1/datasets/{dataset_id} 200 계약 위반: ['data_card']: 'privacy' is a required property | OBS build result: ['FAILED'] FAILED 매니페스트·데이터 카드가 계약과 맞지 않습니다: /data_card ('privacy' is a required property)
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I could not refute this by reading the contract and the current code in wt/rv.

Contract: createDatasetVersion (contracts/openapi.yaml:4172-4174) says the large path checks every condition first, then makes a BUILDING version and returns 202, and only goes FAILED if a condition changes in the meantime. The request body's data_card is $ref dataset-version-manifest#/properties/data_card (DatasetVersionCreate, about line 6741), and that schema requires privacy along with nine other sections (contracts/schemas/dataset-version-manifest.schema.json:37). 422 Unprocessable is a documented response (openapi.yaml:4216). Dataset.data_card in the GET bodies uses the same subschema (about line 6725).

Implementation:
- VersionCreate.data_card is a bare dict[str, Any] (backend/app/api/datasets.py:118), so the request schema is not enforced at the edge.
- Sync branch: build_version calls _materialize, and schema_problems('dataset-version-manifest') there (services/datasets.py:172-175) is the only data_card check. It raises RuleViolation, which gives 422.
- Queued branch (api/datasets.py:133-139): start_build (services/datasets.py:240-254) runs only _validate on the assets, stores the card as is, makes the BUILDING row and enqueues dataset_build. Nothing checks data_card before the 202.
- finish_build (services/datasets.py:269-275) then runs _materialize, hits the same schema problem and records FAILED with build_error. A defect that existed at request time is reported the same way as a condition that changed meanwhile.
- dataset_view (services/datasets.py:285-293) sets data_card from versions[-1] whatever its status, so an operator's GET /v1/datasets/{id} and GET /v1/datasets return a data_card that breaks the Dataset schema while the version is BUILDING and after it is FAILED. Consumers are not affected, because visible_versions keeps only READY versions.

Finder's probe (test_zz_dsa_probe.py::test_dsa_versions_async_and_lineage): it sends the same bad card (privacy removed) to both paths, uses the operator, and sets dataset_sync_max_items=1 with two approved shared assets. It tests what it claims.

Prior records: an earlier probe in docs/review-2026-11-05/repro/contract-sweep/datasets-sharing-deid-assets/test_probe_s3.py:89 ("P9") exercised the same thing. But contract-sweep-findings.md:299 says that bundle never produced results, and nothing in HANDOFF.md, docs/review-2026-11-12/README.md or the ADRs records it as known, fixed or decided. So it is not a duplicate.

Side: fix_impl is right. The contract text is clear and the sync path already behaves that way. Severity: medium is right. It is a validation gap that only an operator hits, it causes no data loss, and the GET response breaks the contract.
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I reproduced it with my own probe, using operator 106 and the ictx fixture with real S3. The same two items and the same card with privacy removed give different results by path. With the sync limit at 200 the request gets 422 RULE_VIOLATION. With dataset_sync_max_items=1 it gets 202 BUILDING and no error code. The probe first makes a READY 1.0.0 with a valid card, and GET /v1/datasets/{id} passes the contract check then. After the queued 2.0.0 is accepted, both GET /v1/datasets/{id} and GET /v1/datasets fail the contract check, because data_card has no privacy section. The build job then ends FAILED with build_error "/data_card ('privacy' is a required property)". The GET still fails the contract after that, so a FAILED version's bad card keeps replacing the card of the earlier valid READY version.

Code: start_build (backend/app/services/datasets.py:240-254) runs only _validate. The only manifest/data_card schema check is in _materialize (:172-175), which runs only in build_version and finish_build. dataset_view (:285-293) takes data_card from versions[-1] whatever its status.

Contract: DatasetVersionCreate.data_card is $ref dataset-version-manifest#/properties/data_card (openapi.yaml:6742), so an invalid card is a request-body violation. The description promises the queued path checks the conditions first (openapi.yaml:4172-4174). Accepting the card with 202 is therefore a contract mismatch.

Not recorded anywhere as known. contract-sweep-findings.md:297 and HANDOFF.md:64 list this bundle as unchecked. A 11-05 probe file (docs/review-2026-11-05/repro/contract-sweep/datasets-sharing-deid-assets/test_probe_s3.py, "P9") touched the same thing, but no finding or decision records it.

The finder's probe is sound: the right role, the same items on both paths, and the queue drained explicitly. Consumers are not affected, because visible_versions filters to READY for them, so the broken GET is operator-only. Severity medium fits: a validation gap plus a contract-breaking operator GET, with no data loss or leak.
- **고칠 쪽:** 구현 — In start_build, before creating the BUILDING row, validate data_card against the dataset-version-manifest data_card subschema (or a manifest skeleton with the known fields) with contracts.schema_problems, and raise the same RuleViolation as the sync path (422, no job). Optionally make dataset_view take data_card from the latest READY version, falling back to none, so a FAILED version's card is never shown as the dataset card. Regression test: dataset_sync_max_items=1, bad card -> 422, no dataset_build job, and GET dataset matches the contract.

### S02. [medium] POST /v1/distributions — missing-audit

**Another consumer organisation's grant gives 404 with no CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT audit**

- **계약:** 404 NotFound: '없음 또는 다른 기관 리소스 (둘을 구분하지 않는다)' (openapi.yaml:4740-4746). Project rule in .kiro/steering/security.md:10 ('다른 기관의 리소스를 요청하면 ... 404를 돌려주고 감사 로그에 남긴다') and security.md:27 (교차 기관 접근 시도 must be audited). A Grant is owned by the consumer organisation (Grant.organization_id).
- **구현:** create_distribution filters with _grants_for(p), which for a CONSUMER is Grant.organization_id == p.organization_id. When the grant belongs to another consumer org it raises NOT_FOUND directly. It does not go through load_scoped and writes no audit row, while load_scoped-based endpoints record CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT. A consumer probing other organisations' grant IDs on the download-URL endpoint leaves no trace.
- **근거:** backend/app/api/datasets.py:346-348 (create_distribution); backend/app/api/datasets.py:288-296 (_grants_for); compare backend/app/api/deps.py:111-121 (load_scoped audits cross-org)
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_dsa_probe.py::test_dsa_distribution_cross_org — OBS other-consumer distribution: 404 NOT_FOUND CROSS_ORG rows added: 0 audit rows for request: [] | OBS own distribution: 201 OK
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I could not refute it. The contract and the project rules do promise this. The NotFound response says '없음 또는 다른 기관 리소스 (둘을 구분하지 않는다)' (contracts/openapi.yaml:4740-4741), and POST /v1/distributions uses that response (openapi.yaml:4471). security.md:10 says a request for another organisation's resource gets 404 and an audit log entry. security.md:27 lists 교차 기관 접근 시도 among the actions that must always be audited.

The current code in the rv checkout (origin/main) behaves as the finding says. create_distribution (backend/app/api/datasets.py:346-348) runs `_grants_for(p).where(Grant.grant_id == body.grant_id)`. For a consumer, _grants_for (datasets.py:288-296) adds `Grant.organization_id == p.organization_id`. If no row comes back, the route raises ProblemError("NOT_FOUND") itself. It never calls load_scoped/get_scoped, and those are the only path that records CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT (deps.py:111-121, tenant.py:79-87). No other audit call runs before the raise, and PERMISSION_DENIED does not fire either, because the CONSUMER role passes `require("distribution:create")`.

The finder's probe is sound. User 110 is a CONSUMER in a separate synthetic consumer org. The grant was issued to the seeded consumer org (user 109). The request authenticated (it got 404, not 401), and the owner's own request got 201. It recorded 0 CROSS_ORG rows and no audit rows at all for that request id.

None of the documents I checked already record this as known, decided or intended. The 2026-11-05 sweep never produced results for this bundle (contract-sweep-findings.md:297-299, '점검하지 못한 묶음'). An old probe, P10 in docs/review-2026-11-05/repro/.../test_probe_s3.py:76, explored the same thing, but no finding was ever written down. HANDOFF section 5 has no matching entry, docs/review-2026-11-12/README.md has none, and no ADR covers grant cross-org audits. The tenant-isolation acceptance test (test_t01 CROSS_ORG_CASES) has no grant or distribution case.

The side is right: fix_impl. The contract and the rules are clear, and the implementation skips the audit. ADR-0041 fixed the same kind of problem for webhook deletes. Medium severity is right too: it is an audit gap that an auditor would notice, the 404 does not leak data, and the hidden activity is a consumer probing grant IDs on the download-URL endpoint.
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I reproduced the finding with my own probe. The probe sets up a second CONSUMER organisation (org 6, user 110). It builds and publishes a dataset version and gets it approved twice (ADMIN_A, then OP), which gives a Grant owned by ORG_CONSUMER. I ran two controls first. Control 0: user 110 is authenticated and has the permission (GET /v1/grants returns 200 with 0 items), so the 404 is not caused by failed auth or a missing role. Control 1: in the same fixture, ADMIN_B reading an ORG_A asset through load_scoped returns 404 and writes 1 CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT row, so the audit path works in this setup. Then user 110 sent POST /v1/distributions with the other organisation's grant_id. Result: 404 NOT_FOUND and 0 CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT rows. A grant id that does not exist also gives 404 NOT_FOUND, so the response does not reveal whether the grant exists. The owning consumer (109) gets 201. In code, backend/app/api/datasets.py:346-348 raises NOT_FOUND straight after the _grants_for(p) filter (datasets.py:288-296, which for a consumer is Grant.organization_id == p.organization_id). It never calls load_scoped (deps.py:111-121), so nothing is audited. security.md:10 and security.md:27 require cross-org access attempts to be written to the audit log. This is not listed as known or fixed in the review documents or HANDOFF that I checked. Earlier findings of the same kind were rated medium (contract-sweep-findings.md #6, #16, #17), and ADR-0041 fixed a similar gap for webhooks. So medium and fix_impl are right. I read the finder's probe and it tests what it claims. Its empty 'audit rows for request' list depends on the X-Request-Id header being used as the audit request_id, which I did not rely on. My probe counts CROSS_ORG rows across the whole audit table.
- **고칠 쪽:** 구현 — Look the grant up unscoped first. If it exists and the caller is not allowed by _grants_for and the owner org differs, record CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT (target_type Grant, organization_id = owner org, path) and raise 404. Alternatively, teach Tenant_Guard/get_scoped the Grant model and use load_scoped. The same _grants_for pattern is used by POST /v1/grants/{id}/revoke and /destruction-confirmation (outside this group), so the fix should probably cover them too.

### S03. [low] GET /v1/datasets — data-semantics

**limit and cursor are validated but ignored: every dataset is returned and next_cursor is always null**

- **계약:** listDatasets declares the Cursor and Limit parameters (openapi.yaml:4127-4129; Limit min 1, max 500, default 50), and the response has next_cursor. api-conventions.md: '목록: 커서 기반 페이지네이션 ?limit=&cursor= ... limit 기본 50, 최대 500.'
- **구현:** contract_query validates the parameters (limit=0, 501 or abc and an over-long cursor give 400), but list_datasets never reads them. It loads every visible dataset (and computes each version's scope and sample URLs) and returns {'items': all, 'next_cursor': None}. With 3 datasets, ?limit=1 returns 3 items. It is the only list handler that hard-codes next_cursor None.
- **근거:** backend/app/api/datasets.py:91-105 (line 105 'next_cursor': None); compare backend/app/api/pagination.py:26-33 (paginate) and backend/app/api/deps.py:126-130 (page_params) used by other list endpoints
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_dsa_probe.py::test_dsa_list_pagination — OBS list limit=1: 200 items 3 next_cursor None | OBS list limit=0: 400
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I could not refute it. The contract does promise pagination here. listDatasets lists the Cursor and Limit parameters (contracts/openapi.yaml:4128-4130), and its 200 response requires items and next_cursor (4138-4141). api-conventions.md:53 makes cursor pagination with a default limit of 50 the rule for every list. The current code ignores both. backend/app/api/datasets.py:91-105 has list_datasets(db, c, p) with no limit or cursor parameter. It runs the full select ordered by dataset_id, loops over every row and returns {"items": out, "next_cursor": None}. This is the only place in backend/app that hard-codes next_cursor None (I grepped for it). Nothing records this as known: HANDOFF.md, docs/review-2026-11-05/*, the ADRs and docs/**/*.md have no entry for dataset list pagination. The ADR-0052 cursor work covers overlapping assets only, and the 11-05 'limit ignored' fix (HANDOFF line 118) was about undeclared limit parameters. The finder's probe tests what it claims: it creates 3 datasets as OP, and with ?limit=1 all 3 come back with next_cursor None. fix_impl is the right side, because the contract text and the convention agree and only the handler is wrong. Low severity is right: data is complete, nothing leaks, and only a client that pages, or a very large catalogue, notices.
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I reproduced this with my own probe. The seed has 0 datasets, so I created 52 as the platform operator (user 106). GET /v1/datasets?limit=1 returned 200 with 52 items and next_cursor null. With no parameters it also returned all 52, so the contract's default of 50 is ignored as well. ?limit=1&cursor=<first dataset_id> returned 200 with all 52 items, starting from the first dataset again, so the cursor is ignored too. The handler at backend/app/api/datasets.py:91-105 takes no limit or cursor parameters: it runs select(Dataset).order_by(dataset_id) with no limit and returns {'items': out, 'next_cursor': None} (line 105). The contract (contracts/openapi.yaml:4125-4141, listDatasets) declares the shared Cursor and Limit parameters and a next_cursor response field. backend/app/api/pagination.py:25-32 (paginate) already exists for this. The finder's probe tests what it claims: the operator role sees every dataset, so the count it reports is not a scope effect. This gap is not recorded as fixed, known or awaiting a decision. The earlier sweep's findings doc lists this bundle as unchecked (contract-sweep-findings.md:297-299), and HANDOFF and the review READMEs do not mention it. An old scratch probe for it (test_p3_list_datasets_ignores_limit) sits in docs/review-2026-11-05/repro/contract-sweep/datasets-sharing-deid-assets/test_probe_ctx.py, but no finding was ever written from it. Severity stays low: no data is lost or leaked. The real effect is an unbounded response that builds a view and sample URLs for every dataset, plus a client that cannot page. fix_impl is the right side, because the contract text matches api-conventions.md.
- **고칠 쪽:** 구현 — Take page_params, order by dataset_id and use pagination.paginate (encode the cursor from dataset_id). For consumers, the 'no visible version' filter is applied in Python after the query, so either move it into SQL (published and an existing READY version) or keep fetching until limit visible rows are collected, so pages are not short and next_cursor stays correct.

### S04. [low] GET /v1/datasets/{dataset_id}/versions/{dataset_version_id}/lineage — wrong-status

**Lineage of a BUILDING or FAILED version returns 409 CONFLICT, which the contract does not list**

- **계약:** getDatasetVersionLineage responses: 200, 401, 403, 404, 429 only (openapi.yaml:4231-4241). The description says nothing about unfinished versions.
- **구현:** If v.status != 'READY' it raises ProblemError('CONFLICT', detail='아직 다 만들지 못한 버전입니다 (...)'), giving 409 for BUILDING and FAILED versions. This is intended: spec 13 task 32 says 계보 is READY only, and tests/integration/test_dataset_build.py asserts 409. The project's own assert_matches_contract rejects the response.
- **근거:** backend/app/api/datasets.py:163-164; backend/tests/integration/test_dataset_build.py (asserts lineage 409 while BUILDING); .kiro/specs/13-sharing-governance/tasks.md:34
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_dsa_probe.py::test_dsa_versions_async_and_lineage — OBS lineage BUILDING: 409 CONFLICT CONTRACT-FAIL: ...: 계약에 409 응답이 없다 | OBS lineage FAILED: 409 CONFLICT
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I could not refute it. The contract at contracts/openapi.yaml:4225-4241 (getDatasetVersionLineage) lists only 200/401/403/404/429, and its description (4227-4229) covers only consumer exclusion and hidden_item_count. Nothing there mentions unfinished versions. The current code at backend/app/api/datasets.py:163-164 raises ProblemError("CONFLICT", detail="아직 다 만들지 못한 버전입니다 ({v.status})") for any status other than READY, so both BUILDING and FAILED get 409. That check runs after the 404 scoping check (159-162), so callers who are allowed to see the version can reach it. The behaviour is intended. .kiro/specs/13-sharing-governance/tasks.md:34 (task 32) says "이용기관·이용 신청·계보·게시는 READY만". backend/tests/integration/test_dataset_build.py:50 asserts lineage == 409 while the version is BUILDING. It checks only the status code and never runs check() against the contract, which is why the gap was not caught. The DatasetVersion schema (openapi.yaml:6720-6721) documents the BUILDING/FAILED states, so a client can hit this. Prior records: the 2026-11-05 sweep saw the same thing in its unfinished probe (docs/review-2026-11-05/repro/contract-sweep/datasets-sharing-deid-assets/test_probe_s3.py:88-105, "P11 lineage BUILDING -> 409 (not in contract)"). That bundle is listed under "점검하지 못한 묶음" (contract-sweep-findings.md:297-300), so the item was never recorded as a finding. HANDOFF.md and the 11-05/11-12 READMEs do not list it as fixed, known, or waiting for a decision. Side: fix_contract is right, because the behaviour follows the spec and CONFLICT is the convention for other state conflicts (api-conventions.md:39). Severity: low is right. The only harm is an undocumented status on an operator/provider read endpoint, with no data or security impact.
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I reproduced it with my own probe. getDatasetVersionLineage lists only 200/401/403/404/429 (contracts/openapi.yaml:4231-4241). Its description says nothing about the version's state. The route raises ProblemError('CONFLICT') whenever v.status != 'READY' (backend/app/api/datasets.py:163-164), and that check runs after the 404 scope check. My probe uses OP 106, a PLATFORM_OPERATOR who holds dataset:lineage, so this is not a role, MFA or scope artifact. The control case proves that: the same request returns 200 and passes the contract once the version is READY. The probe forced the async path with dataset_sync_max_items=1 and observed: 'V3X BUILDING: 409 CONFLICT CONTRACT-FAIL ...: 계약에 409 응답이 없다', then 'V3X READY: 200 CONTRACT-OK', then 'V3X FAILED: 409 CONFLICT CONTRACT-FAIL ...' (the A deid was lowered to PROGRAM_REVIEW with REVOKE_NOW between build start and finish_build). The finder's probe tests what it claims: the same OP role and real BUILDING/FAILED versions. In that probe the 'async bad card' request is the one whose 409 is observed. It does not matter, because my probe with a valid card gives the same result. The behaviour is intended. Spec 13 task 32 (.kiro/specs/13-sharing-governance/tasks.md:34) says '이용기관·이용 신청·계보·게시는 READY만', and tests/integration/test_dataset_build.py:50 asserts 409 while BUILDING. The 409 is not covered by any other documented rule either: the generic Conflict component text only covers commit conflicts, and only on operations that list 409. This finding is not recorded as fixed or known in either review README or in HANDOFF. The earlier sweep's leftover probe docs/review-2026-11-05/repro/contract-sweep/datasets-sharing-deid-assets/test_probe_s3.py (P11) saw the same thing but never turned it into a finding, because that bundle is listed under '점검하지 못한 묶음'. Severity low and side fix_contract are correct.
- **고칠 쪽:** 계약 문구 — Add '409': {$ref: '#/components/responses/Conflict'} to getDatasetVersionLineage, with a description line saying that a version that is not finished yet (BUILDING) or that failed (FAILED) gives 409 CONFLICT. Then run validate_contracts and tests/contract (generated clients need regeneration by the orchestrator).

### S05. [low] POST /v1/datasets/{dataset_id}/versions — wrong-status

**item_asset_ids format rules are not enforced: duplicates are silently deduplicated (201) and malformed IDs get 422 instead of 400**

- **계약:** DatasetVersionCreate.item_asset_ids: {type: array, minItems: 1, maxItems: 20000, uniqueItems: true, items: asset_id} (openapi.yaml:6740). A body that breaks the schema is BadRequest '입력 형식 오류 (VALIDATION_ERROR)' (400).
- **구현:** VersionCreate.item_asset_ids is list[str] with only length bounds. The handler runs sorted(set(...)), so [X, X] is accepted and builds a 1-item version with 201. The request echo (idempotency body) keeps the duplicates. A string that is not an asset_id (for example 'not-an-asset-id') reaches _validate and returns 422 RULE_VIOLATION '자산 존재 ... 없음' instead of 400 with a /item_asset_ids pointer.
- **근거:** backend/app/api/datasets.py:114-118 (VersionCreate), 132 (sorted(set(...))); backend/app/services/datasets.py:133-135
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_dsa_probe.py::test_dsa_versions_async_and_lineage — OBS duplicate item ids: 201 None 1 | OBS malformed item id: 422 RULE_VIOLATION
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I could not refute it. The contract is as quoted. contracts/openapi.yaml:6741 defines DatasetVersionCreate.item_asset_ids as {type: array, minItems: 1, maxItems: 20000, uniqueItems: true, items: $ref common.schema.json#/$defs/asset_id}. The asset_id pattern is ^ast_[0-9A-HJKMNP-TV-Z]{26}$ (contracts/schemas/common.schema.json:26-29). The operation documents 400 BadRequest, '입력 형식 오류 (VALIDATION_ERROR)' (openapi.yaml:4211, 4716-4717), and api-conventions.md:34 maps VALIDATION_ERROR to 400 for input format errors, with JSON Pointer errors.

The implementation does not match. backend/app/api/datasets.py:117 declares `item_asset_ids: list[str] = Field(min_length=1, max_length=20000)`, which has no item pattern and no uniqueness check. Line 132, `ids = sorted(set(body.item_asset_ids))`, silently drops duplicates, so [X, X] produces a 1-item version and returns 201. The idempotency echo at line 151, `body.model_dump`, still holds the duplicates. Malformed IDs reach services/datasets.py:133-135 (_validate) and come back as RuleViolation 422 '자산 존재 / ... 없음', not 400.

None of these documents mention it: docs/review-2026-11-05/contract-sweep-findings.md, docs/review-2026-11-12/README.md, HANDOFF.md, the ADRs. The earlier datasets repro probes do not cover it either. The project's own precedent backs fix_impl. The webhooks event_types check (api/webhooks.py:45) and assets steps check (api/assets.py:201) both raise 400 VALIDATION_ERROR with code 'unique' and a pointer. ADR-0033 and ADR-0063 make server models enforce the contract patterns and return 400. The finder's probe (test_zz_dsa_probe.py lines 57-63) tests exactly these two inputs as operator OP, against a deid asset that is otherwise valid. Its printed output agrees with the code path. Severity low is right: it is an edge case and nothing is lost or leaked.
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I reproduced it with my own probe on current origin/main (rv checkout). The contract has DatasetVersionCreate.item_asset_ids as {type: array, minItems: 1, maxItems: 20000, uniqueItems: true, items: $ref common.schema.json#/$defs/asset_id} (contracts/openapi.yaml:6741). The asset_id pattern is ^ast_[0-9A-HJKMNP-TV-Z]{26}$ (contracts/schemas/common.schema.json:26-28). The operation lists '400': BadRequest (openapi.yaml:4211), and api-conventions.md:34 maps format errors to 400 VALIDATION_ERROR with JSON Pointer errors. The implementation declares item_asset_ids as list[str] with only length bounds (backend/app/api/datasets.py:114-118) and then runs ids = sorted(set(body.item_asset_ids)) (datasets.py:132). So duplicates are silently merged. A malformed ID only fails in services/datasets.py:_validate (lines 133-135) as UnmetCondition '자산 존재 ... 없음', which comes back as 422 RULE_VIOLATION. My probe uses an operator (106) with an APPLIED SANDBOX_PARTNERS de-identified asset. It prints: control [X] -> 201 with 1 item; [X, X] -> 201 with 1 manifest item; ['not-an-asset-id'] -> 422 RULE_VIOLATION; control version='x' -> 400 VALIDATION_ERROR. The last line shows that the model's own pattern checks do produce 400 here, so the 422 for a malformed ID is specific to the missing item pattern and not a fixture or role effect. The finder's probe tests the same thing correctly. This is not recorded in docs/review-2026-11-05 or 2026-11-12 or in HANDOFF. Other endpoints already reject duplicates with 400 (webhooks.py:46, onboarding.py:70, assets.py:202), so fix_impl is the consistent side. Severity low is right: it is an edge case, no data is lost, and the version built is still correct.
- **고칠 쪽:** 구현 — Validate in VersionCreate: each item matches the asset_id pattern from common.schema.json, and add a validator that rejects duplicates. Both raise 400 VALIDATION_ERROR with a /item_asset_ids/<i> pointer. Keep the 422 for well-formed IDs that do not exist.

**일치를 확인한 작업**

- POST /v1/datasets — PLATFORM_OPERATOR only (dataset:write), extra fields forbidden, title 1..200 gives 400, 201 Dataset with empty versions, DATASET_CREATED audit
- GET /v1/datasets/{dataset_id} — consumers see only published datasets with READY L3+ versions (404 otherwise, existence hidden); providers see only datasets that include their org; operator sees all; PROGRAM_VIEWER/REVIEWER 403 (data_card defect covered by the versions finding)
- POST /v1/datasets/{dataset_id}/publish — operator only; 404 for a missing dataset; 422 RULE_VIOLATION when there is no READY version or the latest READY manifest/data_card fails the schema; DATASET_PUBLISHED audit; returns Dataset
- GET /v1/catalog/dcat — unauthenticated; application/ld+json; only published datasets whose version scope (minimum current item scope, deleted item = PRIVATE, no items = PRIVATE so BUILDING/FAILED are excluded) is PUBLIC; uses the latest such version
- POST /v1/distributions (other aspects) — CONSUMER only; 410 GONE for revoked, expired or out-of-period grants; 201 matches the Distribution schema; DISTRIBUTION_ISSUED audit without URLs; recipient manifest included
- POST /v1/datasets/{dataset_id}/versions (other aspects) — 404 for a missing dataset, 409 for a duplicate version number, 422 for ineligible assets on both paths, 201/202 bodies match the contract, Idempotency-Key handled by run_idempotent
- GET /v1/datasets/{dataset_id}/versions/{dataset_version_id}/lineage (other aspects) — CONSUMER 403 (dataset:lineage excludes it), provider sees only own items with hidden_item_count, version not in the dataset or not including the provider's org gives 404

**발견 담당의 메모 (발견으로 올리지 않은 것)**

> The probe file stays at C:/Users/user/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/69809d01a353b176/ef4ed640-70bd-41cd-a22c-ed40e2ba8d1d/scratchpad/night/wt/rv/backend/tests/integration/test_zz_dsa_probe.py; all 3 tests pass and print OBS lines. Run it with: bash "$N/bin/odrun.sh" rv "python -m pytest -q -p no:cacheprovider -s tests/integration/test_zz_dsa_probe.py". No tracked files were changed.
> 
> Things I reproduced or saw but left out:
> - POST /v1/distributions accepts a paths item longer than 512 characters (contract maxLength 512) with 201. It only filters, so it has no effect.
> - A grant whose period has not started gets 410 GONE ('기간 밖'). The Gone wording says '만료된'. That reads as consistent enough.
> - Re-publishing overwrites published_at and writes another DATASET_PUBLISHED. The contract says nothing about this.
> - The sync version build copies every item to the DATASET zone before the manifest/data_card check, so a 422 leaves orphan copies in storage. Clients cannot observe this through the contract.
> - DATASET_CREATED, DATASET_PUBLISHED and DISTRIBUTION_ISSUED audits are written without connection=db.connection(). That is the general audit-atomicity topic the earlier sweep deliberately skipped.
> - Providers (ORG_ADMIN/ORG_MEMBER) can see versions and the latest data_card of a dataset that includes their org, even when those versions are made only of other orgs' items. The contract does not define provider scope for getDataset, so I did not report it; an owner may want to decide it.
> 
> The cross-org 404-without-audit pattern from _grants_for also affects POST /v1/grants/{id}/revoke and /destruction-confirmation, which are outside this group. The earlier interrupted probe docs/review-2026-11-05/repro/contract-sweep/datasets-sharing-deid-assets/test_probe_s3.py (P10) saw the same thing.
> 
> The sync path returning 422 RULE_VIOLATION for a schema-invalid data_card, where a strict reading of the request schema would give 400, is codified by the acceptance test (test_t08_t15_sharing.py), so I did not report it.

## datasets (이용 권한·이용 신청) — 발견 5건

### S06. [medium] POST /v1/grants/{grant_id}/revoke, POST /v1/grants/{grant_id}/destruction-confirmation, POST /v1/access-requests/{access_request_id}/decisions — missing-audit

**Requests for another organisation's grant or access request return 404 but write no CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT audit**

- **계약:** NotFound response (openapi.yaml:4740-4746): '없음 또는 다른 기관 리소스 (둘을 구분하지 않는다)'. security.md:10: '다른 기관의 리소스를 요청하면 존재 여부를 숨기기 위해 404를 돌려주고 감사 로그에 남긴다.' SWEEP rule: cross-org gives 404 plus a CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT audit.
- **구현:** The three routes look the row up through a role-filtered select (_grants_for / _requests_for). If the row is outside the caller's scope they raise NOT_FOUND straight away and never check whether the row exists for another organisation, so no audit is written. Cases tested: a CONSUMER of a second consumer organisation confirming destruction on organisation 5's grant; ORG_ADMIN of org B (no assets in the version) revoking that grant; ORG_ADMIN of org B deciding on a request for a version that holds only org A assets. In the same run, a cross-org device GET goes through load_scoped and does write the audit.
- **근거:** backend/app/api/datasets.py:257-260 (decide), 312-314 (revoke), 325-327 (destruction-confirmation), 288-296 (_grants_for), 231-239 (_requests_for); compare backend/app/api/deps.py:111-123 (load_scoped records CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT); .kiro/steering/security.md:10
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_dsb_a.py::test_a_cross_org_404_without_audit — A other-consumer destruction: 404 NOT_FOUND | unrelated ORG_ADMIN revoke: 404 NOT_FOUND | unrelated ORG_ADMIN decide: 404 NOT_FOUND; A new audit rows: []; A compare cross-org device GET: 404 ['CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT']
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I could not refute it. Reading the contract and the current code on origin/main (rv checkout) confirms it.

Contract: all three operations return the shared 404 response (openapi.yaml:4285 revoke, 4320 destruction-confirmation, 4425 decisions). That response is NotFound at openapi.yaml:4740-4746, described as '없음 또는 다른 기관 리소스 (둘을 구분하지 않는다)'.

Rules: security.md:10 says another organisation's resource gets 404 and an audit record. The mandatory audit list in security.md also names '교차 기관 접근 시도'.

Implementation:
- decide_access_request (backend/app/api/datasets.py:257-260) selects through _requests_for(db, p) (231-239). That query filters by consumer organization_id, or by provider_organization_ids for ORG_ADMIN/MEMBER.
- revoke_grant (312-314) and confirm_destruction (325-327) select through _grants_for(p) (288-296).
- In all three, scalar_one_or_none() returning None goes straight to `raise ProblemError("NOT_FOUND")`. Nothing checks whether the row exists for another organisation, and no c.audit.record call happens on that path.
- The tenant guard does write the audit. load_scoped (deps.py:111-123) calls get_scoped (tenant.py:79-87); when no row is in scope, get_scoped looks up the row's owner organization_id and raises NotFoundInScope(cross_org=...), and load_scoped then records CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT. These routes do not use it.

Not already known: the review documents, HANDOFF section 5 and ADR-0019 say nothing about grants or access requests on this point. The earlier sweep treated the same pattern as a real fix_impl gap at medium severity: contract-sweep-findings.md #6, #16 and #17 (a short-circuit before load_scoped, so no CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT). ADR-0041 #3 fixed the same pattern for webhook delete.

The finder's three cases are real cross-org requests: a consumer of a second organisation, and ORG_ADMINs of an organisation with no assets in the version. The row belongs to the consumer organisation (and the provider organisations), not to the caller's.

The side is right: fix_impl, because the contract text and security.md are explicit. Severity medium matches the precedent (a missing mandatory audit; the 404 itself hides existence, so there is no leak).

I did not run the finder's probe, since this is the reading lens. The code path leaves no room for an audit write.
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — My own probe reproduces the finding. I checked the finder's probe first: the roles are right (CONSUMER for destruction-confirmation, ORG_ADMIN for revoke and decide), and the 404 comes from the route's scoped lookup, not from a permission or MFA failure. To rule out the MFA question, my probe sends every call with mfa=True.

Probe result: the grant and the pending access request belong to consumer org 5, on a version built only from org A assets. ORG_ADMIN B and a second consumer org cannot see either row in the GET lists. Their writes all return 404 NOT_FOUND and add zero audit rows:
- consumer2 destruction-confirmation: 404, no new audit rows
- adminB revoke: 404, no new audit rows
- adminB decide: 404, no new audit rows

A grant id that does not exist also returns 404 with no rows, so the two cases look the same from outside. The control call works: ORG_ADMIN A revokes the same grant and gets 200 REVOKED, which proves the row exists and the requests above really were cross-org.

Code path: backend/app/api/datasets.py:257-260 (decide), 312-314 (revoke) and 325-327 (destruction-confirmation) call _requests_for (231-239) or _grants_for (288-296). When the lookup returns None they raise NOT_FOUND straight away. Only load_scoped (backend/app/api/deps.py:111-123), via get_scoped (app/services/tenant.py), checks who owns the row and writes CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT.

The written rules require that audit:
- security.md:10: "다른 기관의 리소스를 요청하면 ... 404를 돌려주고 감사 로그에 남긴다"
- .kiro/specs/02-identity-tenancy/design.md:31
- the contract's NotFound response says 404 covers other organisations' resources

Not already recorded: the 2026-11-05 sweep never reported this bundle ("점검하지 못한 묶음"). Its leftover probe test_probe_s3.py P10 noticed the same gap but nothing was written up. ADR-0041 fixed the same kind of gap for webhooks, which shows the project treats it as fix_impl. Medium severity and the fix_impl side are both correct: there is no existence leak, only the missing audit record that an auditor would look for.
- **고칠 쪽:** 구현 — When the scoped query returns None, run db.get(Grant|AccessRequest, id). If the row exists, record CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT (result=DENIED, organization_id=row.organization_id, target_type Grant/AccessRequest, target_id, details.path), the same way load_scoped does, then raise NOT_FOUND. Possible approach: a small helper shared with POST /v1/distributions, which has the same pattern (datasets.py:346-348; not in this group). Add these paths to the T01 CROSS_ORG_CASES (the cases need a grant fixture).

### S07. [medium] POST /v1/access-requests — wrong-status

**Impossible or extreme period dates give 500 INTERNAL instead of 400**

- **계약:** access-request.schema.json: period.start_date / end_date are {type: string, format: date}. BadRequest (openapi.yaml:4716-4725): '입력 형식 오류 (VALIDATION_ERROR)'. SWEEP: bad-but-parseable input must not give 500.
- **구현:** Period validates the dates only against the regex ^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}$. create_request then calls date.fromisoformat(), which raises ValueError for 2026-02-30 and 2026-13-01. It also computes end + timedelta(days=1), which raises OverflowError for end_date 9999-12-31, a valid format=date value. Neither exception is handled, so both go to the generic handler and give 500 INTERNAL.
- **근거:** backend/app/api/datasets.py:192-195 (pattern-only Period); backend/app/services/datasets.py:331-339 (fromisoformat; end + timedelta(days=1)); backend/app/errors.py _unhandled -> INTERNAL
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_dsb_a.py::test_b_bad_dates_give_500 — B: {'impossible start': (500, 'INTERNAL'), 'month 13': (500, 'INTERNAL'), 'end 9999-12-31': (500, 'INTERNAL')}
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I could not refute this finding. The contract promises 400 for these inputs. contracts/schemas/access-request.schema.json lines 12-16 type period.start_date and end_date as {type: string, format: date}, and the POST /v1/access-requests responses include '400': BadRequest (openapi.yaml around 4418, defined at 4716-4725 as "입력 형식 오류 (VALIDATION_ERROR)"). '2026-02-30' and '2026-13-01' are not valid format=date values, so a client should get 400. '9999-12-31' is a valid date, and nothing in the contract or domain.md bounds the period, so 500 is never a documented outcome for it.

The code does what the finding says:
- backend/app/api/datasets.py:192-195: Period checks the two dates only with the pattern ^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}$.
- backend/app/services/datasets.py:331-332: date.fromisoformat() raises ValueError for both impossible dates.
- backend/app/services/datasets.py:339: end + timedelta(days=1) raises OverflowError for 9999-12-31, because end >= start passes first.
- Nothing translates these exceptions. The create() closure and run_idempotent have no try/except. backend/app/errors.py registers handlers only for ProblemError, RequestValidationError, RuleViolation, InvalidStateTransition, StarletteHTTPException and DBAPIError, so ValueError and OverflowError reach _unhandled at errors.py:163-166, which returns INTERNAL (500).

I confirmed the Python behaviour on the host. ValueError: "day 30 must be in range 1..28 for month 2 in year 2026" and "month must be in 1..12, not 13". OverflowError: "date value out of range". The finder's probe test_zz_dsb_a.py::test_b_bad_dates_give_500 uses CONSUMER 109 on a published L3 version with a valid AI_TRAINING purpose. The purpose checks (datasets.py:322-329) therefore pass before the date parsing, so the probe tests what it claims.

This is not already known. contract-sweep-findings.md "점검하지 못한 묶음" says this bundle was never reported; only an unreported probe (docs/review-2026-11-05/repro/contract-sweep/datasets-sharing-deid-assets/test_probe_s3.py P7) tried the same inputs. ADR-0056 / review-2026-11-12 V3 fixed format: date-time in the jsonschema validators, not this pydantic model. HANDOFF has no matching open item.

The side is right: fix_impl. The contract text is correct and the behaviour is not.

Medium severity stands. Feb-30 and month-13 are ordinary client typos that a web form can send, and they get 500 instead of 400. The 9999-12-31 part on its own is low, the same as the earlier inspections finding #13 (date(9999,12,31)+1d, rated low).
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I reproduced this with my own probe against current origin/main in the rv checkout. The probe uses a fresh fixture: a published L3 version, user 109 as CONSUMER, purpose AI_TRAINING. It includes control cases to rule out unrelated causes. The control with valid dates gives 201 REQUESTED and writes the ACCESS_REQUESTED audit row. The control with a bad pattern (2026/12/01) gives 400 VALIDATION_ERROR. The control with end before start gives 422 RULE_VIOLATION. So the body, role and fixture are correct. Four inputs that match the pattern but are not real dates all give 500 INTERNAL and write no audit row: end_date 2027-02-30, start_date 2026-13-01, end_date 2027-01-00, and end_date 9999-12-31. Cause in the code: backend/app/api/datasets.py:192-195 checks Period.start_date and end_date only against the pattern ^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}$. backend/app/services/datasets.py:331-332 then calls date.fromisoformat(), which raises ValueError for impossible dates. :337 computes end + timedelta(days=1), which raises OverflowError at date.max. Neither error is handled, so both reach errors.py _unhandled and become INTERNAL. The contract types both fields as {type: string, format: date} (contracts/schemas/access-request.schema.json:15-16), so all four values are valid-looking input that the contract says should be rejected as format errors (400 VALIDATION_ERROR). I found no record of this as fixed, known or awaiting a decision. The 11-05 bundle 'datasets·sharing·deid·assets' was never reported (contract-sweep-findings.md:299). An unreported earlier probe (docs/review-2026-11-05/repro/contract-sweep/datasets-sharing-deid-assets/test_probe_s3.py P7) tried the same two impossible dates, but no finding was written from it. The fix side is right (fix_impl), and medium severity matches the similar inspections 9999-12-31 overflow finding in the 11-05 sweep.
- **고칠 쪽:** 구현 — Type Period.start_date/end_date as datetime.date in strict ISO mode, or add a field_validator that runs date.fromisoformat, so impossible dates give 400 VALIDATION_ERROR with /period/start_date or /period/end_date. Reject end_date at date.max (or set an upper bound) with 400 before the +1 day. Add the dates to a regression test.

### S08. [low] POST /v1/access-requests — data-semantics

**agreement_version is required by the contract schema but optional (and nullable) in the implementation; the web client never sends it**

- **계약:** contracts/schemas/access-request.schema.json 'required': [..., "agreement_accepted", "agreement_version"], agreement_version {type: string, maxLength: 32}, so a request without it should get 400 VALIDATION_ERROR (openapi.yaml:4418).
- **구현:** AccessCreate declares agreement_version: str | None = Field(None, max_length=32). A request that omits it gets 201, and so does one with explicit null; the AccessRequest row is stored with agreement_version NULL. The first-party web form (web/src/pages/Sharing.tsx:233-236) does not send the field, so in practice no stored request says which version of the user agreement was accepted.
- **근거:** contracts/schemas/access-request.schema.json (required list); backend/app/api/datasets.py:206; backend/app/services/datasets.py:340; backend/app/db/models.py:1048 (nullable); web/src/pages/Sharing.tsx:233-236
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_dsb_a.py::test_c_agreement_version_optional — C omitted: 201 REQUESTED | null: 201 REQUESTED
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I could not refute it by reading the code. (1) The contract really does require the field. contracts/openapi.yaml createAccessRequest (around line 4400) points its requestBody at ./schemas/access-request.schema.json. That schema's line 7 lists "agreement_version" under required, and line 21 types it as {type: string, maxLength: 32}, not nullable. The response list includes 400 BadRequest. The examples (access-request.ai-training.json, invalid/access-request.no-agreement.json) both carry "agreement_version": "2027-01". (2) The implementation really does accept it as optional. backend/app/api/datasets.py:206 declares `agreement_version: str | None = Field(None, max_length=32)`. Nothing else in the route checks it, and services/datasets.py:340 stores body.get("agreement_version"), so a missing or null value is saved as NULL (models.py:1048, migration 0011 line 55 nullable=True). A request without the field passes Pydantic and gets 201. (3) The first-party web client never sends it: web/src/pages/Sharing.tsx:233-236 posts dataset_version_id, purpose, purpose_detail, security_measures, period and agreement_accepted only. (4) The mismatch is not recorded anywhere as fixed, known or decided. The only earlier trace is the 2026-11-05 probe docs/review-2026-11-05/repro/contract-sweep/datasets-sharing-deid-assets/test_probe_s3.py (P7b). That bundle is listed under '점검하지 못한 묶음' in contract-sweep-findings.md:297-300, with no result recorded. No ADR, HANDOFF entry, spec or domain.md text mentions agreement_version. (5) needs_owner is the right side. Nowhere does the system keep a 'current user-agreement version': no setting in config.py or .env.example, no endpoint, and the AccessRequest response schema does not expose the field. So fix_impl alone would break the web form, and the client would have nothing to send. Whether the version is mandatory evidence is a product decision. requirements 8.1 only says '이용 약정에 동의해', and domain.md:134 puts '이용자 약정 확인' at the operator's PROVIDER_APPROVED→GRANTED step, not in the request body. I am lowering severity to low. Nothing uses or exposes the stored value, a missing field is accepted rather than rejected, and no flow breaks; it is a leniency and evidence gap, not a status a client would trip over.
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I reproduced the finding with my own probe. The contract requires the field: contracts/schemas/access-request.schema.json:7 lists agreement_version in "required" with {type: string, maxLength: 32}, and openapi.yaml:4408 uses that schema as the requestBody of createAccessRequest. Both contract examples carry it ("2027-01"), and web/src/contract/api.gen.ts:5169 types it as required (`agreement_version: string`). The implementation treats it as optional: backend/app/api/datasets.py:206 has `agreement_version: str | None = Field(None, max_length=32)`, backend/app/services/datasets.py:340 stores body.get(...), and models.py:1048 / migration 0011:55 make the column nullable. The web form at web/src/pages/Sharing.tsx:233-236 posts without the field, and so does the acceptance helper tests/acceptance/test_t08_t15_sharing.py:78-82 (access()).

My probe uses a consumer (109, CONSUMER role), a published L3 version, otherwise valid bodies and only 127.0.0.1. Observed: omitted 201 REQUESTED, null 201 REQUESTED, "v9" 201 REQUESTED, 33 chars 400 VALIDATION_ERROR. Stored agreement_version is None for omitted and null and 'v9' when given. So the maxLength check works and only the "required" part is not enforced.

The finder's probe is sound: right role, real published version, and the only thing that changes between its requests is agreement_version. This is not recorded anywhere I checked: contract-sweep-findings.md, the review-2026-11-12 docs, HANDOFF.md and the ADRs. docs/review-2026-11-05/repro/.../test_probe_s3.py:59-63 probed it once (P7b), but no finding was ever written for it.

Side needs_owner is correct. Nothing in backend/app, domain.md or the specs defines agreement versions or a "current agreement version" the web could send. spec 13 requirements.md:90 only says the consumer agrees to the user agreement, and domain.md:134 only says the operator checks it. Making the field mandatory therefore needs a product decision about where the version value comes from.

I lowered severity to low. Accepting a body the contract would reject breaks no client. The field is unverified client free text even when sent, so a missing value loses little real evidence. No data loss, leak or broken flow results.
- **고칠 쪽:** 사람 결정 — The owner decides whether the accepted user-agreement version is mandatory evidence (domain.md grant_status 'PROVIDER_APPROVED → GRANTED: 이용자 약정 확인'). If yes: make the field required and non-null in AccessCreate, have the web form send the current agreement version, and leave the contract as is (fix_impl). If no: drop agreement_version from the schema's required list, or document that it is optional (fix_contract).

### S09. [low] POST /v1/access-requests/{access_request_id}/decisions — wrong-status

**Operator approval of a version that fell below L3 returns 422 RULE_VIOLATION, which the operation does not document**

- **계약:** decideAccessRequest responses (openapi.yaml:4438-4449) list 200, 400, 401, 403, 404, 409, 429. There is no 422.
- **구현:** Sequence: the provider approves, then ORG_ADMIN downgrades the asset from APPROVED_PARTNERS to SANDBOX_PARTNERS with KEEP_UNTIL_EXPIRY, then the operator sends APPROVE. datasets.decide raises RuleViolation and the client gets 422 RULE_VIOLATION ('데이터셋 버전 등급이 이용기관 제공 조건(L3)에 못 미칩니다'). The contract test helper rejects the response: '계약에 422 응답이 없다'. The behaviour matches domain.md (L3 is needed before granting); only the contract is silent.
- **근거:** backend/app/services/datasets.py:383-385; contracts/openapi.yaml:4438-4449
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_dsb_a.py::test_d_operator_approve_below_l3_gives_undocumented_422 — D downgrade L3->L2: 201 APPLIED; D operator approve: 422 RULE_VIOLATION 데이터셋 버전 등급이 이용기관 제공 조건(L3)에 못 미칩니다 CONTRACT-FAIL: ... 계약에 422 응답이 없다
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I could not refute it. (1) The contract's decideAccessRequest (contracts/openapi.yaml:4425-4449) lists only 200/400/401/403/404/409/429 and says nothing about refusing on grade. (2) backend/app/services/datasets.py:383-385 raises RuleViolation in the operator APPROVE branch when version_scope(db, v) is below CATALOG_MIN (L3). The route (backend/app/api/datasets.py:253-272) catches only PermissionError, so the exception reaches the global handler in backend/app/errors.py:119-122, which returns 422 RULE_VIOLATION with unmet_conditions. (3) I re-ran the finder's probe in od-rv-py. It printed 'D downgrade L3->L2: 201 APPLIED' and then 'D operator approve: 422 RULE_VIOLATION 데이터셋 버전 등급이 이용기관 제공 조건(L3)에 못 미칩니다 CONTRACT-FAIL: ... 계약에 422 응답이 없다', and the test passed. The probe is sound: the provider ORG_ADMIN approves first, the downgrade uses KEEP_UNTIL_EXPIRY, and then the operator approves. (4) The behaviour is intended. ADR-0019 line 18 says L3/L4 are re-evaluated when the operator decides, and line 23 says a version holding a downgraded asset drops below L3. design.md (13-sharing-governance) also requires L3 for consumers. So the code is right and only the contract is silent, which makes fix_contract the correct side. (5) The issue is not already recorded. contract-sweep-findings.md, the review READMEs and HANDOFF do not mention this operation's 422. The 2026-11-05 probes for this bundle touched the decisions endpoint only for other checks. Low severity is right: the path needs a downgrade between provider approval and operator approval.
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I reproduced it myself with my own probe. The probe first runs a control: the same roles and fixture, with no downgrade, give operator APPROVE 200 GRANTED. That rules out a wrong role, a missing MFA or a bad fixture. Then the provider (101) approves, giving PROVIDER_APPROVED. Next ORG_ADMIN shares the asset to SANDBOX_PARTNERS with KEEP_UNTIL_EXPIRY, which returns 201 APPLIED. The operator (106) then sends APPROVE and gets 422 RULE_VIOLATION '데이터셋 버전 등급이 이용기관 제공 조건(L3)에 못 미칩니다'. The contract check helper fails with 'POST /v1/access-requests/{access_request_id}/decisions: 계약에 422 응답이 없다'. The code path is backend/app/services/datasets.py:383-385, where a RuleViolation is raised when LEVEL[version_scope] < LEVEL[CATALOG_MIN]. The route backend/app/api/datasets.py:253-272 does not catch it, so it maps to 422. contracts/openapi.yaml decideAccessRequest lists only 200/400/401/403/404/409/429. The behaviour itself is intended: domain.md:75 makes L3 a condition of operator approval. So the contract is the side that is silent. The case is not recorded as known: docs/review-2026-11-05/repro/contract-sweep/datasets-sharing-deid-assets/test_probe_s3.py has an unfinished probe 'P8' for exactly this case. That bundle is listed under '점검하지 못한 묶음' in contract-sweep-findings.md, and no finding was ever written for it. No ADR, HANDOFF entry or review README mentions it.
- **고칠 쪽:** 계약 문구 — Add '422': {$ref: '#/components/responses/Unprocessable'} to decideAccessRequest. A description line could say that operator approval is refused with RULE_VIOLATION when the version's current grade is below L3. Then add a contract test for this path.

### S10. [low] POST /v1/access-requests/{access_request_id}/decisions — data-semantics

**Decisions made after deadline_at are still accepted and can produce GRANTED until the worker's expire pass runs**

- **계약:** AccessRequest.deadline_at (openapi.yaml:6763): '이때까지 결정이 없으면 거부'. Spec 13 requirement 8.3 (.kiro/specs/13-sharing-governance/requirements.md:92): if no approval within the deadline, the request SHALL be DENIED.
- **구현:** datasets.decide never compares now with req.deadline_at. Requests are only denied when the worker's maintenance runs datasets.expire, every job_lease_reap_interval_s (60 s by default) and not at all while the worker is down. A provider approval and then an operator approval sent after the deadline both return 200, the request ends up GRANTED and a grant is issued. For contrast, distribution and break-glass check the time directly instead of waiting for the worker.
- **근거:** backend/app/services/datasets.py:353-366 (no deadline check), 405-413 (deadline only enforced in expire); backend/app/workers/main.py:296-300; backend/app/config.py:117
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_dsb_a.py::test_e_decisions_accepted_after_deadline — E provider after deadline: 200 PROVIDER_APPROVED | operator after deadline: 200 GRANTED grt_01M77Y8XEHA8F6BJ85X5MJ78P1 | listed: GRANTED 2026-11-12T12:13:38Z
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I could not refute it. The contract and spec make the promise: AccessRequest.deadline_at in contracts/openapi.yaml (around line 6763) is described as '이때까지 결정이 없으면 거부'. Spec 13 requirement 8.3 (.kiro/specs/13-sharing-governance/requirements.md, 요구사항 8 item 3) says a request with no approval inside the deadline SHALL be DENIED. The domain.md grant_status table lists 'REQUESTED, PROVIDER_APPROVED → DENIED' by SERVICE when the deadline passes. The implementation does not check it: backend/app/services/datasets.py::decide (around lines 353-390) never reads req.deadline_at or compares it with now. Its guards are only role, duplicate decision and current status, so a provider APPROVE on REQUESTED and then an operator APPROVE on PROVIDER_APPROVED both succeed after the deadline, and a Grant is created. The deadline is only enforced in datasets.expire (around lines 405-413, `deadline_at < now`), which the worker's maintenance loop calls (backend/app/workers/main.py around lines 296-300). That loop runs every job_lease_reap_interval_s = 60 (backend/app/config.py:117), and not at all while the worker is down. The finder's probe (backend/tests/integration/test_zz_dsb_a.py::test_e_decisions_accepted_after_deadline) is sound: it sets deadline_at one hour in the past directly in the DB, runs no expire pass, and uses the right roles (provider ADMIN_A, then OP). It shows 200 PROVIDER_APPROVED, then 200 GRANTED with a grant_id. The case is not listed as known, fixed or waiting for a decision anywhere I searched for deadline/기한: ADRs, docs/review-2026-11-05, docs/review-2026-11-12 and HANDOFF. The 409 Conflict the fix would return is already in this operation's contract responses, so fix_impl (with no contract change needed) is the right side. Low severity fits: the window is normally at most about 60 s, unbounded only while the worker is stopped, and only an authorised provider and operator can act in it.
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — My own probe reproduces the finding. It uses the app's clock (ctx.c.clock.now()) rather than the finder's datetime.now, so a clock mismatch is ruled out. It sets deadline_at 5 minutes in the past on two fresh requests. On one of them, provider ORG_ADMIN 101 approves (200 PROVIDER_APPROVED) and then operator 106 approves (200 GRANTED, grant grt_01M77YGKV0WFBZVC90N6X93NDA, deadline_at 2026-11-12T13:12:50Z, clock 13:17:50Z). Running datasets.expire afterwards gives {'denied': 1}: the untouched request becomes DENIED and the already-granted one stays GRANTED. So the result depends on whether the worker's pass happens to run before or after the late decision.

Code check: datasets.decide (backend/app/services/datasets.py:353-391) never compares now with req.deadline_at. Only expire (around line 405) applies `deadline_at < now`, and the worker maintenance loop calls it (backend/app/workers/main.py:296-300). The route (backend/app/api/datasets.py:253-272) adds no check either.

What the contract and spec say: AccessRequest.deadline_at is described as '이때까지 결정이 없으면 거부'. Spec 13 req 8.3 says the request SHALL be DENIED if there is no approval within the deadline. domain.md:135 lists '응답 기한 초과' as a REQUESTED/PROVIDER_APPROVED → DENIED reason.

Nothing in HANDOFF, the review docs or the ADRs records this as known. Severity low is right: the window is normally one maintenance interval, but it has no limit while the worker is down. The side is fix_impl.
- **고칠 쪽:** 구현 — In decide, if req.status is REQUESTED or PROVIDER_APPROVED and now >= req.deadline_at, move the request to DENIED (SERVICE), notify like expire does, and return 409 INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION (detail: 응답 기한 초과), committing the denial. Simpler option: refuse with 409 and leave the transition to the worker. Add a regression test that sets deadline_at in the past.

**일치를 확인한 작업**

- GET /v1/grants: scope (consumer: own organisation; provider: versions with its assets; operator: all), roles (PROGRAM_VIEWER and REVIEWER get 403), response matches the Grant schema, REVOKED rows carry the notice
- POST /v1/grants/{grant_id}/revoke (except the cross-org audit gap): ORG_ADMIN and operator only, GRANTED→REVOKED, 409 INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION on repeat, notice and consumer notification, GRANT_REVOKED audit, response matches the contract
- POST /v1/grants/{grant_id}/destruction-confirmation (except the cross-org audit gap): CONSUMER only, only on REVOKED/EXPIRED, 409 on repeat, GRANT_DESTRUCTION_CONFIRMED audit, response matches the contract
- GET /v1/usage-history: PROVIDER roles only (operator, viewer, reviewer and consumer get 403), versions with own assets only, consumer organisation name present, REQUESTED/DECIDED/GRANTED/DOWNLOAD_ISSUED/REVOKED items match the contract
- GET /v1/access-requests: consumer sees its own, provider sees requests on versions with its assets, operator sees all, the other roles get 403, response matches the contract
- POST /v1/access-requests (except the date-500 and agreement_version findings): 404 for invisible, unpublished or not-READY versions, 422 for data-card purposes and tier-2 agreement purposes (organisation count only), 422 when end < start, idempotency, ACCESS_REQUESTED audit
- POST /v1/access-requests/{access_request_id}/decisions (except the 422, deadline and cross-org findings): ORG_MEMBER and CONSUMER get 403, provider can decide only while REQUESTED, operator approval needs PROVIDER_APPROVED (409), duplicate decision gives 409, one DENY ends the request, ACCESS_DECIDED audit

**발견 담당의 메모 (발견으로 올리지 않은 것)**

> Probe files (left in place): C:/Users/user/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/69809d01a353b176/ef4ed640-70bd-41cd-a22c-ed40e2ba8d1d/scratchpad/night/wt/rv/backend/tests/integration/test_zz_dsb_a.py (tests a-e, one per finding; each asserts the current wrong behaviour, so it passes now and fails once fixed) and test_zz_dsb_b.py (consistency probe for usage-history, grants, lists and revoke). Both run green against origin/main in od-rv-py (about 15 s and 7 s).
> 
> The earlier sweep's leftover probe docs/review-2026-11-05/repro/contract-sweep/datasets-sharing-deid-assets/test_probe_s3.py (P7, P7b, P8, P10) already hinted at findings 1-4. They were never written up, because that bundle is listed as unchecked, and I re-reproduced all four on current code.
> 
> The same cross-org audit gap exists on POST /v1/distributions (datasets.py:346-348). It is outside this group, so I did not report it.
> 
> Edge cases seen but not reported:
> - A grant whose expires_at has passed stays status GRANTED in GET /v1/grants until the worker's expire pass. Destruction-confirmation on it then gives 409, although the grant is in fact expired.
> - pending_provider_approvals still shows the remaining count after a request is DENIED.
> - Requests denied by the deadline produce no DECIDED item in usage-history.
> - The GRANT_REVOKED audit is written under the consumer organisation (g.organization_id), while ACCESS_DECIDED uses the decider's organisation.
> None of these contradicts explicit contract text.
> 
> I did not change any tracked file.

## sharing (공유 심사) — 발견 6건

### S11. [high] PATCH /v1/assets/{asset_id}/classification — data-semantics

**Making an asset stricter leaves it shared at L2+ (SECURITY at L2, inherited SECURITY on derived assets)**

- **계약:** classifyAsset (openapi.yaml:2100-2140): '운영자의 민감도·개인정보 비해당 지정'. asset-metadata.schema.json allOf invariant 'L2 이상 등급의 불변식': sharing_scope ∈ {SANDBOX_PARTNERS, APPROVED_PARTNERS, PUBLIC} ⇒ sensitivity_class ∈ {INTERNAL, RESTRICTED}; PUBLIC ⇒ sensitivity_class=INTERNAL. domain.md:58 says derived assets inherit the strictest sensitivity_class; domain.md:74/76 say L2+ needs sensitivity ≠ SECURITY and L4 needs INTERNAL. The handler's own docstring (api/sharing.py:180) says '더 엄격하게 바꾸면 파생본 등급은 규칙으로 다시 본다'.
- **구현:** It only assigns sensitivity_class/privacy_status and writes ASSET_CLASSIFIED (api/sharing.py:192-202). It never re-evaluates or lowers sharing_scope of the asset or its descendants. A DEID asset at SANDBOX_PARTNERS set to SECURITY stays SANDBOX_PARTNERS, and GET /v1/assets/{id} then returns a 200 body that breaks the asset-metadata schema invariant. Setting the ORIGINAL to SECURITY makes the derived asset's effective sensitivity SECURITY, but its scope stays at L2. Partners keep access to data that an operator has just marked security-sensitive.
- **근거:** backend/app/api/sharing.py:177-202 (docstring 180, no re-evaluation); backend/app/domain/sharing_rules.py:53-55,84,92 (effective_sensitivity used only when evaluating an upgrade); contracts/schemas/asset-metadata.schema.json allOf[1],[2]; precedent for the data_role→CORE change that does downgrade: backend/app/api/assets.py:139-145
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_shr_a.py::test_classification_breaks_shared_invariant — classify SECURITY on L2 deid: 200 {... 'sensitivity_class': 'SECURITY'}; GET asset: 200 SANDBOX_PARTNERS SECURITY; asset contract check failed: ['sensitivity_class']: 'SECURITY' is not one of ['INTERNAL', 'RESTRICTED'] (the other two schema errors come from the test helper's fixture data); orig classify: 200 deid scope: SANDBOX_PARTNERS effective_sensitivity: SECURITY
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I could not refute it. The current code on origin/main matches the finding.

1. The handler writes the new values and stops. backend/app/api/sharing.py:192-202 sets sensitivity_class/privacy_status, writes ASSET_CLASSIFIED and returns. Nothing reads or changes sharing_scope for the asset or its descendants. The handler's own docstring (sharing.py:180) says "더 엄격하게 바꾸면 파생본 등급은 규칙으로 다시 본다", and nothing in the code does that.

2. effective_sensitivity is checked only on upgrades. rules.evaluate (domain/sharing_rules.py:84, :92) is the only place that reads it, and it runs only when a scope upgrade is requested. rules.max_scope_for is used only in api/sharing.py:121, a display path. No other sensitivity check runs at access time (I grepped backend/app for "sensitivity"). So partners keep L2/L3 access after an asset or its original is marked SECURITY.

3. The documents require it.
- The contract's own invariant: asset-metadata.schema.json allOf[1] requires sensitivity_class ∈ {INTERNAL, RESTRICTED} when sharing_scope is L2+, and allOf[2] requires INTERNAL for PUBLIC.
- domain.md:58 says derived assets inherit the strictest sensitivity.
- domain.md:74/76 set the L2/L4 conditions.
- domain.md ch.4 says the schema invariant checks that stored state does not contradict those conditions.
- Requirement 13 (requirements.md:49) says the same about inheritance.
After the PATCH, GET /v1/assets/{id} returns a 200 body that breaks the published schema invariant.

4. The finder's probe is fair. It sets SANDBOX_PARTNERS directly with _set_scope, but the starting state is reachable through the normal flow. An earlier full-flow probe shows this: docs/review-2026-11-05/repro/contract-sweep/datasets-sharing-deid-assets/test_probe_ctx.py::test_p1_classification_breaks_l2_invariant goes through candidate → legal review → APPROVE → TIER2 agreement → share=APPLIED, then classifies.

5. It is not recorded anywhere as known or decided. That earlier probe file exists, but contract-sweep-findings.md:299 says this bundle produced no findings because the finder stopped at its usage limit. HANDOFF.md:64 and docs/GPT-HANDOFF-PROMPT.md:173 list the bundle as unchecked. No ADR or review README mentions it.

Side: fix_impl is right. The invariant and the docstring settle that behaviour must change. Only how it changes is a product choice.

Severity: high is right. Data an operator has just marked security-sensitive stays shared with partners at L2/L3 (and the asset is still within the L2+ range).
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I reproduced it independently with a different setup from the finder's. The finder's probe set the scope directly with sharing._set_scope and skipped the 2-tier agreement. My probe goes through the real flow instead: candidate, START/PASS_LEGAL_REVIEW, mark_processed, APPROVE, a TIER2 agreement, then a POST share-requests to SANDBOX_PARTNERS with state=APPLIED.

What the probe showed:
(1) An operator (106) sends PATCH classification sensitivity_class=SECURITY on the L2 DEID. The response is 200. GET /v1/assets/{id} then still returns sharing_scope=SANDBOX_PARTNERS with sensitivity_class=SECURITY. The contract check then fails with ['sensitivity_class']: 'SECURITY' is not one of ['INTERNAL', 'RESTRICTED'], which is the asset-metadata allOf "L2 이상 등급의 불변식". The other contract error, checksum_sha256, is fixture noise and was already there before the change. rules.max_scope_for now gives PROGRAM_REVIEW, and evaluate(L2) reports unmet 'sensitivity_class ≠ SECURITY'.
(2) Setting the ORIGINAL to SECURITY returns 200. The DEID keeps SANDBOX_PARTNERS while effective_sensitivity=SECURITY and max_scope_for=PROGRAM_REVIEW.

Why this happens in the code:
- backend/app/api/sharing.py:192-202 only assigns the fields and writes the audit row. Its own docstring at line 180 promises that the rules are re-checked when a value becomes stricter.
- Nothing else re-checks either. A grep for max_scope_for and downgrade( in app/ finds only review_view (for display) and the data_role→CORE path in assets.py:139-145.
- The exposure reaches past the asset's own row. services/datasets.py:59-64 (version_scope) uses each item's stored sharing_scope, and visible_versions (datasets.py:299-302) and the grant path (datasets.py:383) check only that level.
