So a dataset version that already holds the asset at L3+ stays visible to consumers and can still be granted after the asset is marked SECURITY.
- _validate (datasets.py:143) re-runs evaluate, so new dataset versions do block the asset. Existing versions and existing grants do not.

This is not already known. docs/review-2026-11-05 has an earlier unfinished probe for it (repro/contract-sweep/datasets-sharing-deid-assets/test_probe_ctx.py::test_p1_classification_breaks_l2_invariant), but contract-sweep-findings.md records it under '점검하지 못한 묶음' with no finding. Nothing in HANDOFF section 5 or the ADRs covers it.

Verdict on side and severity: fix_impl is right. The invariant is part of the contract, and domain.md section 4 says SECURITY cannot go above L1. High is right too: data an operator has just marked security-sensitive stays shared at L2 or higher.
- **고칠 쪽:** 구현 — After the change, for the asset and every descendant, compute rules.max_scope_for(facts_for(...)). Where the current sharing_scope is above it, call sharing.downgrade(..., 'REVOKE_NOW', ...) to that level (L1 at most for SECURITY), the same way as the data_role→CORE change in assets.py:139-145, and record the lowered assets in the ASSET_CLASSIFIED details. The other option is to refuse with 422 RULE_VIOLATION until ORG_ADMIN lowers the scope. Which of the two to use, and whether REVOKE_NOW is the right default for grant handling, is a product choice. The invariant itself is not optional.

### S12. [medium] POST /v1/assets/{asset_id}/share-review — missing-behavior

**RESUBMIT moves a NONE share_status straight to CANDIDATE without the data_role=BYPRODUCT check (CORE originals enter review)**

- **계약:** actOnShareReview summary (openapi.yaml:2039): '심사 단계 처리 (domain.md 5장 share_status 표의 주체만)'. domain.md:119 'NONE → CANDIDATE | ORG_ADMIN, ORG_MEMBER | data_role=BYPRODUCT'. domain.md:127 'REJECTED → CANDIDATE | ORG_ADMIN | 보완 후 재신청'. markShareCandidates (openapi.yaml:1979): 'BYPRODUCT 원본만 CANDIDATE'. Requirement 13-1.1 and 13-4.1.
- **구현:** ACTIONS maps RESUBMIT to target CANDIDATE. Only the (current, target) pair is looked up in states.SHARE, and (NONE, CANDIDATE) is allowed for ORG. So RESUBMIT on a NONE asset by ORG_MEMBER or ORG_ADMIN succeeds. The data_role check exists only in sharing.mark_candidates. A CORE original that share-candidates excludes with reason CORE can be pushed to CANDIDATE this way, and the operator can then start legal review. PASS_LEGAL_REVIEW would then queue a deidentify job for CORE data. ORG_MEMBER also gets the RESUBMIT action, which is meant for ORG_ADMIN only.
- **근거:** backend/app/services/sharing.py:199-212 (ACTIONS, states.transition only); backend/app/domain/states.py:35,43; backend/app/api/sharing.py:154-159; the BYPRODUCT check exists only at backend/app/services/sharing.py:180
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_shr_a.py::test_resubmit_from_none_skips_byproduct_rule — share-candidates CORE: 200 {'candidates': [], 'excluded': [{... 'reason': 'CORE'}]}; RESUBMIT on NONE CORE original as 102: 200 CANDIDATE; stored: CORE CANDIDATE; START_LEGAL_REVIEW on CORE: 200 LEGAL_REVIEW
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — The finding holds on both reading the code and running it. Contract: actOnShareReview (contracts/openapi.yaml, the share-review post summary) says '심사 단계 처리 (domain.md 5장 share_status 표의 주체만)'. domain.md:119 makes NONE → CANDIDATE conditional on data_role=BYPRODUCT, and domain.md:127 gives RESUBMIT's meaning, REJECTED → CANDIDATE by ORG_ADMIN after 보완. Spec 13 requirements.md:30 adds 'data_role=BYPRODUCT인 자산만 share_status=CANDIDATE'. Implementation: backend/app/services/sharing.py ACTIONS maps RESUBMIT to CANDIDATE. review() checks only variant=ORIGINAL plus states.transition('share_status', current, target, roles). states.SHARE[('NONE','CANDIDATE')] = ORG (backend/app/domain/states.py:35), so a NONE CORE original passes. The route in backend/app/api/sharing.py act_share_review raises 403 only when the role is not in the allowed set, and ORG is allowed. The data_role check exists only in mark_candidates (sharing.py, `if a.data_role != "BYPRODUCT"`). I ran the finder's probe and it passed: the CORE asset was excluded by share-candidates, RESUBMIT then gave 200 CANDIDATE with a NONE→CANDIDATE review event, the stored row was CORE/CANDIDATE, and the operator's START_LEGAL_REVIEW gave 200 LEGAL_REVIEW. None of these record it as known or decided: docs/review-2026-11-05, docs/review-2026-11-12, HANDOFF.md or the ADRs. The finder's earlier repro (probe_ctx2) covered a different case (CANDIDATE→LEGAL_REVIEW by a member). One part of the finding is wrong: 'ORG_MEMBER also gets the RESUBMIT action, which is meant for ORG_ADMIN only.' domain.md allows ORG_MEMBER for NONE → CANDIDATE, so the member reaching CANDIDATE is not a separate role defect. The defects are that RESUBMIT is accepted from a state other than REJECTED, and that the BYPRODUCT condition is skipped on this path. The existing test test_sharing.py:165 already pins that a member gets 403 on RESUBMIT from REJECTED. Severity medium is right. ADR-0019 keeps a CORE original's derivatives at L1 or below, so this does not open sharing beyond L1 directly. It does break a documented condition, lets CORE data into legal review, and lets PASS_LEGAL_REVIEW queue deidentify on CORE data. fix_impl is right because contract and domain.md agree.
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I reproduced it with my own probe, written separately from the finder's. The finder's probe tries MEMBER first and stops at the first 200, so I parametrized over both roles and assert the result. The asset is a fresh test_catalog._asset original, and I assert it is CORE / ORIGINAL / NONE before the calls. Output for 102 (ORG_MEMBER) and for 101 (ORG_ADMIN) is the same. share-candidates returns 200 with excluded reason CORE. Then RESUBMIT returns 200 CANDIDATE, and the stored row is CORE CANDIDATE. An audit row SHARE_REVIEW_RESUBMIT {from: NONE, to: CANDIDATE} is written. The operator's START_LEGAL_REVIEW then returns 200 LEGAL_REVIEW, and PASS_LEGAL_REVIEW with a legal_basis_ref returns 200 PROCESSING, which queues the deidentify job for CORE data (sharing.py review(), PROCESSING branch).

Why it happens: sharing.ACTIONS maps RESUBMIT to CANDIDATE (sharing.py:199-202), and review() only calls states.transition. states.SHARE[(NONE, CANDIDATE)] allows ORG (states.py:35), so the route check (api/sharing.py:154-159) does not object either. The data_role=BYPRODUCT check exists only in mark_candidates (sharing.py:180). domain.md:119 makes NONE->CANDIDATE conditional on data_role=BYPRODUCT, and domain.md:127 limits RESUBMIT-style resubmission (REJECTED->CANDIDATE) to ORG_ADMIN. The contract summary at openapi.yaml:2039 points to that table.

The review documents, HANDOFF and the specs do not record this as known (grep found nothing relevant). Severity stays medium, not high: approval still needs a derived copy with review_result=PASSED, and the scope rules look at ancestors' data_role through rules.Lineal. So a CORE original probably cannot actually reach L2 or higher this way. I did not prove that last step. Side fix_impl is right: the contract and domain.md are clear.
- **고칠 쪽:** 구현 — In sharing.review, accept RESUBMIT only when a.share_status == 'REJECTED' (otherwise raise InvalidStateTransition, 409). Also check data_role == 'BYPRODUCT' on any transition into CANDIDATE (422 RULE_VIOLATION with an unmet condition), so the share-review path cannot do what share-candidates refuses.

### S13. [medium] POST /v1/assets/{asset_id}/share-review — scope-or-permission

**REVIEWER always gets 404 (and a false CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT audit) on share-review and share-requests, even for an assigned review target**

- **계약:** actOnShareReview: '심사 단계 처리 (domain.md 5장 share_status 표의 주체만)'. domain.md:122 'QUALITY_REVIEW → REPROCESS | REVIEWER, PLATFORM_OPERATOR'. domain.md 2장: REVIEWER metadata scope = '배정된 검수 대상'. The Permission_Table grants share:read and share:review to REVIEWER, so GET share-review and GET share-requests are also meant to work for that role.
- **구현:** tenant._role_filter has no branch for REVIEWER and returns false(). load_scoped therefore 404s every asset for a REVIEWER. Because the reviewer's organization (ORG_OPERATOR) differs from the asset owner, each attempt also writes a CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT audit. This happens even when the reviewer holds an ASSIGNED deid_reviews row for that original and its DEID child. The QUALITY_REVIEW → REPROCESS transition the contract and domain table give to REVIEWER cannot be reached on this operation; the same request as PLATFORM_OPERATOR succeeds.
- **근거:** backend/app/services/tenant.py:37-53 (no REVIEWER branch → false()); backend/app/api/deps.py:111-123; backend/app/domain/permissions.py:110,113; backend/app/domain/states.py:40; backend/app/api/sharing.py:36-39,134-137,149-152; for comparison deid reviews scope by assignment at backend/app/api/deid.py:32-34
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_shr_a.py::test_reviewer_cannot_reach_share_review — REVIEWER GET share-review: 404 GET share-requests: 404 POST REQUEST_REPROCESS: 404 NOT_FOUND; CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT rows added: 3; OP POST REQUEST_REPROCESS: 200 REPROCESS
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 — The code facts are right. tenant._role_filter (backend/app/services/tenant.py:37-53) has no REVIEWER branch, so load_scoped (backend/app/api/deps.py:111-123) returns 404 to a REVIEWER on GET/POST share-review and GET share-requests, and records CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT because the owner org differs. But this does not break anything the contract promises, and the finding's main claim is wrong.

(1) The contract gives no role list for getShareReview, actOnShareReview or listShareRequests, and documents 404 for all three (contracts/openapi.yaml:2017-2066). The actOnShareReview summary '심사 단계 처리 (domain.md 5장 share_status 표의 주체만)' limits who may act. It does not promise that every subject in the table can act through this endpoint.

(2) The domain transition QUALITY_REVIEW -> REPROCESS for REVIEWER can be reached, through the designed path. A deid-review REJECT decision calls deid._rerender, which calls sharing.review(..., 'REQUEST_REPROCESS', roles | {'REVIEWER'}, ...) (backend/app/services/deid.py:181-188). The reviewer is scoped by assignment there (backend/app/api/deid.py:32-34). Spec 14 req 4.5/5.1 puts the reviewer's work in Review_Tool, 'assigned targets only'. The web UI sends REVIEWER only to /review (web/src/App.tsx:31,49) and never to /sharing. So the claim 'the QUALITY_REVIEW -> REPROCESS transition ... given to REVIEWER cannot be reached' is false at the domain level. It is only unreachable on this one endpoint, which is not the reviewer's endpoint.

(3) The CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT rows are not 'false'. A reviewer asked for another organisation's asset outside its Tenant_Guard scope. The project rule (security.md / SWEEP rules) is exactly 404 plus that audit. The same thing happens on any Asset route a REVIEWER calls.

What remains is an internal inconsistency, not a contract-vs-implementation mismatch. REVIEWER sits in share:read and share:review in the Permission_Table (backend/app/domain/permissions.py:110,113), but Tenant_Guard never lets that grant through, so the entry does nothing. Whether reviewers should also see the share-review view of assigned originals, or should lose these grants, is a product decision. It is not a defect a client relying on the contract would hit.

Not already recorded: HANDOFF.md and the docs/review-2026-11-05 and docs/review-2026-11-12 READMEs do not mention it. I did not run the finder's probe. Its observed output (404 x3, three CROSS_ORG rows, operator 200) matches the code reading and is not disputed.
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — My own probe reproduced it, and I wrote it independently of the finder's. Setup: a QUALITY_REVIEW BYPRODUCT original with a DEID child linked by lineage, plus an ASSIGNED deid_reviews row whose reviewer_id is usr 108. Seed check: usr 108 has roles ['REVIEWER'] and belongs to org ...0004, not ORG_A. That user's GET /v1/deid-reviews returns the review, so the assignment is real. Every share-sharing call from that user still fails with 404 NOT_FOUND, with or without MFA: GET share-review, GET share-requests and POST share-review REQUEST_REPROCESS. The POST alone writes 1 CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT row for the asset, and share_status stays QUALITY_REVIEW. The same POST as PLATFORM_OPERATOR (106) returns 200 and moves the asset to REPROCESS. Cause, read in code: backend/app/services/tenant.py:37-53 `_role_filter` has no REVIEWER branch and falls through to `return false()`. backend/app/api/deps.py:111-123 `load_scoped` then writes CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT because the reviewer's org differs from the owner org. This contradicts the following. permissions.py:110 and :113 grant share:read and share:review to REVIEWER. states.py:40 and domain.md:124 give QUALITY_REVIEW→REPROCESS to REVIEWER and PLATFORM_OPERATOR. domain.md:41 sets REVIEWER scope to '배정된 검수 대상 | 배정된 대상의 원본·파생본'. The finder's probe tests what it claims: right role, an assignment row, a correct state. Its omission of MFA does not matter, since my mfa=True run gives the same 404. I found no record of this in docs/review-2026-11-05, docs/review-2026-11-12, HANDOFF.md or the ADRs. Severity stays medium, not high. The reviewer can still reach REPROCESS indirectly: a deid-review decision of MISSED or REJECT calls sharing.review(..., 'REQUEST_REPROCESS', roles | {'REVIEWER'}, ...) at backend/app/services/deid.py:181-188. So the core flow is not blocked. What remains: three operations the permission table grants to REVIEWER are unreachable, and each attempt writes a false cross-org audit row that an auditor would see.
- **고칠 쪽:** 구현 — Give REVIEWER an assignment-based scope for Asset: original_asset_id or deid_asset_id in deid_reviews where reviewer_id = principal.user_id (for example a REVIEWER branch in _role_filter for the Asset model using an EXISTS subquery). Do not log a reviewer's out-of-assignment miss as cross-org. If the owner instead decides that reviewers act only through /v1/deid-reviews, remove REVIEWER from share:read/share:review and change the contract summary. Either way the false CROSS_ORG audits must stop.

### S14. [medium] POST /v1/assets/{asset_id}/share-review — missing-audit

**The in-handler 403 for a role outside the transition's actor set writes no PERMISSION_DENIED audit**

- **계약:** 403 is a documented response (openapi.yaml:2063). security.md:26-27 '감사 로그 (반드시 기록): … 권한 거부 …'. The same pattern was accepted as findings #15 and #25 in docs/review-2026-11-05/contract-sweep-findings.md.
- **구현:** When the transition exists but the caller's roles are not in its actor set (for example ORG_MEMBER START_LEGAL_REVIEW, or ORG_ADMIN APPROVE), the handler raises ProblemError('FORBIDDEN') directly. No audit row is written for that request. Permission-table denials in require() do write PERMISSION_DENIED.
- **근거:** backend/app/api/sharing.py:155-157; compare backend/app/api/deps.py:99-105
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_shr_a.py::test_in_route_forbidden_has_no_audit — MEMBER START_LEGAL_REVIEW: 403 FORBIDDEN PERMISSION_DENIED added: 0; audit rows for that request: []; table-level 403 for comparison: 403 PERMISSION_DENIED added: 1
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I could not refute it. The contract and security rule say this denial must be audited, the handler does not audit it, the two earlier cases of the same pattern were fixed, and nothing records this one as known or decided. (1) Contract: actOnShareReview documents 403 Forbidden (contracts/openapi.yaml, post /v1/assets/{asset_id}/share-review responses), and its summary limits the action to the actors in the domain.md 5장 share_status table. .kiro/steering/security.md:26-27 lists 권한 거부 under 감사 로그 (반드시 기록). (2) Implementation: backend/app/domain/permissions.py:113 gives share:review to PROVIDER|PLATFORM_OPERATOR|REVIEWER, so ORG_MEMBER and ORG_ADMIN pass require(). backend/app/domain/states.py:36 says CANDIDATE->LEGAL_REVIEW is PLATFORM_OPERATOR only. backend/app/api/sharing.py:155-157 then raises ProblemError('FORBIDDEN') with no c.audit.record. Permission-table denials do write PERMISSION_DENIED in a separate commit (backend/app/api/deps.py:99-105). (3) Precedent: the 2026-11-05 sweep accepted the same pattern as #15 (break-glass self-decision) and #25 (operator device enrollment). Both are now fixed with an in-route PERMISSION_DENIED audit (backend/app/api/break_glass.py:84, backend/app/api/context.py:101-103). So the project already treats in-route 403s as needing an audit. (4) I found nothing marking this one as known, decided or intended: I grepped HANDOFF.md, docs/review-2026-11-05/*, docs/review-2026-11-12/README.md and docs/adr for share-review, 공유 심사 and PERMISSION_DENIED. (5) Re-running the finder's probe gave the same result. MEMBER START_LEGAL_REVIEW returns 403 FORBIDDEN, adds 0 PERMISSION_DENIED rows, and leaves no audit rows for that request_id. The table-level 403 used for comparison adds 1 PERMISSION_DENIED row. Side fix_impl and severity medium are right: an auditor would see a denied attempt to change a share state with no audit row, and the contract text needs no change.
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I reproduced it with my own probe. The finder's probe has a weakness: it looks rows up by the response x-request-id, and the server ignores a client-sent X-Request-Id and makes its own. So my probe also counts every audit_log row before and after each request, which settles it whatever the request-id handling does.

Results (tests/integration/test_zz_shrv3x_a.py::test_actor_mismatch_403_audit, 2 cases, MFA tokens used so MFA is not the cause):
- 102 (ORG_MEMBER) START_LEGAL_REVIEW on CANDIDATE: 403 FORBIDDEN, detail 'CANDIDATE → LEGAL_REVIEW은 PLATFORM_OPERATOR의 일입니다'. Rows for that request: [], total audit delta 0.
- 101 (ORG_ADMIN) APPROVE on QUALITY_REVIEW: 403 FORBIDDEN, total audit delta 0.
- Control on the same asset: 106 (OP) START_LEGAL_REVIEW returns 200 and writes SHARE_REVIEW_START_LEGAL_REVIEW/SUCCESS under its response request id. So the lookup method does find rows when they exist.

The 403 comes from the role check at backend/app/api/sharing.py:155-157, not from require(). share:review is granted to ORG_ADMIN, ORG_MEMBER, OP and REVIEWER (permissions.py:113), so the permission-table check passes and the route's own check refuses. No audit call comes before the raise. In contrast, deps.py:99-105 records PERMISSION_DENIED for permission-table denials.

The contract lists 403 for this operation (openapi.yaml, actOnShareReview responses), and security.md:26-27 requires auditing 권한 거부 (permission denials). The project already fixed this same pattern after earlier findings #15 and #25: break_glass.py:84 and context.py:101 now write PERMISSION_DENIED before their in-handler 403. So this is an implementation gap. No ADR, review document or HANDOFF section 5 entry makes it intended. The side (fix_impl) and severity (medium: missing audit an auditor would hit) are both right.
- **고칠 쪽:** 구현 — Before raising, call c.audit.record('PERMISSION_DENIED', result='DENIED', principal=p, organization_id=a.organization_id, target_type='Asset', target_id=a.asset_id, source_ip=client_ip(request), details={'action': 'share:review/' + body.action, 'from': a.share_status, 'to': target}). Use the separate-commit path like require() and break_glass.py:84 so the audit survives the request rollback.

### S15. [low] POST /v1/assets/{asset_id}/share-requests — wrong-status

**Requesting the current scope gives 409 INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION, which createShareRequest does not document**

- **계약:** createShareRequest responses (openapi.yaml:1955-1973): 201, 400, 401, 403, 404, 422, 429. There is no 409, although the operation also takes Idempotency-Key, whose parameter text promises 409 IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT / 409 CONFLICT (openapi.yaml:4637-4639).
- **구현:** request_scope raises InvalidStateTransition when target_scope == the current sharing_scope, so the response is 409 INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION. run_idempotent can also return 409 IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT or CONFLICT. None of these 409s is in the operation's responses.
- **근거:** backend/app/services/sharing.py:116-117; backend/app/services/idempotency.py:38,82; contracts/openapi.yaml:1955-1973
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_shr_a.py::test_same_scope_request_status — same-scope share-request: 409 INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION; contract check failed: AssertionError POST /v1/assets/{asset_id}/share-requests: 계약에 409 응답이 없다
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I could not refute this. The contract and code say what the finding says. (1) The contract: createShareRequest (contracts/openapi.yaml:1942-1973) lists only 201/400/401/403/404/422/429. Its description says nothing about asking for the scope the asset already has. It does take the IdempotencyKey parameter, and that parameter's text (openapi.yaml:4637-4639) promises 409 IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT and 409 CONFLICT with Retry-After. ShareRequestCreate.target_scope (openapi.yaml:6437) is the plain sharing_scope enum, so a client may send the current scope and it passes the schema. (2) The code: backend/app/services/sharing.py:116-117 raises states.InvalidStateTransition when target == a.sharing_scope. backend/app/errors.py:43 and :126 map that to 409 INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION. backend/app/api/sharing.py:63-65 wraps create() in run_idempotent. backend/app/services/idempotency.py raises ProblemError("IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT") on the same key with a different body, and _busy() raises CONFLICT with Retry-After:1. All of these are 409, and none is in the operation's responses. (3) Nothing records this as known or decided. HANDOFF.md and docs/review-2026-11-12/README.md never mention share-requests. docs/review-2026-11-05/contract-sweep-findings.md never checked this bundle. (4) Side and severity hold. There is a precedent: sweep #23 (POST /v1/devices, same pattern: '409 is implied only by the Idempotency-Key parameter text') was settled by adding 409 to the contract ('#23 장비 등록 — ... 다른 본문으로 같은 멱등 키를 쓸 때의 409 ... 계약에 명시한다'). Rejecting a same-scope request is sensible behaviour, so fix_contract is right. It is an edge case, so low severity is right. The finder's probe (test_zz_shr_a.py::test_same_scope_request_status) checks what it claims. It uses ORG_ADMIN on a PRIVATE deid asset, asks for target PRIVATE, and runs the contract check. Reading the code gives the same 409 it reports. I did not re-run it, because this is the reading lens.
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I reproduced this independently with my own probe. The finder's probe was checked too: it posts as ORG_ADMIN 101 on a fresh DEID asset whose scope is already PRIVATE, so the 409 comes from the same-scope check and not from a role, fixture or MFA problem. My probe uses its own DEID asset, also PRIVATE. Same-scope POST target_scope=PRIVATE returns 409 INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION both with and without MFA. A first L1 request (PROGRAM_REVIEW) with Idempotency-Key returns 201 APPLIED. The same key with a different body returns 409 IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT. All three 409 responses fail assert_matches_contract with "계약에 409 응답이 없다". Code: backend/app/services/sharing.py:116-117 raises InvalidStateTransition when target == cur, and backend/app/api/sharing.py:63-65 wraps create() in run_idempotent. contracts/openapi.yaml:1955-1973 lists only 201/400/401/403/404/422/429, although IdempotencyKey (4637-4639) and api-conventions.md:39-41,55 promise these 409 codes. The components/responses/Conflict (4747) already describes CONFLICT, IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT and INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION, so the fix is only to reference it. I found no ADR, review document or HANDOFF item that records this as known or intended. The earlier sweep treated the same pattern (createDevice #23) as a contract fix. Turning down a no-op change of scope is reasonable behaviour, so fix_contract and low severity are correct.
- **고칠 쪽:** 계약 문구 — Add '409': {$ref: '#/components/responses/Conflict'} to createShareRequest, with a description covering target_scope equal to the current scope (INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION) and the idempotency conflicts. Then regenerate the clients (orchestrator).

### S16. [low] POST /v1/share-candidates — wrong-status

**Accepts asset_ids=[] (with session_id) and duplicate asset_ids although the schema says minItems 1, uniqueItems true**

- **계약:** markShareCandidates request body (openapi.yaml:1991): asset_ids {type: array, minItems: 1, maxItems: 1000, uniqueItems: true}; 400 is documented.
- **구현:** Candidates.asset_ids only sets max_length=1000. The emptiness check at line 98 runs only when session_id is also missing, and duplicates are silently collapsed with set(). Both bodies get 200 instead of 400.
- **근거:** backend/app/api/sharing.py:91,98-101
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_shr_a.py::test_candidates_body_limits — asset_ids=[] with session_id: 200; duplicate asset_ids: 200 {'candidates': ['ast_...'], 'excluded': []}
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I could not refute it. Contract (contracts/openapi.yaml:1991) says markShareCandidates asset_ids is {type: array, minItems: 1, maxItems: 1000, uniqueItems: true}, and 400 BadRequest is documented (line 2011). In the current origin/main code at backend/app/api/sharing.py:89-101, Candidates.asset_ids is `list[str] | None = Field(None, max_length=1000)`, so neither min_length nor a uniqueness check exists. The only emptiness check (line 98, `if not body.asset_ids and not body.session_id`) fires only when session_id is missing too, so {asset_ids: [], session_id: ...} passes. Line 101 runs `sorted(set(body.asset_ids or []))`, which silently collapses duplicates. The finder's probe (test_zz_shr_a.py::test_candidates_body_limits) does send exactly those two bodies as ORG_MEMBER, and its recorded output (200 / 200) matches the code path. The review docs, HANDOFF and ADRs do not record this as known or decided: the only share-candidates mentions in docs/review-2026-11-05 are repro probes using valid bodies, and steering has no rule allowing lenient handling of uniqueItems. Other routes in the same codebase do reject duplicates with 400 VALIDATION_ERROR (assets.py:200-202 code 'unique', webhooks.py:44-46), so rejecting is the house pattern and fix_impl is the right side. Severity stays low: the effect is benign (set-collapse, an empty list ignored when session_id is present) and no data or security impact.
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — My own probe reproduces both cases. The contract for markShareCandidates (contracts/openapi.yaml, /v1/share-candidates requestBody) says asset_ids is {type: array, minItems: 1, maxItems: 1000, uniqueItems: true} and lists 400 BadRequest. The implementation (backend/app/api/sharing.py:91) declares `asset_ids: list[str] | None = Field(None, max_length=1000)`, with no min_length and no uniqueness check. The only emptiness guard (sharing.py:98-100) is `if not body.asset_ids and not body.session_id`, so an empty list is rejected only when session_id is also missing. sharing.py:101 then silently collapses duplicates with `sorted(set(...))`. Probe output as MEMBER 102 (ORG_MEMBER, a PROVIDER role with share:candidate), on a seeded ctx DB with a fresh BYPRODUCT/NONE asset: {asset_ids: []} -> 400 VALIDATION_ERROR (but with errors[].code 'minProperties', not 'minItems'); {asset_ids: [], session_id: SES_A} -> 200 {'candidates': [], 'excluded': []}; {asset_ids: [aid, aid]} -> 200 {'candidates': [aid], 'excluded': []}; {} -> 400. The finder's probe tests what it claims: correct role, real asset, no reliance on stale state. Nothing in the 2026-11-05 or 2026-11-12 review documents, HANDOFF, the steering files or the ADRs records this as known or intended. Other routes do enforce list minimums, for example datasets.py:117 `item_asset_ids: list[str] = Field(min_length=1, ...)`, so fixing the implementation is consistent with the codebase. The impact is a lenient edge case with no data or security effect, so low severity and fix_impl both stand.
- **고칠 쪽:** 구현 — asset_ids: list[str] | None = Field(None, min_length=1, max_length=1000) plus a field_validator that rejects duplicates (400 VALIDATION_ERROR, code uniqueItems). Alternatively drop minItems/uniqueItems from the contract if lenience is intended.

**일치를 확인한 작업**

- GET /v1/assets/{asset_id}/share-requests — share:read roles, tenant 404 + CROSS_ORG audit via load_scoped, oldest-first order (requested_at, id), ShareRequest shape (only the REVIEWER scope gap noted under the share-review finding)
- POST /v1/assets/{asset_id}/share-requests — ORG_ADMIN only (403 for others, audited by require), Sharing_Rules evaluation with 422 unmet_conditions, ORIGINAL/CORE capped at L1, downgrade requires existing_grant_handling (422), L3/L4 → IN_REVIEW, idempotent replay, SHARE_SCOPE_REQUESTED audit (only the undocumented 409 noted)
- POST /v1/share-requests/{share_request_id}/decisions — PLATFORM_OPERATOR only, 404 unknown id, 409 when not IN_REVIEW or scope moved, conditions re-evaluated at decision time (422, and the request stays IN_REVIEW with no decided_* and no audit — probe test_zz_shr_b.py), DENY → DENIED, SHARE_SCOPE_DECIDED audit
- GET /v1/assets/{asset_id}/share-review — ShareReview shape, events in order, derived list and max_scope (apart from the REVIEWER gap)
- POST /v1/assets/{asset_id}/share-review — actor table for the other transitions, legal_basis_ref required for PASS_LEGAL_REVIEW (422), reason required for REJECT/REQUEST_REPROCESS (422), deidentify job queued on PROCESSING, APPROVED copied to PASSED derived assets, WITHDRAW lowers derived assets to ≤L1, org notified on REJECTED/REPROCESS, 409 for transitions not in the table
- POST /v1/share-candidates — BYPRODUCT originals only, CORE / NOT_ORIGINAL / STATUS_<share_status> exclusions, session expansion limited to own org and not-deleted assets, cross-org ids → 404 + audit, 400 when both fields are missing
- PATCH /v1/assets/{asset_id}/classification — PLATFORM_OPERATOR only, basis_ref/reason required, minProperties check, ANONYMIZED_VERIFIED needs a derived asset and legal_basis_ref (422), ASSET_CLASSIFIED audit with before/after/basis_ref

**발견 담당의 메모 (발견으로 올리지 않은 것)**

> The probe files are in the rv worktree and left in place: backend/tests/integration/test_zz_shr_a.py (6 probes) and backend/tests/integration/test_zz_shr_b.py (decision-rollback check, consistent). All print observations and pass. Nothing tracked was edited.
> 
> In test_classification_breaks_shared_invariant, the asset contract check also fails on location_precision and checksum_sha256. Both come from the shared test helper tests/integration/test_catalog.py::_asset, not from the code under test; only the sensitivity_class error is the finding.
> 
> Not reported, because the text is ambiguous or the topic is general or known:
> - share-review events use inconsistent actor values: share-candidates writes the role string ('ORG_MEMBER'), other steps write the principal_id, and deid writes 'deid-review'. The contract does not define actor.
> - GET share-requests is summarised as '공유 등급 변경 이력', but it lists only ShareRequest rows. Downgrades caused by WITHDRAW or by the data_role→CORE change do not appear. The response description says '신청 목록', so the text is ambiguous.
> - WITHDRAW quietly defaults existing_grant_handling to REVOKE_NOW. The contract does not require the field, and the default is the strict choice.
> - The success audits in these handlers are written outside the request transaction (no connection=db.connection()). That is the known general audit-atomicity topic.
> - share-candidates writes one audit row for N assets (target_id = the first one). Candidate marking is not in the security.md must-audit list.
> 
> On the classification finding: choosing between auto-downgrade and refusing with 422, and whether REVOKE_NOW is the right default for grant handling, may need the owner. Either way the stored state must not break the asset-metadata invariant.

## deid (비식별 검수·태그) — 발견 5건

### S17. [medium] GET /v1/deid-reviews/{review_id}/frame — missing-audit

**An operator refused the original frame (no break-glass grant) gets 403 with no PERMISSION_DENIED audit**

- **계약:** contracts/openapi.yaml:2201-2204 getDeidReviewFrame: other organisations' original frames are shown to an operator only with a valid break-glass grant, '없으면 403'. security.md:26-27 '감사 로그 (반드시 기록): … 권한 거부 … 긴급 열람'. The earlier sweep's #15 and #25 treated route-level 403s without this audit as findings, and both were fixed.
- **구현:** The handler raises ProblemError('FORBIDDEN') itself when active_grant_for returns None and writes no audit row. Only permission-table denials in require() record PERMISSION_DENIED. So an operator trying to open an original frame without a grant leaves no trace, while an ORG_MEMBER refused at the table level is audited.
- **근거:** backend/app/api/deid.py:69-75 (raise with no audit); backend/app/api/deps.py:100-105 (only table denials are audited); .kiro/steering/security.md:27
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_deid_a.py::test_frame_operator_original_403_no_permission_denied_audit — OPERATOR ORIGINAL frame: 403 FORBIDDEN PERMISSION_DENIED delta: 0 / MEMBER frame: 403 PERMISSION_DENIED delta: 1
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I tried to refute this and could not. The contract says it: contracts/openapi.yaml, getDeidReviewFrame description, says an operator sees another organisation's original frame only with a valid break-glass grant, "없으면 403". security.md:26-27 lists 권한 거부 and 긴급 열람 among events that must always be audited.

The code behaves as described. In backend/app/api/deid.py:69-75, when active_grant_for() returns None the handler raises ProblemError("FORBIDDEN") itself and writes no audit row. In backend/app/api/deps.py:100-105, only permission-table denials in require() record PERMISSION_DENIED. The operator passes the table check (deid_review:read) and is refused only by the handler.

The project already treats this pattern as a defect and fixes it this way:
- Earlier sweep #15 and #25 (docs/review-2026-11-05/contract-sweep-findings.md:24,42) were the same handler-level 403 with no audit, and both were fixed.
- The fixed code is at backend/app/api/break_glass.py:84 and backend/app/api/context.py:101. Both write PERMISSION_DENIED before raising.

It is not recorded as known or waiting for a decision. HANDOFF section 5 line 273 is about break-glass for assets the operator's own organisation owns, a different question. ADR-0029 does not exempt grantless attempts.

I re-ran the finder's probe in od-rv-py. It prints "OPERATOR ORIGINAL frame: 403 FORBIDDEN PERMISSION_DENIED delta: 0" and "MEMBER frame: 403 PERMISSION_DENIED delta: 1". The test fails at line 62, assert (0 - 0) == 1. Delta 0 shows the 403 comes from the handler, not from require(), which would have written a row.

The finder's side (fix_impl) and severity (medium, a missing audit an auditor would hit) are both right.
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I reproduced it with my own probe, which looks up audit rows by X-Request-Id rather than counting by action. Operator 106 (PLATFORM_OPERATOR in ORG_OPERATOR, tried with and without MFA) asked for which=ORIGINAL on an ORG_A review assigned to 108, with no break-glass grant. The response was 403 FORBIDDEN with the handler's own detail '운영자의 원본 프레임 열람은 유효한 긴급 열람 승인이 있어야 합니다', and audit_log held 0 rows for that request_id. That detail text proves the 403 came from deid.py:69-75 (active_grant_for returned None, then raise). It did not come from require() or an MFA gate, because the permission table (permissions.py:128, deid_review:read = REVIEWER and PLATFORM_OPERATOR, no MFA) lets the operator through. Two controls back this up. The same operator asking for which=DEID got past the grant check and only failed later at FakeStorage.iter_object, so it is not a permission problem. An ORG_MEMBER (102) request in the same test wrote ('PERMISSION_DENIED','DENIED') under its request_id, so the request_id lookup works. The contract (openapi.yaml getDeidReviewFrame: '없으면 403') and security.md:26 (권한 거부 must be audited) match the finding. This is the same pattern as earlier findings #15 and #25, both fixed by writing PERMISSION_DENIED before raising (break_glass.py:84, context.py:101). I found nothing in the 2026-11-12 review README or HANDOFF that marks it as known or waiting for a decision. The finder's probe is sound (right role, right fixture, and the 403 has the right cause). Severity medium and side fix_impl are right.
- **고칠 쪽:** 구현 — Before raising FORBIDDEN, write c.audit.record('PERMISSION_DENIED', result='DENIED', principal=p, organization_id=a.organization_id, target_type='Asset', target_id=a.asset_id, details={'action':'deid_review:frame_original','reason':'NO_BREAK_GLASS_GRANT','review_id':...}). It must stay recorded although the request fails, so keep it on the separate-commit path. The download-url route (assets.py:309-312) has the same gap but belongs to another group.

### S18. [medium] POST /v1/deid-reviews/{review_id}/decision — missing-behavior

**REJECT (and MISSED with manual_regions) returns 200 but nothing is reprocessed when the original is no longer in QUALITY_REVIEW**

- **계약:** contracts/openapi.yaml:2235 summary: '통과·미탐(같은 회차 재검수, 수동 가림 영역이면 다시 렌더링)·반려(재처리)'. 409 Conflict is documented for this operation.
- **구현:** decide() sets the derived review_result=FAILED and calls _rerender. That moves share_status only from QUALITY_REVIEW/REPROCESS, but enqueues a deidentify job in every case. deidentify() then returns SKIPPED because the original is not PROCESSING. The client gets 200 status=REJECTED, the derived is FAILED for good, and no new derived or review is ever made. This can be reached without misuse: _recheck_session re-flags every PASSED review in the session whatever the original's share_status, so a recheck of an already APPROVED original that the reviewer rejects ends this way. It also happens after the share is withdrawn (NONE) or rejected by the operator, because neither cancels the ASSIGNED review.
- **근거:** backend/app/services/deid.py:172-175, 180-191 (_rerender guards), 51-53 (deidentify skips unless PROCESSING), 194-200 (recheck has no share_status filter); backend/app/services/sharing.py:228-233 (withdrawal leaves reviews ASSIGNED); .kiro/steering/domain.md:124 (only QUALITY_REVIEW → REPROCESS exists)
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_deid_a.py::test_reject_decision_without_reprocess[APPROVED-True|NONE-False|REJECTED-False] — share=APPROVED: decision 200 REJECTED; job=True deidentify=SKIPPED; child.review_result=FAILED; open reviews=0 (same for NONE and REJECTED)
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I could not refute it. The code, the contract and the docs all match the finding. (1) Contract: contracts/openapi.yaml decideDeidReview has the summary '반려(재처리)' and 'MISSED with 수동 가림 영역이면 다시 렌더링', and it lists 409. Spec 14 요구사항 4.3 says WHEN 수동 가림 영역 추가 → 다시 렌더링. (2) Code: backend/app/api/deid.py:122-138 does not check share_status before calling deid.decide. In deid.py:172-175, REJECT sets child.review_result=FAILED and calls _rerender. _rerender (deid.py:180-191) changes the original only when it is in QUALITY_REVIEW or REPROCESS, but at :189 it enqueues 'deidentify' in every case. deidentify (deid.py:51-53) returns SKIPPED unless share_status==PROCESSING, so no new derived and no new review are ever made. (3) All three states can be reached. APPROVED: _recheck_session (deid.py:194-214) re-assigns every PASSED review in the session with no share_status filter, and the earlier sweep probe docs/review-2026-11-05/repro/.../test_probe_ctx3.py::test_p13 drives exactly this flow (APPROVED original, recheck after MISSED). NONE: domain.md 5장 allows '모든 상태 → NONE', and sharing.review (sharing.py:224-229) changes only the derived assets' scope and share_status, never DeidReview. REJECTED: QUALITY_REVIEW → REJECTED is allowed for the operator, and it also leaves the review ASSIGNED. (4) The issue is not recorded anywhere. HANDOFF 5장 has no deid-review item. contract-sweep-findings.md lists this bundle as unchecked, and P13 never became a finding. (5) I re-ran the finder's probe test_zz_deid_a.py::test_reject_decision_without_reprocess. All 3 parametrizations fail at `assert out == "OK"` with 'SKIPPED' == 'OK', and the log shows deid_skipped, so the 200 really comes with a skipped reprocess. A caveat on the probe: it builds the states directly instead of driving them through the flows. I checked by reading that each state is reachable. The side, needs_owner, is right. domain.md 5장 has no APPROVED → REPROCESS transition, and it does not say whether a withdrawal or a share rejection cancels open reviews. domain.md cannot be changed here. Severity stays medium. The APPROVED-recheck case is the real one: a reviewer's REJECT/MISSED is silently a no-op, and the derived asset stays FAILED with no follow-up. In the NONE and REJECTED cases, not reprocessing is arguably what was intended (the share was withdrawn or rejected, and a resubmit → PROCESSING starts a fresh deid job). There the defect is only the misleading 200 plus an orphan job that is queued and then skipped.
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I confirmed this with my own probe, which reaches the bad state through the real API instead of writing it straight into the database as the finder's probe does. The finder's probe puts an ASSIGNED review on an APPROVED/NONE/REJECTED original directly. Mine gets there the way a user would:
(1) The reviewer sends PASS on review A.
(2) The operator approves original A (POST /v1/assets/{id}/share-review APPROVE, 200, APPROVED).
(3) The reviewer sends MISSED on review B in the same session. _recheck_session opens a recheck review A2 for A's child even though A is APPROVED (deid.py:194-214 has no share_status filter).
(4) The reviewer sends REJECT on A2.
Output: `REJECT on recheck: 200 REJECTED; deidentify=['SKIPPED']; orig.share=APPROVED child.review_result=FAILED child.share=APPROVED; open reviews=0 derived count=1`.

Second path: ORG_ADMIN withdraws while review is ASSIGNED (share-review WITHDRAW, 200, NONE), then the reviewer sends REJECT. Output: `REJECT after withdraw: 200 REJECTED; deidentify=['SKIPPED']; orig.share=NONE child.review_result=FAILED`.

Cause in the code: _rerender (deid.py:180-191) changes share_status only when it is QUALITY_REVIEW or REPROCESS, but it always enqueues the job. deidentify (deid.py:51-53) then returns SKIPPED for any original that is not PROCESSING. So the contract's '반려(재처리)' promise is broken. The client gets 200 and no reprocessing happens, no new derived asset, no new review.

A side effect not in the finding: after the APPROVED recheck path, the child keeps share_status=APPROVED with review_result=FAILED.

Not already recorded: contract-sweep-findings.md 297-300 lists this bundle as never checked, and HANDOFF, review-2026-11-12 and the ADRs say nothing on it. Severity medium fits: a client gets 200 for a reprocess that never happens and nobody is told, but nothing leaks and nothing is lost. needs_owner is right. domain.md 5장 has no APPROVED→REPROCESS transition and does not say whether a withdrawal or rejection cancels open reviews, and domain.md may not be changed.
- **고칠 쪽:** 사람 결정 — The owner must decide: (a) what a recheck REJECT/MISSED means for an APPROVED original (domain.md 5장 has no APPROVED → REPROCESS, and domain.md may not be changed here); (b) whether a withdrawal or rejection should cancel open reviews. Until then: refuse with 409 INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION when the original's share_status is not QUALITY_REVIEW/REPROCESS and the result needs reprocessing, and say so in the contract instead of returning 200 and queueing a job that will be skipped.

### S19. [medium] GET /v1/deid-reviews/{review_id}/frame — scope-or-permission

**An operator gets DEID frames of derivatives that are no longer under review (share withdrawn, review decided)**

- **계약:** contracts/openapi.yaml:2202 '운영자는 비식별본(심사 중인 파생본)만 보고'. domain.md:40 PLATFORM_OPERATOR binary: '원본은 긴급 열람 승인으로만. 파생본은 심사 중인 대상만'.
- **구현:** For which=DEID the only check is _review(), which lets an operator open any review. Neither the review status nor the original's share_status is checked, so frames of a derived whose share was withdrawn (share_status NONE) and whose review is already PASSED are still served to the operator.
- **근거:** backend/app/api/deid.py:28-36, 64-69 (no under-review condition for DEID); .kiro/steering/domain.md:40
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_deid_a.py::test_operator_deid_frame_after_share_withdrawn — OPERATOR DEID frame on withdrawn share, decided review: 200 image/jpeg
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I could not refute it. Reading the contract and the current code confirms the finding.

Contract: contracts/openapi.yaml:2202 says the operator sees only DEID frames of derivatives that are '심사 중'. domain.md:40 (PLATFORM_OPERATOR, binary column) says '파생본은 심사 중인 대상만'. In this project '심사' means share review (share_status, which runs per original). Evidence: domain.md:79 and :90 (NONE→CANDIDATE→LEGAL_REVIEW→PROCESSING→QUALITY_REVIEW→APPROVED/REJECTED/REPROCESS), 14 requirements.md:4 '공유 후보로 심사 중인 자산', and services/deid.py:53, which only processes originals whose share_status is PROCESSING.

Implementation: backend/app/api/deid.py:32-36 `_review()` lets any non-machine PLATFORM_OPERATOR open any review, whatever its status. review_frame (deid.py:64-76) checks only for which=ORIGINAL, and only when the asset belongs to another org, against a break-glass grant. For which=DEID nothing checks rv.status, the original's share_status or the derived's state. The frame is cut from the DEID binary, served with 200 image/jpeg and audited as DEID_REVIEW_FRAME_VIEWED.

The state is reachable. Share WITHDRAW (services/sharing.py:201, 228-232) and a data_role change to CORE (api/assets.py:146-147) both set share_status to NONE and leave the DeidReview row in place, so the operator can still pull frames afterwards. In the CORE case those frames come from data the provider just marked competitive.

This is not recorded anywhere as known or decided. HANDOFF -1u and the table row at :273 cover only the ORIGINAL frame and operator-org-owned assets. ADR-0029 and the review-2026-11-05/11-12 documents have no entry for operator access to DEID frames.

The other operator binary path does not cover this either. download-url (assets.py:303-309) requires break-glass for any cross-org variant, so this frame endpoint is the only place the '파생본은 심사 중인 대상만' rule could be applied, and it is not applied.

The finder's probe (test_zz_deid_a.py::test_operator_deid_frame_after_share_withdrawn) uses user 106, a PLATFORM_OPERATOR in ORG_OPERATOR (seed.py:55), share=NONE, review PASSED and a real video on moto S3. It tests what it claims. I did not re-run it (reading lens), but the code path makes the 200 certain.

Severity medium is right: an operator reads a binary outside the domain table's scope. Every view is audited, the content is de-identified and no tenant user is exposed. Side fix_impl is right: the contract and domain.md agree, and the code lacks the check.
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I reproduced it on my own through the real API flow, without setting any state by hand. Setup: a BYPRODUCT original in QUALITY_REVIEW, a DEID child linked by lineage relation=DEID with an uploaded video (ictx/moto), and an ASSIGNED review for reviewer 108. Steps: (1) operator 106 GET frame?which=DEID returned 200, which is legitimate because the share is under review. (2) Reviewer POST decision PASS returned 200, so the review is PASSED. (3) ORG_ADMIN 101 (mfa) POST /v1/assets/{oid}/share-review action=WITHDRAW returned 200. The state is now orig=NONE, child=NONE, review=PASSED. (4) Operator GET frame?which=DEID still returned 200 image/jpeg. Probe output: "ASSIGNED/QUALITY_REVIEW: 200; decision 200; PASSED/QUALITY_REVIEW: 200; withdraw 200 NONE; state(orig,child,review)=('NONE', 'NONE', 'PASSED'); after withdraw: 200". Code: backend/app/api/deid.py:32-36 `_review` lets any non-machine PLATFORM_OPERATOR open any review. In review_frame (deid.py:64-77) the only extra check covers which=ORIGINAL on another organisation's asset, which needs break-glass. For which=DEID nothing checks the review status or the original's share_status. Contract openapi.yaml:2202 says '운영자는 비식별본(심사 중인 파생본)만', and domain.md:40 says PLATFORM_OPERATOR binaries '파생본은 심사 중인 대상만'. Download-url (assets.py:303-311) never gives an operator another organisation's derivative without a grant, so this frame route is the only derivative path, and it has no 'under review' limit. The finder's probe set share NONE directly. That shortcut is valid, and my probe confirms the same state is reachable through the real WITHDRAW transition (domain.md 5장 '모든 상태 → NONE, ORG_ADMIN'). This is not listed as known or decided. HANDOFF -1u fixed only the ORIGINAL frame, and its open owner item is about operator-org-owned originals, not derivatives. Severity medium fits: it is a binary-scope violation of domain.md 2장. The derivative is de-identified, but after a MISSED/REJECTED decision its frames can still contain unblurred regions. Side fix_impl.
- **고칠 쪽:** 구현 — For a non-assigned operator with which=DEID, require that the original is still in share review (share_status in PROCESSING/QUALITY_REVIEW/REPROCESS) or the review is ASSIGNED. Otherwise return 403 with a PERMISSION_DENIED audit, or require a break-glass grant as download-url does. Confirm the exact meaning of '심사 중' with the owner if it is unclear.

### S20. [low] POST /v1/deid-reviews/{review_id}/decision — data-semantics

**A second MISSED in the same session opens a duplicate recheck review for a derived whose recheck is still pending**

- **계약:** contracts/openapi.yaml:2235 '미탐(같은 회차 재검수 …)', 200 '결정된 검수 (재검수로 표시한 검수 ID 포함)'. Spec 14 요구사항 4.4: mark the session's deid results for recheck.
- **구현:** _recheck_session selects other reviews in the session with status PASSED and assigns a new recheck for each. The original PASSED review stays PASSED, so every later MISSED in the session assigns another recheck for the same derived even while an earlier one is still ASSIGNED. Reviewers get duplicate work, and recheck_review_ids reports a new review for something already marked.
- **근거:** backend/app/services/deid.py:194-214 (dedupes only within one call; does not check existing ASSIGNED reviews for the same deid_asset_id)
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_deid_a.py::test_recheck_creates_duplicate_open_reviews — first MISSED recheck ids: ['rvw_…V81'] second MISSED recheck ids: ['rvw_…08R'] open reviews for A's child: 2
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I could not refute it, and the finder's probe fails the same way when I run it. Code: backend/app/services/deid.py:194-214 `_recheck_session` selects reviews in the session with `status == "PASSED"` other than the one being decided, and dedupes only through a per-call `seen` set. `assign()` (deid.py:129-148) always inserts a new ASSIGNED DeidReview and never looks for an existing open review on the same deid_asset_id. Nothing changes the original PASSED review when a recheck is opened, and child.review_result=None does not stop it being selected. So every later MISSED in the same session opens another recheck for the same derived, even while an earlier one is still ASSIGNED. The probe test_zz_deid_a.py::test_recheck_creates_duplicate_open_reviews is sound: three pairs share SES_A, A's review is PASSED, B and C are ASSIGNED, the REVIEWER decides B then C as MISSED, and the test counts ASSIGNED reviews on A's child. I ran it in od-rv-py and it fails at line 100 (`assert len(open_a) == 1`). The pattern can be hit more ways than the probe shows: if B's MISSED rechecks C, and that recheck is later decided MISSED, A gets a second recheck too. Contract: openapi.yaml decideDeidReview says '미탐(같은 회차 재검수…)' and the 200 response 'recheck_review_ids' means reviews it marked for recheck. Spec 14 req 4.4 says to mark the session's deid results as recheck targets. That reads as marking a state, not adding work each time. A second open review for something already marked is extra reviewer workload and adds to the reviewer's open count in `_pick_reviewer`. Neither HANDOFF, docs/review-2026-11-05 (P13 there is a different L2-invariant issue), docs/review-2026-11-12 nor any ADR records this as known or intended. Severity low and side fix_impl are right: it is an edge case with no data loss or leak, and the contract text is fine.
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I reproduced the finding with my own probe. My setup was independent of the finder's: three DEID pairs in seed session SES_A, A with a PASSED review, B and C with ASSIGNED reviews, no other reviews in the session beforehand, a REVIEWER account with MFA, and real POST /decision calls. Both MISSED decisions returned 200. The first returned recheck_review_ids ['rvw_…CMQ8'] and the second ['rvw_…C2P']. After that, A's derived had three reviews: the original PASSED one plus two ASSIGNED recheck=True reviews for the same reviewer, and the operator's GET /v1/deid-reviews?status=ASSIGNED listed both. The cause is in backend/app/services/deid.py:194-214. _recheck_session selects reviews in the session with status == "PASSED" and deduplicates only inside one call (the `seen` set). It never checks for an ASSIGNED review already open on the same deid_asset_id. The original PASSED review stays PASSED after a recheck is created; only child.review_result is set to None. So every later MISSED in the session creates another recheck. The finder's probe tests what it claims: _pair in test_zz_deid_a.py uses SES_A for all three reviews, the reviewer role is right, and the result does not depend on stale state. Spec 14 requirement 4.4 says the session's deid results are to be 'marked' (표시) for recheck. Marking something that is already marked should not create new work, and the contract's 200 text, 'the review IDs marked for recheck', implies the same. I found nothing in HANDOFF, the review documents or the ADRs that records this as known or intended. Severity low (duplicate reviewer work, misleading recheck_review_ids, no data loss or leak) and side fix_impl are both right.
- **고칠 쪽:** 구현 — In _recheck_session, skip a deid_asset_id that already has an ASSIGNED review (or return that existing review's ID instead of creating a new one).

### S21. [low] PATCH /v1/assets/{asset_id}/tags — data-semantics

**Adding a tag that a model already set records a HUMAN ADD, but the tag stays source=MODEL, and the weather correction can later delete it**

- **계약:** contracts/openapi.yaml:2346 '태그 사람 수정 (이력 기록)'. AssetTags.tags[].source enum [MODEL, HUMAN] (5337). tagging.apply_weather docstring and spec 14 요구사항 6.2/6.3: human tags (HUMAN) are not touched by the correction.
- **구현:** edit_tags inserts the HUMAN row with ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING on (asset_id, tag, t_offset), so a whole-asset MODEL tag with the same name blocks it. It still records an ADD edit by the user. The response then shows the tag as source=MODEL next to a human ADD in the history. Because the row is still MODEL, apply_weather deletes a FOG the person explicitly added. A REMOVE of a tag that does not exist is also recorded as an edit.
- **근거:** backend/app/services/tagging.py:205-215; backend/app/db/models.py:1135 (UniqueConstraint asset_id, tag, t_offset); backend/app/services/tagging.py:164-169 (deletes source=MODEL rows)
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_deid_a.py::test_patch_tags_history_vs_state and ::test_human_added_tag_then_removed_by_weather_model — PATCH tags: 200 tags: [('FOG', 'MODEL')] edits: [('ADD', 'FOG'), ('REMOVE', 'BUOY')] / weather: {'removed': ['FOG']} tags after: [] edits: [('ADD','FOG','usr_'), ('REMOVE','FOG','env-')]
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I could not refute the finding. The code works as described, and both probes fail the way the finder says (re-run in od-rv-py: "2 failed in 16.41s").

What the code does:
- backend/app/services/tagging.py:205-210 (edit_tags) inserts the HUMAN row at t_offset=-1.0 with .on_conflict_do_nothing(), then always adds an AssetTagEdit with action=ADD.
- backend/app/db/models.py:1135 has UniqueConstraint(asset_id, tag, t_offset). So when a whole-asset MODEL row with the same tag already exists, the insert silently does nothing and the row stays source=MODEL.
- apply_weather (tagging.py:162-170) deletes every source=MODEL row with that tag and writes a REMOVE edit as env-weather@1.

This contradicts what the code itself promises:
- The apply_weather docstring (tagging.py:134) says "사람이 단 태그(HUMAN)는 건드리지 않는다".
- The AssetTag docstring (models.py:1129) says "사람이 고친 것은 source=HUMAN".
- The contract (openapi.yaml:2346, "태그 사람 수정 (이력 기록)") and the source enum [MODEL, HUMAN] at 5337 only make sense if a person's ADD shows up as HUMAN.

The scenario happens in real use, not just in the probe:
- The only whole-asset (t_offset=-1) FOG/HIGH_WAVE rows come from apply_weather itself. Object FOG rows from tag_scenes use t_offset=t0.
- apply_weather runs again after an external backfill (services/external.py:223) and after env_join (workers/main.py:143).
- So this sequence works: low visibility adds a MODEL FOG; a person confirms it with ADD; a later backfill reports clear visibility; the FOG the person confirmed is deleted. The edit history then shows a human ADD followed by a model REMOVE.
- The same mismatch (the ADD is recorded but the row stays MODEL) also happens for DAY/NIGHT, though apply_weather never deletes those.

Nothing in HANDOFF.md, docs/review-2026-11-05, docs/review-2026-11-12 or the ADRs records this as known or decided. The only related HANDOFF.md line (161) says the weather correction is done and its threshold values still need confirming.

Severity is low: it is an edge case, and the only loss is a tag label (plus a wrong source in the response).

Side: fix_impl for the ADD/upsert part. The secondary claim, that a no-op REMOVE is recorded as an edit, is not a contract violation. Spec 14 requirement 6.2 only asks that the edit history be kept, and the contract says nothing about whether no-op edits count. If anything changes there, it is optional.
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I reproduced it with my own probe, using only the normal production order of events: no hand-inserted MODEL row and no second model ref. The weather correction (the env_join worker and external.refresh_env both call tagging.apply_weather) adds FOG as source=MODEL, model_ref=env-weather@1 when visibility is 300 m. A member (user 102, asset:write) then sends PATCH {add:[FOG]} and gets 200, but the tag stays [('FOG','MODEL','env-weather@1')] even though the history now shows ADD FOG by usr_...102. Later the observations are joined again with visibility 20000 m, and apply_weather returns {'removed':['FOG']}. GET then returns tags [] with edits [ADD FOG usr_..102, REMOVE FOG env-weather@1]. So the FOG the person confirmed is deleted. That contradicts the apply_weather docstring (tagging.py:135, "사람이 단 태그(HUMAN)는 건드리지 않는다") and the summary of the requirement 6.2 operation ('태그 사람 수정'). Cause: edit_tags (tagging.py:205-209) inserts the HUMAN row with on_conflict_do_nothing against UniqueConstraint(asset_id, tag, t_offset) (models.py:1135), but still adds the ADD edit row. apply_weather (tagging.py:164-169) then deletes every source=MODEL row with that tag. The finder's probe is valid too: correct role, and it uses the real PATCH and GET routes. It does seed the MODEL row by hand with the vision model's ref, but my probe shows the same result arising naturally from the weather model. The second part also reproduces: PATCH {remove:[BUOY]} on an asset with no tags returns 200 and records REMOVE BUOY. That part is weaker, though. The contract (openapi.yaml:2346, AssetTags.edits) does not say whether edits that change nothing are recorded, and keeping the human's intent in the history is defensible. I found nothing in HANDOFF section 5 or the review READMEs that already records this. Low severity is right: it is an edge case with no leak, though it does lose a human tag.
- **고칠 쪽:** 구현 — On a human ADD, upsert: change an existing row with the same (asset_id, tag, t_offset) to source=HUMAN, model_ref=NULL, confidence=NULL. Record REMOVE edits only when a row was actually deleted (or say in the contract that no-op edits are kept).

**일치를 확인한 작업**

- GET /v1/deid-reviews — reviewers see only reviews assigned to them and operators see all, as the summary says. The status enum is validated (an invalid value gives 400 through RequestValidationError). Permission is REVIEWER/PLATFORM_OPERATOR, and API keys/devices are refused because the rule has no scope.
- GET /v1/deid-reviews/{review_id} — a review that is not assigned to the caller, or does not exist, gives 404 (요구사항 4.5). The response matches the DeidReview schema, and the processing record carries the '처리 이력' note.
- GET /v1/deid-quality — operators get every organisation and ORG_ADMIN only their own (deid.quality_report organization_id filter). Required fields are present and the note is '처리 이력'. Other roles get 403 with a PERMISSION_DENIED audit.
- GET /v1/assets/{asset_id}/tags — permission asset:read with load_scoped: a cross-organisation request gets 404 plus CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT; PROGRAM_VIEWER sees only L1 assets of assigned projects; deleted assets are hidden. The response matches AssetTags (a t_offset of -1 is shown as null).
- GET /v1/scenes/search — q length 1..200 and limit 1..100 are enforced (400). A query with no known words gives 400. Results are limited with scope_filter, so deleted assets and other organisations are excluded for providers and API keys, and PROGRAM_VIEWER gets only L1. query_tags, model and items match the contract.
- GET /v1/deid-reviews/{review_id}/frame (other parts) — t is required and must be at least 0; nan and inf give 400. A missing or deleted binary, or a time outside the video, gives 404. The assigned reviewer sees ORIGINAL and DEID. An operator needs a grant for another organisation's original, and with one the request is audited as BREAK_GLASS_ACCESS plus DEID_REVIEW_FRAME_VIEWED.
- POST /v1/deid-reviews/{review_id}/decision (other parts) — the body schema (extra forbidden, maxItems 500, minimums) matches. MISSED without missed[] gives 400. Deciding a review twice gives 409 INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION. PASS records PASSED/PSEUDONYMIZED on the derived only, and recheck_review_ids is returned.

**발견 담당의 메모 (발견으로 올리지 않은 것)**

> The probe file is C:/Users/user/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/69809d01a353b176/ef4ed640-70bd-41cd-a22c-ed40e2ba8d1d/scratchpad/night/wt/rv/backend/tests/integration/test_zz_deid_a.py, left in place. Its 8 tests each fail on the current code to show a finding; the parametrized REJECT case runs 3 times. Run them with: bash $N/bin/odrun.sh rv "python -m pytest -q -p no:cacheprovider -s tests/integration/test_zz_deid_a.py".
> 
> Deliberately not reported:
> - The operator-organisation (ORG_OPERATOR) asset exception for break-glass on original frames: it is already an open owner decision (HANDOFF 0장 -1u and section 5).
> - Operator metadata reads of reviews are not audited, and DEID_REVIEW_DECIDED / DEID_REVIEW_FRAME_VIEWED are committed outside the request transaction (no connection=). The earlier sweep listed both as cross-cutting topics it chose not to report.
> - PATCH tags writes no audit_log row (tag edits are not in the security.md must-audit list; asset_tag_edits keeps the history).
> - The vocabulary-limited tag values and the empty-body 400 on PATCH tags are not documented in the contract (low, contract silence).
> - The vocabulary error pointer is always /add, even when the bad value is in remove (nit).
> - t=1e308 gives 404 rather than 400 (404 is documented).
> - deid-quality counts the same derived's detections once per review, including rechecks, so detected is inflated when rechecks exist. The contract does not define the denominator, so this was left out.
> - The text_tags substring matching of the baseline model (for example '비' matching '장비', '새' matching '새벽'). That is ADR-0020 baseline behaviour.
> 
> The download-url route (assets.py:309-312) has the same missing PERMISSION_DENIED audit on a refused break-glass request as finding 1. It belongs to the assets group and should be checked there.
> 
> Reviewers are assigned with no organisation check (_pick_reviewer picks any ACTIVE REVIEWER). In the seed, reviewers are in the operator organisation, so no mismatch is visible in these operations. A product decision may be needed if provider-organisation users can ever hold REVIEWER.

## assets (조회·수정·재처리) — 발견 5건

### S22. [medium] POST /v1/assets/{asset_id}/reprocess — missing-behavior

**Reprocessing an asset that FAILED during integrity verification leaves it stuck in VALIDATING for good**

- **계약:** openapi.yaml:1893 reprocessAsset description: "FAILED 자산은 실패 직전 단계부터 다시 시작한다." (domain.md 5장: FAILED → 실패 직전 단계, PLATFORM_OPERATOR, 재처리)
- **구현:** integrity_verify is registered with _asset_failed (workers/main.py:91). When it runs out of retries after the RECEIVED→VALIDATING commit (for example a storage read error, ADR-0058), mark_failed moves the asset VALIDATING → FAILED with failed_from=VALIDATING (pipeline.py:447-455; PROCESSING includes VALIDATING, states.py:12,30). reprocess then only sets ingest_status back to failed_from (assets.py:212-214) and queues the steps the caller asked for (META/PROXY/THUMBNAIL). It never queues integrity_verify again. media_probe and make_proxy return SKIPPED unless the status is REGISTERED (pipeline.py:179,363), and a SKIPPED result does not continue the chain. So the API answers 202, the asset sits in VALIDATING with no job to move it on, and any later reprocess gets 409 (assets.py:215-216). Deletion during VALIDATING is also refused (ADR-0046). Before the call the asset could still be retried from FAILED; afterwards it can only be repaired by editing the DB. The upload reservation is not released either.
- **근거:** backend/app/api/assets.py:208-231; backend/app/services/pipeline.py:179,363,447-455; backend/app/workers/main.py:84-91; backend/app/domain/states.py:12,30-31
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_asa_b.py::test_b1_reprocess_failed_in_validating_gets_stuck — after exhausted integrity_verify: ('FAILED', failed_from VALIDATING); reprocess: 202 job_ids=[media_probe]; state VALIDATING; after worker drain: media_probe DONE (skipped), state still VALIDATING, no integrity_verify job; second reprocess: 409 CONFLICT 'VALIDATING 상태에서는 재처리할 수 없습니다'
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I read the code and could not refute this. The finder's probe also reproduces it when run again.

Contract: openapi.yaml:1893 says "FAILED 자산은 실패 직전 단계부터 다시 시작한다", and domain.md 5장 has "FAILED → 실패 직전 단계 | PLATFORM_OPERATOR | 재처리".

How the asset ends up in FAILED from VALIDATING:
- workers/main.py:91 registers integrity_verify with _asset_failed as its on_exhausted hook.
- When the retries run out, pipeline.py:447-455 mark_failed stores failed_from=VALIDATING. VALIDATING is in PROCESSING (states.py:12,30), so the move to FAILED is allowed.
- A stream or storage error after the first transaction commits RECEIVED→VALIDATING (ingest.py:641-657) reaches exactly this path once the retries are used up.

What reprocess then does:
- assets.py:212-214 only sets the status back to VALIDATING. It then queues JOB_FOR_STEP of the requested steps; integrity_verify is not one of them.
- media_probe returns SKIPPED unless the asset is REGISTERED (pipeline.py:179). make_proxy returns SKIPPED unless it is REGISTERED/PROXIED/READY_PRIVATE (pipeline.py:363).
- A SKIPPED result does not call next_step (main.py:73-78).

Nothing else picks the asset up again:
- The only other integrity_verify enqueues are the upload complete call (ingest.py:543) and quarantine revalidation (assets.py:166). Revalidation needs QUARANTINED.
- A second reprocess gets 409 (assets.py:215-216).
- Deletion is refused in VALIDATING (retention.py:38 VERIFYING_INGEST_STATES, ADR-0046). From FAILED it was allowed.

So the 202 call turns a recoverable FAILED asset into one that stays in VALIDATING for good.

I ran test_zz_asa_b.py::test_b1 in od-rv-py and it passed with the observed output: 202 with a media_probe job → state VALIDATING (failed_from VALIDATING) → after draining the worker, media_probe is DONE and the state is still VALIDATING → second reprocess 409 "VALIDATING 상태에서는 재처리할 수 없습니다". The probe forces VALIDATING and calls mark_failed directly. That is the same thing _asset_failed does, so the shortcut is fair.

Not already known:
- ADR-0058 and review-2026-11-12 C2 fixed the job-retry "resume" case.
- The case recorded as awaiting a decision is the separate RECEIVED one (no RECEIVED→FAILED transition; README:113, HANDOFF.md:69).
- The case where verification retries are exhausted in VALIDATING and then reprocess is called is not recorded anywhere.

Side and severity:
- fix_impl is correct. The contract and domain.md describe the intended behaviour, and the fix needs no domain.md change because FAILED→VALIDATING is already in the table.
- medium is fair. It needs a verification run that exhausted its retries (a long storage outage) plus an operator reprocess, but the result can only be repaired in the database and the upload reservation stays leaked.
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I reproduced it independently with a real flow rather than the finder's shortcut. The finder's probe set ingest_status=VALIDATING by hand and called mark_failed directly. My probe uploads a real object through moto, breaks storage.iter_object, and runs the real worker until integrity_verify uses up its retries (5/5 → FAILED). Then I restore storage and call reprocess as PLATFORM_OPERATOR 106 with MFA. Observed: after exhaustion the asset is ('FAILED', failed_from 'VALIDATING') with upload reserved_bytes=128 and org reserved_bytes=128. Reprocess returns 202 with job_ids=[media_probe]. After draining the worker the asset is VALIDATING, media_probe is DONE (it returned SKIPPED because the status is not REGISTERED, pipeline.py:179, so next_step never runs, workers/main.py:76), no integrity_verify job exists, and the reservation is still 128. A second reprocess returns 409 CONFLICT 'VALIDATING 상태에서는 재처리할 수 없습니다' (assets.py:215-216). Code path: reprocess only does FAILED→failed_from (assets.py:212-214) and queues JOB_FOR_STEP jobs. integrity_verify is enqueued only at ingest completion (ingest.py:543) and by the QUARANTINED revalidate PATCH (assets.py:163-166). reap_expired_leases only returns RUNNING jobs, and retention refuses to delete RECEIVED/VALIDATING assets (retention.py:38,184), so nothing ever recovers the asset. This is not the already-fixed C2 item in docs/review-2026-11-12/README.md:44. ADR-0058 fixed resume while the job still has attempts left. This finding is about the FAILED-after-exhaustion → reprocess path, which C2 does not mention. Its RECEIVED-side note is about the scanner and an owner decision, a different case. The contract (reprocessAsset: FAILED assets restart from the step before the failure) is consistent with domain.md 5장 FAILED → previous step. The implementation does not restart verification, so fix_impl is right. Medium severity fits: the asset gets stuck, the quota reservation leaks, and an operator action makes it unrecoverable without DB edits. It needs 5 consecutive storage failures first.
- **고칠 쪽:** 구현 — When failed_from is VALIDATING (or RECEIVED), also queue integrity_verify with resume (ADR-0058 already makes a resumed verification safe) and chain the requested steps after it. The other option is to refuse with 409 until verification is restarted, and say so in the contract. Add a regression test that runs mark_failed on a VALIDATING asset, then reprocess, then drains the worker, and expects REGISTERED/READY_PRIVATE.

### S23. [medium] PATCH /v1/assets/{asset_id} — missing-audit

**DATA_ROLE_CHANGED SUCCESS audit is committed even when the PATCH fails and is rolled back**

- **계약:** security.md 감사 로그 (반드시 기록): "data_role 변경". SWEEP rule: an audit must not be written when the request failed or was rolled back. ADR-0053: audits recorded without connection=db.connection() are committed separately from the request transaction.
- **구현:** patch_asset writes c.audit.record("DATA_ROLE_CHANGED", ...) without a connection (assets.py:148-150), so the row commits in its own transaction straight away. The session_id handling afterwards in the same request can still fail: RULE_VIOLATION 422 for a session in another project (assets.py:155-159), 404 for a session that does not exist or is out of scope (assets.py:155), or 409 for a registered asset (assets.py:171). The request transaction then rolls back and data_role stays as it was, but the audit log keeps a SUCCESS DATA_ROLE_CHANGED row with {from,to}. The audit trail then claims a change that never happened.
- **근거:** backend/app/api/assets.py:133-171 (audit at 148-150 without connection=; later raises at 155,157,171); backend/app/services/audit.py:82
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_asa_a.py::test_a2_failed_patch_keeps_data_role_changed_audit — PATCH {data_role:BYPRODUCT, reason, session_id:<other-project session>} -> 422 RULE_VIOLATION, data_role in DB: CORE, new audit rows: [DATA_ROLE_CHANGED SUCCESS {from CORE,to BYPRODUCT}]; same with a nonexistent session -> 404, data_role CORE, DATA_ROLE_CHANGED row written again
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — Reading the current code on origin/main (rv checkout) confirms the finding. In backend/app/api/assets.py:148-150, patch_asset calls c.audit.record("DATA_ROLE_CHANGED", ...) with no connection= argument. In backend/app/services/audit.py:82, AuditLogger.record opens its own self.engine.begin() when connection is None. That means the SUCCESS row with {from,to} commits at once in a separate transaction. The same handler can still raise after that point: load_scoped on body.session_id at assets.py:155 (404 when the session does not exist or is out of scope), RULE_VIOLATION when the session belongs to another project (assets.py:156-159), and CONFLICT for a registered asset (assets.py:171). The request transaction then rolls back and data_role stays unchanged, but the audit row remains. The finder's probe (test_zz_asa_a.py::test_a2) checks this correctly. It uses an ORG_ADMIN user (101) so the role check passes, a real CORE asset and a session in another project, and it reads data_role and the audit rows through fresh connections. The pattern is not covered by any accepted trade-off. ADR-0053 ('결과/감수할 점') and ADR-0055 (line 46) accept separate-connection audits that survive a rare *commit failure*. They do not cover a deterministic validation error raised later in the same handler. The 2026-11-05 security review already fixed this same pattern for the operations-policy PATCH (HANDOFF.md -1u item 3: flush first, audit in the same transaction), which supports fix_impl. security.md:27 lists 'data_role 변경' as a mandatory audit, and SWEEP defines missing-audit to include audits written for failed or rolled-back requests. Severity medium and side fix_impl are right: an auditor would see a role change that never happened. It needs a combined PATCH that fails, so it is not high.
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I reproduced this with my own probe on a third failure path the finder did not use. In backend/app/api/assets.py:148-150 `c.audit.record("DATA_ROLE_CHANGED", ...)` is called without `connection=`. In backend/app/services/audit.py:82 that call takes `self.engine.begin()`, so the row is committed in its own transaction straight away. The session_id block runs later in the same request and can still raise 404 (line 155, load_scoped), 422 RULE_VIOLATION (lines 156-159) or 409 CONFLICT (line 171). When that happens the request transaction rolls back and data_role stays unchanged, but the SUCCESS audit row remains.

My probe used the 409 path, which is independent of the finder's 422 and 404 cases: an ADMIN (101) PATCHes a READY_PRIVATE asset with {data_role: BYPRODUCT, reason, session_id: <another session in the same project>}. Observed: `409 CONFLICT`, data_role in DB `CORE`, audit rows for the asset: `[('DATA_ROLE_CHANGED', 'SUCCESS', '{"to": "BYPRODUCT", "from": "CORE"}')]`. As a control, a PATCH that succeeds writes exactly one such row and changes the role to BYPRODUCT. So the failed request leaves a row that looks exactly like a real change.

I also read the finder's probe critically: it uses ADMIN 101, which has ORG_ADMIN, so the FORBIDDEN branch is not what is being hit. Its 422 and 404 cases are valid, and its assertions read the DB state after the request.

None of the review documents or HANDOFF mention DATA_ROLE_CHANGED. HANDOFF item -1u fixed the same pattern for the operations policy PATCH ("flush 먼저 + 감사를 같은 트랜잭션"), so the project treats this pattern as a bug. That makes fix_impl the right side. Medium severity is right: an auditor would see a false data_role change, but no data is lost or leaked.
- **고칠 쪽:** 구현 — Pass connection=db.connection() to the DATA_ROLE_CHANGED audit so it commits or rolls back with the change, or move the audit after all validation and the final flush. Add a test that sends a failing combined PATCH and expects no DATA_ROLE_CHANGED row.

### S24. [medium] PATCH /v1/assets/{asset_id} — missing-audit

**403 for a data_role change by a non-ORG_ADMIN writes no PERMISSION_DENIED audit**

- **계약:** openapi.yaml:1827 summary: "data_role 변경(ORG_ADMIN, 사유 필수)" and 403 Forbidden. security.md 감사 로그 (반드시 기록): "권한 거부". Table-level denials write PERMISSION_DENIED (deps.py:102). The earlier sweep fixes #15 and #25 added the same audit for 403s raised inside handlers.
- **구현:** asset:write allows ORG_MEMBER, so the role check for data_role is inside the handler: raise ProblemError("FORBIDDEN") (assets.py:134-135) with no audit. An ORG_MEMBER trying to reclassify an asset gets 403 and nothing is logged. A PROGRAM_VIEWER hitting the same route is stopped by require() and gets a PERMISSION_DENIED row.
- **근거:** backend/app/api/assets.py:133-135; backend/app/api/deps.py:97-105; backend/app/domain/permissions.py:69
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_asa_a.py::test_a3_member_data_role_403_has_no_permission_denied_audit — member data_role: 403 FORBIDDEN new audit rows: []; compare viewer PATCH: 403 new audit rows: [PERMISSION_DENIED DENIED {action: asset:write}]
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I could not refute this. The contract, the code and the probe all agree with the finding.

1. Contract: openapi.yaml:1827 summary reads "회차 지정·data_role 변경(ORG_ADMIN, 사유 필수)·위치 공개 정밀도" and lists 403 Forbidden. security.md:27 says "권한 거부" must always be logged.
2. Code: permissions.py:69 gives "asset:write" to every PROVIDER role, so require() lets ORG_MEMBER through. The ORG_ADMIN check happens inside the handler: assets.py:134-135 raises ProblemError("FORBIDDEN") with no c.audit.record call. Only require() (deps.py:101-104) and two handler paths write PERMISSION_DENIED: break_glass.py:84 and context.py:101. Those two are the earlier sweep's fixes #15 and #25 for this same pattern (contract-sweep-findings.md:24, 42, 190, 260), so the project already counts an unaudited 403 inside a handler as a defect.
3. Not already recorded: the earlier sweep never finished the datasets·sharing·deid·assets bundle (contract-sweep-findings.md:297-299, HANDOFF.md:64). Its leftover probe test_probe_ctx2.py has a "member PATCH data_role" case but no finding was written. Nothing in HANDOFF section 5, docs/review-2026-11-12/README.md or the ADRs makes this an intended exception.
4. Probe: I re-ran the finder's probe in od-rv-py and it passed (1 passed), confirming no PERMISSION_DENIED row. It does what it claims.
