The asset starts with a data_role different from BYPRODUCT, so the change branch is reached. User 102 (ORG_MEMBER) passes require() and is stopped inside the handler. The comparison case, user 105 (PROGRAM_VIEWER), is stopped by require() and does get a PERMISSION_DENIED row.

Medium severity is right: an auditor would see the gap, and #15 and #25 were treated the same way. fix_impl is right: the contract text and security.md are clear, and only the code needs to change.
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I reproduced this with my own probe. backend/tests/integration/test_zz_asav2x_a.py::test_v_member_data_role_denied_unaudited matches audit rows by the X-Request-Id the server returns, not by seq ranges.

Observed output:
- Control: ORG_MEMBER 102 PATCHes location_precision on the same ORG_A asset and gets 200. So the member passes require("asset:write") (permissions.py:69, Rule(PROVIDER)) and the tenant scope check. The later 403 therefore comes from the check inside the handler, not from scope or the permission table.
- Member PATCH {data_role: BYPRODUCT, reason} gets 403 FORBIDDEN "data_role 변경은 ORG_ADMIN만 할 수 있습니다". Audit rows for that request_id: [].
- The same request with mfa=True also gets 403 with no rows. Missing MFA is not the cause.
- Viewer 105, same body: 403, with one row ('PERMISSION_DENIED', 'DENIED', usr_...105, target None, {"action": "asset:write", "mfa_required": false}).
- data_role in the DB stays CORE.

Code: assets.py:134-135 raises ProblemError("FORBIDDEN") and records nothing. security.md:27 lists 권한 거부 (permission denial) as an event that must be audited, and openapi summary "data_role 변경(ORG_ADMIN, 사유 필수)" names ORG_ADMIN as the gate. This is the same pattern the earlier sweep fixed as #25 (context.py:101, device registration denied inside the handler) and #15 (break_glass.py:84). I found no record in docs/review-2026-11-05, review-2026-11-12 or HANDOFF that marks this case as known or intended. The finder's probe tests what it claims: same asset, a correct ORG_MEMBER, and a seq-range audit check. Its result agrees with mine. The fix_impl side and medium severity (an auditor would miss the denial) are right.
- **고칠 쪽:** 구현 — Before raising, record PERMISSION_DENIED (result=DENIED, target Asset, details {action: 'asset:data_role', from/to}), following break_glass.py:84 and context.py:101. Add a regression test.

### S25. [medium] PATCH /v1/assets/{asset_id} — missing-audit

**Moving data_role to CORE lowers the sharing_scope of derived assets (and can revoke their grants) with no audit for them**

- **계약:** security.md 감사 로그 (반드시 기록): "공유 등급 변경(사유 포함)", "이용 승인·철회". 13번 요구사항 2.2: on BYPRODUCT→CORE the system lowers sharing_scope to L1 or below and runs the downgrade handling of 요구사항 10. On the share-requests path that handling writes SHARE_SCOPE_REQUESTED with from/to/handling, and the grant revoke endpoint writes GRANT_REVOKED.
- **구현:** For the asset and every descendant above L1, patch_asset calls sharing.downgrade(..., "PROGRAM_REVIEW", "REVOKE_NOW", ...) (assets.py:142-145). downgrade only changes sharing_scope and runs datasets.on_downgrade, which revokes GRANTED grants through datasets.revoke and writes no audit (sharing.py:161-169, datasets.py:425-447). The only audit is one DATA_ROLE_CHANGED row on the original with details {from,to} roles (assets.py:148-150). It does not name the descendants whose scope changed, their old scope, or any grants revoked. No ShareRequest row records the REVOKE_NOW choice either. An auditor looking at the derived asset's history finds nothing, even though it went from SANDBOX_PARTNERS to PROGRAM_REVIEW.
- **근거:** backend/app/api/assets.py:139-150; backend/app/services/sharing.py:161-169; backend/app/services/datasets.py:425-447; compare backend/app/api/sharing.py:57-60 and backend/app/api/datasets.py:316
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_asa_a.py::test_a4_core_downgrade_of_descendant_scope_not_audited — PATCH orig {data_role:CORE, reason} -> 200; deid scope now: PROGRAM_REVIEW (was SANDBOX_PARTNERS); share_requests for deid: 0; new audit rows: [DATA_ROLE_CHANGED on orig only, details {to: CORE, from: BYPRODUCT}] (nothing targets or mentions the deid). Grant revocation without GRANT_REVOKED is shown by code only, not by the probe.
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I could not refute it. The code reading matches the claim and the finder's probe passes when I run it again. (1) The contract side holds. security.md:27 lists "공유 등급 변경(사유 포함)", "data_role 변경" and "이용 승인·철회" as audits that must always be written. 13-sharing-governance requirement 2.2 (requirements.md:38) says a BYPRODUCT→CORE change must run the downgrade handling of requirement 10. Requirement 10.3 (line 112) says that handling records the existing-grant choice. (2) The implementation side holds. assets.py:139-145 calls sharing.downgrade(..., "PROGRAM_REVIEW", "REVOKE_NOW", ...) on the asset and on each descendant above L1. sharing.py:161-169 only sets sharing_scope/updated_at and then calls datasets.on_downgrade. datasets.py:436-447 revokes every GRANTED grant through datasets.revoke (datasets.py:425-433). That writes grant columns and a consumer notice but no audit row. The only audit is DATA_ROLE_CHANGED on the original, with details {from,to} for data_role only (assets.py:148-150). Since requirement 3.6 keeps ORIGINAL assets at L1 or below, the scope lowering in practice only ever hits descendants. That makes the gap total for them: the audit log has nothing that targets or names them, nothing with their old scope, and nothing about REVOKE_NOW or the revoked grants. Compare the share-request path, which writes SHARE_SCOPE_REQUESTED with from/to/existing_grant_handling (api/sharing.py:57-60), and manual revoke, which writes GRANT_REVOKED (api/datasets.py:316). (3) It is not already known. I searched docs/review-2026-11-05, docs/review-2026-11-12, HANDOFF.md and docs/adr for data_role, downgrade (하향) and GRANT_REVOKED and found nothing about this. The 11-05 sweep never checked this bundle (HANDOFF.md:64). (4) The scenario is reachable: a DEID derived from a BYPRODUCT original may legitimately be at L2 or above. I re-ran backend/tests/integration/test_zz_asa_a.py::test_a4_core_downgrade_of_descendant_scope_not_audited in od-rv-py and it passed: 1 passed. Its assertions are that the PATCH returns 200, the deid scope becomes PROGRAM_REVIEW, and no new audit row targets or mentions the deid. The probe tests what it claims: it uses the ORG_ADMIN user and a reason, and sets the deid scope directly to stand in for the earlier share flow. Medium severity is right: an auditor would hit this as a missing audit, with no data loss or leak. fix_impl is right: the contract text (security.md, requirement 10.3) is clear, and the PATCH contract only says that sharing_scope cannot be set directly.
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — My own probe confirms the finding and goes one step further than the finder's probe: the grant revocation also leaves no audit, and I observed it directly instead of inferring it from code. Setup: a BYPRODUCT original with a DEID child at SANDBOX_PARTNERS (set by raw SQL, the same way the finder did, because the SharingBypass guard blocks setting it through the ORM). The DEID is in a dataset version that has a GRANTED grant for ORG_CONSUMER. ORG_ADMIN (101, mfa=True) sends PATCH orig {data_role:CORE, reason} and gets 200. Afterwards the DEID's scope is PROGRAM_REVIEW and the grant is REVOKED (revoke_reason '제공기관 공유 등급 하향: data_role CORE 변경: reclassify core'). The request wrote exactly one audit row: ('DATA_ROLE_CHANGED', 'SUCCESS', 'Asset', <orig id>, '{"to": "CORE", "from": "BYPRODUCT"}'). No row targets or mentions the DEID or the grant, and there is no GRANT_REVOKED row. Code path: backend/app/api/assets.py:139-150 calls sharing.downgrade (services/sharing.py:161-169), then datasets.on_downgrade (datasets.py:432-447), then datasets.revoke (datasets.py:421-430). None of these write audit. The two manual paths do: api/sharing.py:57-60 writes SHARE_SCOPE_REQUESTED with from/to/existing_grant_handling, and api/datasets.py:316-317 writes GRANT_REVOKED. Rules broken: security.md 감사 로그 (반드시 기록) lists "공유 등급 변경(사유 포함)" and "이용 승인·철회", and domain.md:83 says "상향·하향 모두 감사 로그에 사유와 함께 남긴다". I found nothing in docs/review-2026-11-05, docs/review-2026-11-12, HANDOFF or the ADRs that records this as known or decided. The finder's probe was sound (right role, right lineage, state set directly), but it could not show the grant side. Severity medium (a missing audit an auditor would hit) and side fix_impl are both correct: the contract and steering text are clear, and the behaviour falls short of them.
- **고칠 쪽:** 구현 — For each asset whose scope is lowered, record a sharing-scope audit in the request transaction: target = that asset, details {from, to: PROGRAM_REVIEW, existing_grant_handling: REVOKE_NOW, cause: DATA_ROLE_CHANGED, origin asset}. Record GRANT_REVOKED for each revoked grant. At minimum, list the affected asset IDs and grant IDs in the DATA_ROLE_CHANGED details. Name the action in an ADR.

### S26. [low] PATCH /v1/assets/{asset_id} — wrong-status

**Assigning a session from another project returns 422 RULE_VIOLATION, which the contract does not list**

- **계약:** openapi.yaml:1835-1846 patchAsset responses: 200, 400, 401, 403, 404, 409, 429 (no 422).
- **구현:** If the target session belongs to a different project than the asset, the handler raises ProblemError("RULE_VIOLATION", unmet_conditions=[session.project_id=asset.project_id]), which is HTTP 422 (assets.py:156-159, errors.py:44). A client generated from the contract has no case for it.
- **근거:** backend/app/api/assets.py:154-159; backend/app/errors.py:44; contracts/openapi.yaml:1835-1846
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_asa_a.py::test_a1_patch_session_other_project_is_undocumented_422 — PATCH other-project session: 422 RULE_VIOLATION documented: False
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I could not refute this finding. In the current code, backend/app/api/assets.py:154-159 loads the target session with load_scoped. If session.project_id != a.project_id, it raises ProblemError("RULE_VIOLATION", unmet_conditions=[{condition: "session.project_id=asset.project_id", ...}]). errors.py STATUS maps RULE_VIOLATION to 422. In contracts/openapi.yaml:1835-1846, patchAsset lists only 200/400/401/403/404/409/429. The AssetPatch schema (openapi.yaml:6353-6367) and the operation description say nothing about the session having to be in the asset's project. A shared `Unprocessable` response already exists (openapi.yaml:4772) and other operations use it. api-conventions.md:45 defines RULE_VIOLATION=422 as a general code, but it does not make 422 implicit on every operation. Other operations list it explicitly. I checked docs/review-2026-11-05/contract-sweep-findings.md, HANDOFF and .kiro for "patchAsset" and "session.project_id", and this case is not recorded as known or decided. Precedent: #22, #23 and #24 in the earlier sweep were the same kind of undocumented 422 RULE_VIOLATION, and all were resolved as fix_contract. The rule is a sensible integrity check, so fix_contract is the right side. Low severity is right: it is an edge case with a clear problem body. I ran the finder's probe on the rv checkout: test_a1_patch_session_other_project_is_undocumented_422 passed, which confirms the 422 and that the contract does not list it.
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I reproduced this on my own, with a different role, asset state and fixture from the finder's probe. The finder used MEMBER 102 on a READY_PRIVATE asset that already had a session. I used ORG_ADMIN 101 with MFA on a RECEIVED asset with session_id=None, which is the normal case where assigning a session is allowed. I added a second ACTIVE project and a session in it, both in ORG_A.

Result of PATCH /v1/assets/{id} {session_id: <other-project session>}: 422 application/problem+json, code RULE_VIOLATION, unmet_conditions=[{condition: session.project_id=asset.project_id, actual: prj_...297}]. The asset's session_id in the database stayed NULL. The control request with the same-project session SES_A returned 200 and set the session. The responses the contract lists for patchAsset are ['200','400','401','403','404','409','429'], so 422 is missing.

Code: backend/app/api/assets.py:154-159 raises ProblemError("RULE_VIOLATION"), and backend/app/errors.py:44 maps RULE_VIOLATION to 422. Contract: contracts/openapi.yaml:1835-1846 has no 422 entry.

Not recorded anywhere yet: a search of docs/, HANDOFF.md and .kiro for "다른 실증사업", "session.project_id" and "patchAsset" found nothing.

The rule itself is sound: an asset and its session must be in the same project. So the contract should change, not the code (fix_contract).

Severity low is right. The API has no way to create a project; projects only come from seed.py or direct DB inserts. So a client only hits this when an organisation has several projects provisioned by operators.
- **고칠 쪽:** 계약 문구 — Add '422': {$ref: '#/components/responses/RuleViolation'} (or the shared UnprocessableEntity response) to patchAsset, with a note that the session must belong to the asset's project, as was done for #22-#24 in the earlier sweep. Keep the implementation.

**일치를 확인한 작업**

- GET /v1/assets — asset:read (PROVIDER, PROGRAM_VIEWER, PLATFORM_OPERATOR, API key with asset:read; device tokens refused); Tenant_Guard via scoped_select (viewer: assigned projects, non-PRIVATE only; deleted assets hidden); query values are checked against the contract (bad enum, session_id pattern, non-Z or offset datetimes, bad metric_code all give 400 VALIDATION_ERROR); limit 1..500; cursor errors give 400; no total count; out-of-scope parent IDs removed from provenance
- GET /v1/assets/{asset_id} — load_scoped gives 404 plus CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT; ASSET_VIEWED audit as security.md requires; EXACT_RESTRICTED location generalized for non-owners; env_context reduced to public_env for other orgs
- GET /v1/assets/{asset_id}/receipt — asset:receipt (PROVIDER plus device/API key with upload:write, device limited to mapped sources by the device filter); 404 until an integrity log entry exists (matches the documented 404 and review F3); response fields match the Receipt schema
- GET /v1/assets/{asset_id}/lineage — asset:read; 404 plus cross-org audit; ancestors and descendants are recursive (depth 20) and filtered by scope_filter so out-of-scope assets are hidden; relation values written by the code (PROXY, THUMBNAIL, CLIP, DEID) are all in the LineageEdge enum (SAMPLE_FRAME→THUMBNAIL is a known HANDOFF 5 item)
- POST /v1/assets/{asset_id}/reprocess (apart from the finding above) — PLATFORM_OPERATOR plus MFA (403 or MFA_REQUIRED otherwise); extra fields, missing reason, empty or duplicate steps give 400; SENSOR_PARSE must be requested alone; non-video or binary-less assets give 409; non-processable states give 409; 202 body matches the contract; REPROCESS_REQUESTED audit with reason and steps; last_reprocessed_at set on the asset and its descendants

**발견 담당의 메모 (발견으로 올리지 않은 것)**

> Probe files (left in place): C:/Users/user/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/69809d01a353b176/ef4ed640-70bd-41cd-a22c-ed40e2ba8d1d/scratchpad/night/wt/rv/backend/tests/integration/test_zz_asa_a.py and .../test_zz_asa_b.py. All 7 tests pass against origin/main (b6bc039); each one asserts the current mismatching behaviour.
> 
> Not reported (minor, ambiguous, or the same kind of thing the earlier sweep left out):
> (1) PATCH accepts {"data_role": <current value>} with no reason, even from ORG_MEMBER, and returns 200. The contract's dependentRequired data_role:[reason] would give 400. PATCH also accepts {"session_id": null} with 200 and no change. These are nothing-to-change cases, like the "nulls accepted" item the earlier sweep left out. Seen in test_a6.
> (2) GET /v1/assets ignores env_min/env_max when env_metric is missing (200, no filtering). The contract only says the bounds are used together with env_metric.
> (3) reprocess accepts variant CLIP although the description says 원본 자산에만. CLIP is the original in C2_EVENT_CLIPS mode, so this looks intended. The contract text could say "원 영상(ORIGINAL·CLIP)". SENSOR_PARSE does not check variant, but nothing creates SENSOR_EXPORT assets today.
> (4) reprocess returns job_ids with only the first job of the chain. The contract does not say one ID per step.
> (5) For a FAILED asset with failed_from=REGISTERED, reprocess restores the state but runs only the requested steps, not the step that failed. Whether "실패 직전 단계부터 다시 시작" means the state or the step is ambiguous. The VALIDATING case is reported because it ends stuck.
> (6) Operator metadata reads write no audit beyond ASSET_VIEWED. The earlier sweep already recorded this as cross-cutting.
> 
> The reprocess finding is related to review 2026-11-12 C2 / ADR-0058 but is a different path. C2 covers a verification interrupted while still VALIDATING, which a re-run of integrity_verify now resumes, and the RECEIVED-stuck case, which waits for a person's decision. This finding is VALIDATING→FAILED→reprocess, which never queues integrity_verify again.

## assets (다운로드·클립·삭제) — 발견 3건

### S27. [medium] GET /v1/deletion-certificates/{certificate_id} — missing-audit

**Cross-org certificate lookup returns 404 but writes no CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT audit**

- **계약:** responses.404 -> components/responses/NotFound: '없음 또는 다른 기관 리소스 (둘을 구분하지 않는다)' (contracts/openapi.yaml:3672, 4740-4741). security.md:10 says a request for another org's resource gets 404 and an audit record, and security.md:27 lists 교차 기관 접근 시도 as an audit that must be written. Project rule: 404 plus CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT.
- **구현:** get_certificate loads the row with a plain db.get and raises ProblemError('NOT_FOUND') when cert.organization_id != p.organization_id and the caller is not an operator. It does not go through load_scoped or Tenant_Guard, so no audit row is written. If an ORG_ADMIN of org A probes org B's certificate IDs, nothing is recorded. The same caller asking for an org-B asset through load_scoped gets a CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT row.
- **근거:** backend/app/api/lifecycle.py:180-186 (line 184-185: `cert = db.get(DeletionCertificate, certificate_id)` / `if cert is None or (cert.organization_id != p.organization_id and not p.has_role("PLATFORM_OPERATOR")): raise ProblemError("NOT_FOUND")`); compare backend/app/api/deps.py:105-116 (load_scoped audits cross_org); .kiro/steering/security.md:10,27
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_asb_a.py::test_cert_cross_org_get_is_not_audited — OBS own-org GET: 200 OK | OBS cross-org GET cert: 404 NOT_FOUND new audit: [] | OBS cross-org GET asset: 404 new audit: [('CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT', 'DENIED', 'Asset', 'ast_...')]
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I could not refute it; the finding holds on origin/main (rv HEAD b6bc039 = origin/main).

What the contract and rules say:
- contracts/openapi.yaml:3652-3673 gives GET /v1/deletion-certificates/{certificate_id} a 404 that references NotFound, described as "없음 또는 다른 기관 리소스 (둘을 구분하지 않는다)" (openapi.yaml:4740-4741).
- .kiro/steering/security.md:10 says another org's resource gets 404 and is written to the audit log. security.md:27 lists 교차 기관 접근 시도 as a mandatory audit.
- The same kind of gap was treated as fix_impl in the earlier sweep (contract-sweep-findings.md:89-92, 196-206).

What the code does:
- backend/app/api/lifecycle.py:180-186 loads the row with a plain `db.get(DeletionCertificate, certificate_id)`. If the org does not match and the caller is not an operator, it raises `ProblemError("NOT_FOUND")`.
- It never goes through load_scoped (deps.py:111-123), which is the only place that writes CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT. The handler also has no Request or Container dependency, so it cannot write an audit row.
- Permission is ORG_ADMIN and PLATFORM_OPERATOR (permissions.py:90), so an ORG_ADMIN of another org reaches this branch.

Is it already known or decided? No.
- Nothing in HANDOFF.md, docs/review-2026-11-05/README.md, docs/review-2026-11-12/README.md, contract-sweep-findings.md or the ADRs records this as fixed, known or waiting for a decision.
- The only earlier trace is an unreported probe, docs/review-2026-11-05/repro/contract-sweep/datasets-sharing-deid-assets/test_probe_ctx.py::test_p2_deletion_certificate_cross_org_audit. It belongs to the bundle that contract-sweep-findings.md:297-300 says was never checked.
- backend/tests/acceptance/test_t01_tenant_isolation.py CROSS_ORG_CASES has no deletion-certificate case, so T01 does not cover it either.

Is the finder's probe sound? Yes.
- ADMIN_A is user 101, an ORG_ADMIN of ORG_A, so the permission check passes and the 404 comes from the org check, not from a 403 or a missing-MFA path.
- The certificate is created by ORG_B's admin with MFA (delete returns 200).
- I re-ran it: own-org GET 200 and matches the contract; cross-org GET cert 404 NOT_FOUND with new audit []; cross-org GET asset 404 with one CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT row.

Severity medium and side fix_impl are correct. The response does not change, so there is no contract change.
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — My own probe reproduces the finding. In it, ORG_B admin 103 deletes an ORG_B asset and gets a certificate back. ORG_A admin 101, who holds deletion_certificate:read (permissions.py:90), then GETs that certificate with and without MFA. Both times the response is 404 NOT_FOUND and no audit_log rows are added. In the same run, 101 GETs the deleted ORG_B asset itself and gets 404 plus one row ('CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT','DENIED','org_...02','Asset',id), written by load_scoped (deps.py:111-122). So the gap is in the handler and not an artefact of the fixture or the role. The owner (103) and the operator (106) get 200; a nonexistent id gets 404 with no audit, which is correct. Code: lifecycle.py:180-186 uses a plain db.get plus a manual org compare, with no audit call and no Container/Request dependency. The contract's 404 (openapi.yaml ~3672, NotFound 'not found or another org's resource') and security.md:10,27 (another org's resource → 404 and an audit record; cross-org access attempts must be audited) support fix_impl. The gap is not recorded as known: contract-sweep-findings.md lists this bundle under '점검하지 못한 묶음'. The only earlier trace is an unreported repro file, docs/review-2026-11-05/repro/contract-sweep/datasets-sharing-deid-assets/test_probe_ctx.py::test_p2_deletion_certificate_cross_org_audit, and no ADR or HANDOFF item covers it. The finder's probe is sound: right roles, and it compares max(seq) before and after each request. Severity medium is right: an auditor would see a missing audit on a security-relevant read. Certificate ids are ULID-based, so enumerating them is impractical, but the audit requirement still applies.
- **고칠 쪽:** 구현 — In get_certificate, when the certificate exists but belongs to another org and the caller is not PLATFORM_OPERATOR, call c.audit.record('CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT', result='DENIED', principal=p, organization_id=cert.organization_id, target_type='DeletionCertificate', target_id=certificate_id, source_ip=client_ip(request), details={'path': request.url.path}) before raising NOT_FOUND. This needs request and c (Container) to be added as dependencies. An alternative is to route the lookup through load_scoped/get_scoped (DeletionCertificate has organization_id, and operators get scope true for metadata). The regression test asserts 404 plus exactly one CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT row with target_type DeletionCertificate. The response does not change, so there is no contract change.

### S28. [medium] POST /v1/assets/{asset_id}/download-url — missing-audit

**Operator download-url refused for lack of a valid break-glass grant (403) leaves no audit row**

- **계약:** createDownloadUrl description (contracts/openapi.yaml:3434-3437): '운영자는 유효한 긴급 열람 승인(대상 자산 또는 그 회차)이 있을 때만 받고, 발급은 BREAK_GLASS_ACCESS로 따로 감사한다 (02번 요구사항 8). 승인이 없으면 403.' .kiro/steering/security.md:27 lists the audits that must be written, including 권한 거부 and 긴급 열람.
- **구현:** The permission table grants asset:download to PLATFORM_OPERATOR, so require() passes and writes no audit. The handler then raises ProblemError('FORBIDDEN') itself when active_grant_for returns None. No PERMISSION_DENIED row and no break-glass row is written. This covers four cases: no grant at all, a grant still REQUESTED, an expired grant, and the approving operator (not the requester) trying to use the grant. So an operator's attempt to fetch another org's original binary without valid break-glass is invisible in the audit log. By contrast, a PROGRAM_VIEWER refused at table level gets PERMISSION_DENIED. This is the same pattern as earlier sweep findings #15 and #25.
- **근거:** backend/app/api/assets.py:304-310 (raise FORBIDDEN without c.audit.record); backend/app/domain/permissions.py:72 (asset:download includes PLATFORM_OPERATOR); backend/app/api/deps.py:76-86 (only table-level denials are audited); tests/integration/test_break_glass.py:41,55,71,89 assert these 403s but never check audit
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_asb_a.py::test_download_url_operator_without_grant_not_audited — OBS download-url operator(no grant): 403 FORBIDDEN new audit: [] | OBS download-url viewer: 403 FORBIDDEN new audit: [('PERMISSION_DENIED', 'DENIED', None, None)]
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I could not refute it. The contract (contracts/openapi.yaml:3434-3437) says "승인이 없으면 403". security.md:27 makes 권한 거부 and 긴급 열람 audits mandatory ("반드시 기록").

Current code on origin/main:
- permissions.py:72 gives asset:download to PLATFORM_OPERATOR, so require() (deps.py ~96-104) passes and writes no audit.
- assets.py:304-310 then raises ProblemError('FORBIDDEN') with no c.audit.record when active_grant_for returns None. The else branch at :311 does the same.
- active_grant_for (services/break_glass.py:105-113) requires requested_by == operator, status ACTIVE, the same org and expires_at > now. So the REQUESTED-grant, expired-grant and approver cases all reach the same unaudited 403.

I re-ran the finder's probe myself. Output: "OBS download-url operator(no grant): 403 FORBIDDEN new audit: []" against "viewer: 403 FORBIDDEN new audit: [('PERMISSION_DENIED','DENIED',None,None)]". The probe is sound: the operator is cross-org to the ORG_A asset, MFA is on, and the binary and storage_key are set.

This is not already known or decided:
- The open owner items in HANDOFF section 5 (lines 273-274) are about operator-org-owned assets and operator raw observation reads, not about denial audits.
- The earlier sweep's identical pattern was fixed in the same way: #15 at break_glass.py:84, and #25.

The status stays the same, so the contract does not change and fix_impl is the right side. Medium severity fits: an audit gap an auditor would hit, here on an attempt to get another org's original binary.
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I reproduced this with my own probe. In ctx, a PLATFORM_OPERATOR (106, ORG_OPERATOR) calls POST /v1/assets/{id}/download-url on an ORG_A asset, and I recorded the audit_log rows written after each call (seq > the max before it). Four cases each return 403 FORBIDDEN and write no new audit rows: (1) no grant, (2) a grant the operator requested that is still REQUESTED, (3) the approving operator 107 trying to use 106's ACTIVE grant, (4) the requester after expires_at was moved into the past. For comparison, PROGRAM_VIEWER 105 gets 403 plus one ('PERMISSION_DENIED','DENIED') row, because the denial comes from require() at backend/app/api/deps.py:99-105. The cause is in the code: backend/app/api/assets.py:304-310. The operator passes require('asset:download'), because the permission table grants it, and the handler then raises ProblemError('FORBIDDEN') when active_grant_for() returns None, without calling c.audit.record. load_scoped also writes nothing, because the operator is allowed to see the metadata. The contract (openapi.yaml createDownloadUrl: '승인이 없으면 403') and security.md:27 ('감사 로그 (반드시 기록): ... 권한 거부 ... 긴급 열람') support fix_impl. ADR-0029 decision 3 lists only request, approve, deny, revoke and expiry audits, and does not say an operator's attempt without a grant should go unaudited. The open items in HANDOFF section 5 (rows 273-274: whether operator-org assets need break-glass, and operators reading raw observations) are a different question. The earlier sweep fixed the same pattern as #15 and #25. Severity medium is right: an auditor cannot see an operator trying to get another organisation's original binary without approval. One side note, unrelated to this finding: in my probe the own-org member call returned 500. That is an artifact of the synthetic asset (_asset with a fake storage_key in FakeStorage), and the finding does not depend on it.
- **고칠 쪽:** 구현 — Before raising FORBIDDEN in both branches of create_download_url, record c.audit.record('PERMISSION_DENIED', result='DENIED', principal=p, organization_id=a.organization_id, target_type='Asset', target_id=a.asset_id, source_ip=client_ip(request), details={'action': 'asset:download', 'reason': 'no_active_break_glass'}). The first branch is the operator without a grant; the second is unreachable today but cheap to cover. Use an existing action code; do not invent a new one in domain.md. The regression test covers an operator with no grant, a REQUESTED grant, an expired grant and the approver: each should give 403 plus one PERMISSION_DENIED row. The status is unchanged, so there is no contract change.

### S29. [low] GET /v1/assets/{asset_id}/clips — data-semantics

**Clip job list silently truncated to the 100 most recent jobs, with no cursor or indicator**

- **계약:** listAssetClips (contracts/openapi.yaml:3461-3479): summary '이 영상에서 요청한 구간 클립 작업과 결과 (… 최근 요청부터)', and the 200 body is {items: ClipJob[]}, described as '클립 작업 목록'. No limit, cursor, next_cursor or truncation flag is defined.
- **구현:** list_clips takes its jobs from PgQueue.jobs_for_asset, whose default limit=100 becomes 'ORDER BY created_at DESC, job_id DESC LIMIT :n'. A video with more than 100 clip jobs returns only the newest 100. Older jobs and their clip_asset_id values cannot be reached, and nothing in the response says the list was cut. This is the same class as earlier finding #12 (overlapping-assets limit 500), which was fixed with cursor pagination (ADR-0052).
- **근거:** backend/app/services/clips.py:116-141 (line 132: `for j in c.queue.jobs_for_asset(JOB_TYPE, a.asset_id):`); backend/app/adapters/pg_queue.py:157-165 (limit: int = 100, LIMIT :n); backend/app/api/assets.py:252-260
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_asb_a.py::test_clip_list_deleted_result_and_cap — OBS jobs in DB: 105 items returned: 100 keys: ['items']
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — The finding holds up. I checked the contract and the current code in the rv checkout. Contract (contracts/openapi.yaml:3458-3484): listAssetClips has no query parameters except AssetId. Its 200 body is only {items: ClipJob[]} ('클립 작업 목록'). It has no limit, next_cursor or truncation flag, and the summary only promises the order ('최근 요청부터'). Implementation: backend/app/api/assets.py:252-260 returns {"items": clips.list_clips(c, db, a)}. backend/app/services/clips.py:123 calls c.queue.jobs_for_asset(JOB_TYPE, a.asset_id) with no limit. The port (backend/app/ports/queue.py:61) and the adapter (backend/app/adapters/pg_queue.py:157-165) both default to limit=100, which becomes 'ORDER BY created_at DESC, job_id DESC LIMIT :n'. So a video with more than 100 clip jobs silently shows only the newest 100. The finder's probe (test_zz_asb_a.py::test_clip_list_deleted_result_and_cap) tests this correctly: it inserts 105 clip_export rows for one asset with the right org and role, then counts the returned items. Nothing records this as known or decided. ADR-0033 (docs/adr/0033-clip-export.md:13,30) only says 'GET → 최근 요청부터' and 'QueuePort.jobs_for_asset로 jobs 표를 읽는다'. docs/review-2026-11-12/README.md section B says clip permissions, quota and caps are fine, and the cap it means is CLIP_MAX_PENDING_PER_ORG, not the list size. HANDOFF -1h, -1z and -1at do not mention it. It is the same kind of problem ADR-0052 fixed for overlapping-assets (the 500 cap). One part of the finding goes too far: live clip result assets are not truly unreachable, because they are still child assets in /v1/assets/{asset_id}/lineage (contracts/openapi.yaml:1867) and the asset list. What really becomes unreachable is the older job records themselves: status, error_code for FAILED jobs, and requested_at. Severity low is right (an edge case: more than 100 clip requests on one video, with no data loss). Side fix_contract is reasonable: the contract says nothing about a cap or paging, and the fix has to start in the contract either way, by adding pagination or by documenting the cap.
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — The finding holds. My own probe sent every job through the real POST /v1/assets/{id}/clips as MEMBER_A (102 requests, all 202) and set each job to DONE right after, so the per-org pending cap of 3 never blocked anything. Then GET /v1/assets/{id}/clips returned status 200 with 100 items, and the body's only key was ['items']. The two oldest jobs were missing and the newest was present. ?limit=200 is ignored and still gives 100. Probe output: "OBS status: 200 requested: 102 returned: 100 keys: ['items']", "OBS oldest two present: False False newest present: True", "OBS query params honoured? limit=200 -> 100". Code: backend/app/services/clips.py:132 calls c.queue.jobs_for_asset(JOB_TYPE, a.asset_id) with no limit. backend/app/adapters/pg_queue.py:157-165 has default limit=100 and `ORDER BY created_at DESC, job_id DESC LIMIT :n`. backend/app/api/assets.py:252-260 returns {"items": ...} and accepts no limit or cursor. The contract (contracts/openapi.yaml:3458-3479) defines no parameters except AssetId and only {items}. No code path deletes old jobs (the only `DELETE FROM jobs` statements are in migrations and remove QUEUED/RUNNING rows), so DONE and FAILED jobs pile up and the cap is reachable in normal use. The finder's probe is fair: it inserts jobs straight into the queue, but going through the API gives the same result. This is not recorded as known or decided in review-2026-11-05, review-2026-11-12/README.md (C7 and C12 cover other clip problems) or HANDOFF. Separately, .kiro/steering/api-conventions.md:53 says lists use cursor pagination `?limit=&cursor=` returning `{items, next_cursor}`, default 50, max 500. This endpoint follows that rule in neither the contract nor the code.
- **고칠 쪽:** 계약 문구 — Change the contract first. Either add limit/cursor query parameters and next_cursor to listAssetClips and implement keyset pagination on (created_at DESC, job_id DESC), as ADR-0052 did for overlapping-assets, or at least document the 100-item cap. Then pass the limit and cursor through jobs_for_asset. Report contract_changed: true.

**일치를 확인한 작업**

- POST /v1/assets/{asset_id}/download-url: role table excludes PROGRAM_VIEWER, REVIEWER, CONSUMER and machine principals (403 + PERMISSION_DENIED). Cross-org provider gets 404 + CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT via load_scoped. Quarantined assets and assets without a binary (UPLOADING/RECEIVED/VALIDATING) get 409. The operator path needs an ACTIVE, unexpired grant requested by the same operator, on the asset or its session. Issuing writes DOWNLOAD_URL_ISSUED, plus BREAK_GLASS_ACCESS when a grant was used; neither audit carries the URL. The 200 shape matches the contract (tests/integration/test_break_glass.py:69). Only the missing denial audit is a finding.
- GET /v1/assets/{asset_id}/clips: allowed to own-org ORG_ADMIN/ORG_MEMBER only. Cross-org gets 404 + audit. Ordering is newest first. DONE jobs carry clip_asset_id and FAILED jobs carry error_code. Response matches the ClipJob schema (probe contract check OK). Only the 100-job truncation is a finding.
- POST /v1/assets/{asset_id}/clips: validation per ADR-0063. Source checks give 409 (no binary, wrong variant or status, unknown duration). Out-of-range or overlong ranges give 400. An active same-range job is returned without new checks, job or audit. QUOTA_EXCEEDED 409 carries quota_bytes/usage_bytes from Quota.require_room. RATE_LIMITED 429 carries Retry-After. The job and the CLIP_EXPORT_REQUESTED audit go into the request transaction (connection=db.connection()). Idempotency replays through run_idempotent, and a key outside 8-128 chars gets 400 (contract_query). Second same-range request after DONE gave 202 with contract OK.
- POST /v1/assets/{asset_id}/legal-hold: PLATFORM_OPERATOR + MFA (no MFA gives 403 MFA_REQUIRED). Reason is required on both set and release. Row lock uses the same order as deletion. A deleted asset gives 404. LEGAL_HOLD_SET/RELEASED audits carry the reason. A NUL in the reason gives 400. The 200 shape matches the contract.
- POST /v1/assets/{asset_id}/deletion: ORG_ADMIN + MFA, own org via load_scoped. Legal hold gives 409 with affected=[LEGAL_HOLD]. A GRANTED dataset gives 409 whatever confirm_affected says. An approved citation gives 409 with the affected list, and confirm_affected=true proceeds. RECEIVED/VALIDATING or a contended upload gives 409. Storage failure gives 503 with Retry-After and rolls back. C0 assets list no objects. A repeat delete gives 404. A NUL in the reason gives 400. The certificate shape matches the contract, and the ASSET_DELETED audit is written.
- GET /v1/deletion-certificates/{certificate_id}: the role table (ORG_ADMIN, PLATFORM_OPERATOR), the own-org 200 shape (contract OK) and operator visibility are consistent. Only the missing cross-org audit is a finding.

**발견 담당의 메모 (발견으로 올리지 않은 것)**

> The probe file is backend/tests/integration/test_zz_asb_a.py in the rv checkout (C:/Users/user/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/69809d01a353b176/ef4ed640-70bd-41cd-a22c-ed40e2ba8d1d/scratchpad/night/wt/rv). Its four tests print OBS lines and pass. Run: bash $N/bin/odrun.sh rv "python -m pytest -q -p no:cacheprovider -s tests/integration/test_zz_asb_a.py".
> 
> Observations that are not findings:
> (1) In the ctx fixture, a member's download-url returns 500. FakeStorage has no presign_get, so this is a fixture artifact, not a product bug. The happy path is covered with a real S3 in tests/integration/test_break_glass.py:68-70 (ictx).
> (2) GET clips edge case. Job 1 finished (DONE) and its clip X was later deleted. A new request for the same range made job 2, which produced clip Y. The listing then shows job 1 with clip_asset_id=Y, because list_clips falls back to matching by clip_range_s (clips.py:136-139). The contract says '결과 자산이 그 뒤 삭제됐으면 clip_asset_id가 없다'. Reproduced in test_clip_list_deleted_result_and_cap ('job1 (result X deleted) clip_asset_id: <Y>'). I left it out as a finding: Y is a live clip of exactly the same source and range, the fallback is intentional (create_derived returns an existing identical result), and the client sees no harm. A fixer could restrict the range fallback to jobs that finished after that clip asset was created.
> (3) Success audits are written outside the request transaction: DOWNLOAD_URL_ISSUED, LEGAL_HOLD_*, ASSET_DELETED. Nothing passes connection=db.connection(). This belongs to the general audit-atomicity topic, which the earlier sweep deliberately left out, so I did not report it.
> (4) ClipRequest.reason accepts an explicit null (str | None), which is the minor 'null for non-nullable' class the earlier sweep left out. The _own_binary FORBIDDEN in the clips handlers can never be reached, because asset:clip is provider-only and providers are org-scoped.
> (5) Already known or awaiting decision, so not re-reported: an operator downloading assets owned by the operator org without break-glass (HANDOFF 5), two-phase outbox deletion (review-2026-11-12 C1), and clip worker concurrency (C7).

## reports (지표·양식·생성) — 발견 4건

### S30. [medium] POST /v1/reports — data-semantics

**PERIOD report OBSERVATION_STAT counts observations from every project of the organisation, and viewers of the report see the other project's device IDs**

- **계약:** contracts/openapi.yaml:715-718 createReport: '보고서 초안 생성 (회차 또는 사업·기간). 지표를 계산해 근거와 함께 저장한다'. The request takes project_id+from+to for a period report, and the Report carries that project_id (openapi.yaml:5771-5778). listReports (openapi.yaml:691) and domain.md 2장 (line 39) let PROGRAM_VIEWER see only '배정 사업의 ... 제출(SUBMITTED)된 보고서·지표'. Spec 10 req 3.2 says the metric stores its source observations.
- **구현:** For a PERIOD scope, metrics._obs_where keeps only organization_id and the observed_at window, with no project condition. Event metrics filter Event.project_id (metrics.py:81) and asset metrics filter Asset.project_id (metrics.py:95), but observations do not. A period report for project A therefore averages in observations from devices of project B in the same organisation, and lists those devices in sources.observation_ranges. After approve+submit, a PROGRAM_VIEWER assigned only to project A sees project B's device IDs and data in the report (reports.metric_view filters only asset_ids, not observation_ranges).
- **근거:** backend/app/services/metrics.py:111-120 (lines 118-119: period branch has no project filter); compare metrics.py:81 and 95-96; backend/app/services/reports.py:53-55, 167-169; backend/app/api/reports.py:391-397
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_rpa_a.py::test_rpa_period_report_mixes_other_project_observations — DEV_A_SENSOR in prj_..201 (value 10.0) and dev_..399 in prj_..299 of the same org (value 30.0). The PERIOD report for prj_..201 gives value 20.0, input 2, observation_ranges=[dev_..303, dev_..399]. After approve 200 and submit 200, viewer 105 in GET /v1/reports sees ranges ['dev_00000000000000000000000303','dev_00000000000000000000000399'].
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I could not refute it. Reading the contract and the current code confirms the finding, and the finder's probe, re-run in od-rv-py, gives the same output.

Contract: createReport's summary is '보고서 초안 생성 (회차 또는 사업·기간)' (contracts/openapi.yaml:717), so a PERIOD report is scoped to one project plus a period. listReports (openapi.yaml:691) limits a PROGRAM_VIEWER to submitted reports of assigned projects. The events and assets metrics already follow this: Event.project_id at metrics.py:81 and :105, Asset.project_id at metrics.py:95.

Code: the observations table has no project_id column (observations.py:39-47). metrics._obs_where, period branch (metrics.py:118-119), keeps only organization_id and the observed_at window, so OBSERVATION_STAT takes in observations from devices of every project in the organisation. has_any_data (metrics.py:108) uses the same condition.

What the viewer sees: reports.metric_view (services/reports.py:167-169) filters only asset_ids. api/reports.py:_view (lines 391-397) passes observation_ranges through unchanged. The trace endpoint (services/reports.py:249, 277) also returns the stored ranges unfiltered.

Probe result (test_zz_rpa_a.py::test_rpa_period_report_mixes_other_project_observations, 1 passed):
- DEV_A_SENSOR is in prj_..201 with value 10.0; dev_..399 is in prj_..299 of the same organisation with value 30.0.
- The PERIOD report for prj_..201 gives value 20.0, input_count 2, and observation_ranges for both devices.
- Approve and submit both return 200. Viewer usr_105, who per seed.py:86 is assigned only to PRJ_A, then gets both device IDs from GET /v1/reports.

Not already known: contract-sweep-findings.md ('점검하지 못한 묶음', about line 297) says this bundle was never checked. An unrecorded probe for the same case (case G) is in docs/review-2026-11-05/repro/contract-sweep/reports-records-contracts-uploads/test_sweep_rrcu.py:113. No ADR, review README, HANDOFF entry or domain.md rule makes this intended.

Side is fix_impl: the contract text is right and the code does not follow it. Severity stays medium. The main harm is a wrong metric value in an approved, submitted evidence report. The leak is limited to device IDs and data aggregated from inside the same organisation, so it does not reach high.
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I reproduced the finding with my own probe, and nothing in the docs marks it as known or already decided. In the PERIOD branch of metrics._obs_where (backend/app/services/metrics.py:118-119), the only conditions are organization_id and the observed_at window. There is no project condition. Events (metrics.py:81) and assets (metrics.py:95-96) do filter on Scope.project_id, so observations are the only inconsistent case. My probe is independent of the finder's. I added a project B in ORG_A whose device dev_..388 is the only source of observations, and used a month (2025-03) that no seed data touches. A PERIOD report for PRJ_A (prj_..201) then returned value 77.0, input_count 1 and observation_ranges ['dev_..388']. The same report for project B gives the identical 77.0. The project A report is therefore made entirely of project B data, instead of showing 0 inputs or 'no record'. After approve (200) and submit (200), viewer 105 (PROGRAM_VIEWER in ORG_AGENCY, assigned only to PRJ_A in seed.py:86) ran GET /v1/reports/{id}. The response was 200, with value 77.0 and project B's device ID in observation_ranges. The same viewer gets 403 FORBIDDEN on GET /v1/observations, because observation:read is not granted to viewers (permissions.py:93). The report is a side channel to data from a project the viewer is not assigned to. That goes against domain.md section 2 ('assigned project' only) and against createReport's project_id scoping. reports.metric_view (reports.py:167-169) filters only asset_ids, not observation_ranges. The finder's probe tests what it claims: correct roles (102 creates, 101 approves and submits, 105 views), the ictx fixture, and a second project in the same org. Its result just adds the seed's DEV_A_SENSOR to the mix. I searched docs/review-2026-11-05, docs/review-2026-11-12, HANDOFF.md and .kiro and found nothing recording this as known or decided. The 11-05 sweep left an unreported probe ('G') for the same case in repro/contract-sweep/reports-records-contracts-uploads/test_sweep_rrcu.py, and that bundle is listed as not checked. The side should be fix_impl: the contract and domain text are right. Severity stays medium. The metric values in submitted reports are wrong and there is limited cross-project exposure (aggregates and device IDs, not raw rows).
- **고칠 쪽:** 구현 — In _obs_where's period branch, limit observations to the project: OBS.source_system_id in (select Device.device_id where project_id == s.project_id and organization_id == s.organization_id), or OBS.session_id in that project's sessions. Make has_any_data use the same condition. Add a regression test with two projects in one org.

### S31. [low] POST /v1/reports — wrong-status

**Period report with a valid late-9999 UTC window returns 500 (KST formatting overflow)**

- **계약:** createReport: from/to are utc_datetime (common.schema.json $defs/utc_datetime), and the documented responses are 201/400/401/403/404/429 (openapi.yaml:715-747). Bad-but-parseable input should not give 500.
- **구현:** generate() calls variables(), which formats period.from and period.to with kst() = dt.astimezone(Asia/Seoul). A from or to after 9999-12-31T14:59:59Z overflows datetime, and the unhandled OverflowError becomes 500 INTERNAL. Same class as sweep #13 (inspections 9999). The request rolls back and no REPORT_CREATED audit is written.
- **근거:** backend/app/services/reports.py:43-44, 58-62, 105; backend/app/api/reports.py:428-441
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_rpa_a.py::test_rpa_period_report_year_9999 — from 9999-12-31T10:00:00Z to 9999-12-31T14:59:59Z gives 201. from 9999-12-31T14:00:00Z or 15:00:00Z with to 9999-12-31T23:59:59Z gives 500 INTERNAL ('OverflowError: date value out of range' at reports.py:44); assert_matches_contract reports '계약에 500 응답이 없다'.
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I could not refute this. The contract (contracts/openapi.yaml, createReport) types from/to as common utc_datetime and lists only 201/400/401/403/404/429. The current code matches the finding.

- api/reports.py:428-433 only checks that from/to parse with parse_rfc3339 and that start < end. parse_rfc3339 guards against overflow only when converting to UTC (domain/normalize.py:45-48).
- reports.generate() calls variables() (services/reports.py:105). variables() calls kst(rep.period_from) and kst(rep.period_to) at line 62. kst() at line 44 runs dt.astimezone(Asia/Seoul), and this overflows for any instant after 9999-12-31T14:59:59Z.

I re-ran the finder's probe (rv container, test_zz_rpa_a.py -k year_9999):
- from 10:00Z / to 14:59:59Z gives 201.
- from 14:00Z / to 23:59:59Z gives 500 INTERNAL ("계약에 500 응답이 없다"). Only `to` overflows here.
- from 15:00Z / to 23:59:59Z gives 500 INTERNAL.
- The REPORT_CREATED audit count went up by exactly 1, so the two 500 requests left no audit row.

This is not recorded as known or intended anywhere. The same class of bug was sweep #13 in docs/review-2026-11-05/contract-sweep-findings.md (POST /v1/inspections with 9999). That one was fixed as an implementation fix: api/monitoring.py:254 now catches OverflowError and returns 400 on /period_key. So fix_impl is the right side, following precedent. Severity low is right: it is an edge case, nothing is left half-done, and it has no security impact.
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — My own probe reproduces the finding, with an exact boundary. A HM (102) creating a PERIOD-template report on PRJ_A got: from 2026-10-01 to 2026-11-01 gives 201 (control); from 9999-12-31T10:00Z to 14:59:59Z gives 201 and matches the contract; from 10:00Z to 15:00:00Z gives 500 INTERNAL; from 15:00Z to 16:00Z gives 500 INTERNAL. In both 500 cases assert_matches_contract says '계약에 500 응답이 없다'. Only the one 201 in the 9999 cases wrote REPORT_CREATED (audit+1), so the 500 requests left no audit and rolled back.

The cause is KST formatting, not parsing. The break is at exactly 15:00:00Z, which is UTC+9 = 10000-01-01 KST. That fits services/reports.py:43-44, where kst() calls dt.astimezone(Asia/Seoul) and is called from variables() at :60 via generate() at :105. It also shows that only `to` needs to overflow (case 10:00Z to 15:00Z).

parse_rfc3339 (domain/normalize.py:37-48) accepts these values: it only turns an OverflowError during conversion to UTC into an error, and a UTC value up to 9999-12-31T23:59:59Z is fine. So the input is valid and parseable, and the 500 is not documented in the contract (POST /v1/reports lists 201/400/401/403/404/429).

This is the same class as the inspections 9999 finding in docs/review-2026-11-05/contract-sweep-findings.md. That one was fixed (backend/tests/integration/test_inspection_period_bounds.py), so the project treats this class as a defect to fix, not as intended behaviour.

The finder's probe is sound: it uses the right role (102 has report:write), a PERIOD template made by OP 106, and a fresh ctx DB. The finder's second case (from 14:00Z) only overflows on `to`, which my probe confirms on its own.

Severity low (an edge case nobody hits in real use) and side fix_impl are both correct.
- **고칠 쪽:** 구현 — In create_report, reject from/to whose KST form overflows (for example, check that start/end + 9h stays representable) with 400 VALIDATION_ERROR on /from or /to. Or make kst() fall back safely for out-of-range values.

### S32. [low] PUT /v1/report-templates/{template_code}/versions/{version}/hwpx — wrong-status

**version=0 is accepted and attaches the HWPX to the latest template version**

- **계약:** openapi.yaml:660-663: path parameter version is {type: integer, minimum: 1}, so version 0 is a format error (400 BadRequest). The summary says the file is attached to the named template version (openapi.yaml:667).
- **구현:** The route declares version as a plain int with no ge=1. reports.latest_template(db, code, version) treats a falsy version as 'latest' ('if version'), so .../versions/0/hwpx returns 200, stores the file on the newest version, and records REPORT_TEMPLATE_HWPX_SET for that version. Negative versions return 404.
- **근거:** backend/app/api/reports.py:323-343 (line 324 'version: int', line 330); backend/app/services/reports.py:47-50 (line 49 'if version')
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_rpa_a.py::test_rpa_hwpx_version_zero_attaches_to_latest — With RPA_TPL v1 and v2 created, PUT .../RPA_TPL/versions/0/hwpx gives 200 with body version=2. The listing shows {1: False, 2: True}, with one REPORT_TEMPLATE_HWPX_SET audit. versions/-1 gives 404 NOT_FOUND.
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I could not refute it, and the finding is real. Contract (contracts/openapi.yaml:660-663): the path parameter version is {type: integer, minimum: 1}, so 0 and negative values should fail validation as 400 BadRequest. The summary at line 667 says the file is attached to the named template version. Implementation: backend/app/api/reports.py:324 declares `version: int` with no Path(ge=1). Line 330 calls reports.latest_template(db, template_code, version). backend/app/services/reports.py:49 uses `if version` (a truthiness test), so version=0 falls through to the 'latest version' branch (order_by version desc). The route then stores the file under the latest version's key (v{t.version}) and records REPORT_TEMPLATE_HWPX_SET for that version. I re-ran the finder's probe in od-rv-py: `OBS hwpx_v0 200 2 RPA_TPL {1: False, 2: True} audit+ 1 ok` and `OBS hwpx_v-1 404 NOT_FOUND`. So 0 gives 200 on v2, and -1 gives 404 where the contract says 400. The probe does what it claims: it uses the OP role, creates two versions, and lists them and the audit before and after. Not already known: docs/review-2026-11-05/contract-sweep-findings.md:297-300 lists the reports·records·contracts·uploads bundle as never checked. Its leftover scratch probe docs/review-2026-11-05/repro/contract-sweep/reports-records-contracts-uploads/test_sweep_rrcu.py:48-58 tested this exact case ('B'), but no finding was ever written for it. I found nothing in HANDOFF.md, docs/review-2026-11-12/README.md or the ADRs that records it as fixed, known or decided. Severity stays low: the route is operator-only (report_template:write) and only a wrong path value triggers it, though a template version that was not named does get changed. Side stays fix_impl, because the contract text is explicit and the behaviour it describes is the sensible one.
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — My own probe reproduced the finding. The finder's probe needed ictx because FakeStorage has no put_object (a ctx run fails with an AttributeError at reports.py:339), so its fixture choice was right. My probe creates RPAV_TPL v1 and v2 as operator 106, then attaches a file to v1. PUT .../RPAV_TPL/versions/0/hwpx then returns 200 with version=2 and has_hwpx=True. The DB now holds hwpx_key ops/report-templates/RPAV_TPL/v2.hwpx, and an audit REPORT_TEMPLATE_HWPX_SET with target RPAV_TPL:v2 is recorded. versions/-1 gives 404 NOT_FOUND and versions/abc gives 400 VALIDATION_ERROR. So the route does validate path types, but it has no minimum. Code: backend/app/api/reports.py:324 declares 'version: int' with no ge=1. backend/app/services/reports.py:49 tests 'if version', so 0 means latest. The contract (openapi.yaml path parameter version {type: integer, minimum: 1}) makes 0 a 400 BadRequest. An earlier 11-05 scratch probe (docs/review-2026-11-05/repro/contract-sweep/reports-records-contracts-uploads/test_sweep_rrcu.py:48 'B. PUT .../versions/0/hwpx') touched this case. But contract-sweep-findings.md:297-299 and HANDOFF.md:64 list this bundle as unchecked, so it is not recorded as known or decided. Severity low is correct: an operator-only edge case where a file lands on a real version that exists. Side fix_impl is correct.
- **고칠 쪽:** 구현 — Declare version with Path(ge=1) so 0 and negative values get 400. In latest_template, test 'version is not None' instead of truthiness, so the other callers cannot hit the same thing.

### S33. [low] PUT /v1/report-templates/{template_code}/versions/{version}/hwpx — wrong-status

**Oversize HWPX body returns undocumented 413 PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE**

- **계약:** openapi.yaml:670-685: the body schema has maxLength 10485760, and the responses are 200/400/401/403/404/429. There is no 413, although components/responses/PayloadTooLarge exists and api-conventions.md:44 maps PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE to 413.
- **구현:** A body over 10 MiB raises ProblemError('PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE'), which gives 413.
- **근거:** backend/app/api/reports.py:333-334; backend/app/errors.py:44
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_rpa_a.py::test_rpa_hwpx_oversize — A body of 10 MiB + 1 byte gives 413 PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE; assert_matches_contract reports '계약에 413 응답이 없다'.
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I couldn't refute this one. The contract lists only 200/400/401/403/404/429 for putReportTemplateHwpx (contracts/openapi.yaml:676-685). The schema has maxLength 10485760, but no response tells a client what happens past that limit. The implementation raises ProblemError("PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE") when len(data) > 10*1024*1024 (backend/app/api/reports.py:333-334), and errors.py:44 maps that to 413. The convention is clear: api-conventions.md:44 maps PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE to 413 for any body or batch size limit, and POST /v1/observations already documents '413': PayloadTooLarge (openapi.yaml:2609). So the behaviour is right and the contract just leaves it out, which makes fix_contract the correct side. This is not an already-known item. HANDOFF.md:61 and review-2026-11-12/README.md:84,116 (V5, owner decision 5) cover a different question: whether there should be an app-wide request body size limit in middleware or only at the proxy. They say nothing about this route's own 10 MiB check being missing from the contract. The web proxy limit (20m) is above 10 MiB, so clients really do see the 413 from the app. I re-ran the finder's probe and got: "OBS hwpx_oversize 413 PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE VIOLATION: ... 계약에 413 응답이 없다". The probe uses the operator role on the seeded EXAMPLE_SESSION_LOG v1 template, so the 404 and 403 checks pass first and the 413 really does come from the size check. Low severity is right: it is an edge case with a documented error code.
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I reproduced this with my own probe, using the seeded PLATFORM_OPERATOR (user 106, MFA on; report_template:write is PLATFORM_OPERATOR only, permissions.py:108) against the seeded template EXAMPLE_SESSION_LOG v1. A body of exactly 10 MiB of zero bytes passes the size check and gets 400 VALIDATION_ERROR from check_hwpx. A body of 10 MiB + 1 byte gets 413 application/problem+json with code PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE. On the 413 response, assert_matches_contract raises "계약에 413 응답이 없다". So the 413 really comes from the route's own size check, not from a wrong role, fixture or other failure. The finder's probe tested the same thing correctly; it only used ctx.as_user(OP) without MFA, which turned out not to matter.

In the code, backend/app/api/reports.py:333-334 does `if len(data) > 10*1024*1024: raise ProblemError("PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE")`, and errors.py maps PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE to 413. In the contract, openapi.yaml lists only 200/400/401/403/404/429 for putReportTemplateHwpx, even though components/responses/PayloadTooLarge exists and another operation already uses it (openapi.yaml:2609). api-conventions.md:44 maps PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE to 413 for body size limits.

No global body-size middleware exists in backend/app. I found nothing about this in contract-sweep-findings.md, docs/review-2026-11-12/README.md or HANDOFF.md.

fix_contract is the right side: the behaviour matches the project-wide convention, and the contract is silent. Low severity is right: it is an edge case, and the status code clients see is the conventional one.
- **고칠 쪽:** 계약 문구 — Add '413': {$ref: '#/components/responses/PayloadTooLarge'} to putReportTemplateHwpx (413 is the project-wide code for body size limits), then regenerate the clients.

**일치를 확인한 작업**

- GET /v1/metric-definitions: report:read (providers, PROGRAM_VIEWER, operator, API key with status:read; devices denied), all versions ordered by code and version, MetricDefinition shape with formula
- POST /v1/metric-definitions: operator only (403 plus PERMISSION_DENIED audit for others), a new version for the same code, per-kind params validation gives 400, METRIC_DEFINITION_CREATED audit
- GET /v1/report-templates: report:read scope, all versions, has_hwpx/hwpx_sha256
- POST /v1/report-templates: operator only, unknown metric codes and duplicate field keys give 400, explicit nulls in text/fields/metrics give 400, a new version for the same code, REPORT_TEMPLATE_CREATED audit
- GET /v1/reports: scope_filter (own org for providers and API keys; viewer limited to assigned projects with a submitted version, showing the latest submitted version), limit/cursor validation (limit 0 gives 400, a NUL cursor gives 400), session_id format gives 400, cursor pagination by report_id

**발견 담당의 메모 (발견으로 올리지 않은 것)**

> Probe file: C:/Users/user/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/69809d01a353b176/ef4ed640-70bd-41cd-a22c-ed40e2ba8d1d/scratchpad/night/wt/rv/backend/tests/integration/test_zz_rpa_a.py (6 tests, OBS lines; the storage-dependent tests use ictx because FakeStorage has no put_object). The earlier sweep's leftover probes B and G (docs/review-2026-11-05/repro/contract-sweep/reports-records-contracts-uploads/test_sweep_rrcu.py) tested hwpx version 0 and period observations, but the findings document never recorded them, so they are reported above against the current code.
> 
> Not reported (low, unclear, cross-cutting or already known):
> (1) POST /v1/reports does not check the session_id/project_id pattern: session_id 'garbage' gives 404 instead of 400. from/to accept non-Z offsets (e.g. +09:00), although utc_datetime requires Z; the same file enforces Z for ObsRef.
> (2) PUT hwpx with a template_code that breaks the pattern gives 404, not 400.
> (3) Concurrent POSTs of the same metric or template code collide on the PK at flush and give 409 CONFLICT through the ADR-0053 mapping. createMetricDefinition and createReportTemplate do not list 409. This is cross-cutting (the generic Conflict text) and I did not reproduce it.
> (4) For a PROGRAM_VIEWER, Report.latest_version shows the count of newer unsubmitted versions (e.g. version 1 shown with latest_version 2, while ?version=2 gives 404). This is a minor existence hint, not covered by contract text.
> (5) PUT hwpx can replace the HWPX of a template version that approved or submitted reports already use, and exports render from the current template file. Whether template versions must be immutable after use is a product decision (needs_owner if pursued).
> (6) Operators reading other organisations' report drafts without audit (GET /v1/reports) is already listed in HANDOFF 5장. The HWPX encoding and ZIP-path items are already V7 '미완'. Success audits outside the request transaction are the known general topic.

## reports (버전·승인·내보내기) — 발견 5건

### S34. [medium] POST /v1/reports/{report_id}/versions — data-semantics

**Creating a new version rewrites period_to of the earlier approved/submitted versions**

- **계약:** contracts/openapi.yaml:780 createReportVersion summary: "최신 데이터로 새 초안 버전을 만든다 (이전 버전은 보존)". Report.version/period_to (openapi.yaml:5780-5785) describe the requested version. requirements 10-5.2: approval fixes (고정) that version.
- **구현:** new_report_version sets rep.period_to on the shared Report row (one row for all versions) to the session's ends_at or now. report_view() takes period_from/period_to from the Report row, not from the version, so GET ?version=1 (APPROVED or SUBMITTED) and the PROGRAM_VIEWER's view of the submitted version now show the new period_to. The EVIDENCE snapshot of v1 still holds the old value, and v1's metrics and frozen variables were computed for the old period. The API now disagrees with the hashed snapshot and with the version's own numbers.
- **근거:** backend/app/api/reports.py:471-474 (rep.period_to = s.ends_at or now); backend/app/services/reports.py:155-158 (report_view uses rep.period_from/rep.period_to); backend/app/services/reports.py:215-218 (snapshot = report_view at approve time); backend/app/db/models.py Report holds period_from/period_to, ReportVersion has none
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_rpb_a.py::test_new_version_rewrites_previous_period_to — OBS period_to v1 before: 2026-11-12T13:14:16Z snapshot: 2026-11-12T13:14:16Z v1 after new version: 2026-11-03T00:00:00Z v1 status: SUBMITTED v2: 2026-11-03T00:00:00Z; viewer sees submitted v1 period_to before=2026-11-12T13:14:16Z after=2026-11-03T00:00:00Z. AssertionError: approved/submitted v1 period_to changed after new version
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I could not refute this finding. Code and probe both confirm it.

(1) Contract: contracts/openapi.yaml:780 says createReportVersion makes a new draft "(이전 버전은 보존)". The Report schema (openapi.yaml:5780-5781) returns period_from/period_to as part of a per-version view. Spec 10 requirements:34 says an approved item cannot be modified and only a new version can change it.

(2) Implementation: backend/app/api/reports.py:471-473 assigns rep.period_to = s.ends_at or now on the single shared Report row before generate(). backend/app/db/models.py:916-917 shows period_from/period_to exist only on Report; ReportVersion has no such columns. backend/app/services/reports.py:155-158 (report_view) reads iso(rep.period_to) for every version. approve() (services/reports.py:215-218) hashes report_view(rep, v) into the EVIDENCE snapshot. So after POST /versions, GET ?version=1 returns a period_to that differs from the hashed snapshot. That version's frozen variables (period.to, services/reports.py:62) and metrics were computed for the old period, so the API also contradicts the version's own numbers.

(3) Not already known: docs/review-2026-11-05/contract-sweep-findings.md:297-300 lists the reports·records·contracts·uploads bundle as never checked. Only an unfinished intermediate probe for this exact case exists (docs/review-2026-11-05/repro/contract-sweep/reports-records-contracts-uploads/test_sweep_rrcu2.py:31-48), and it was never recorded as a finding. Nothing in HANDOFF, the ADRs or domain.md makes this intended. domain.md:92/144 only covers the SUPERSEDED transition.

(4) I re-ran the finder's probe in od-rv-py: tests/integration/test_zz_rpb_a.py::test_new_version_rewrites_previous_period_to fails on the assertion 'approved/submitted v1 period_to changed after new version'. The probe is sound. It uses the manager (102) to create and to make the new version, ORG_ADMIN (101) to approve and submit, and the viewer (105) to read. It compares against the real EVIDENCE snapshot.

Side fix_impl is correct. Medium severity is right: an auditor or the PROGRAM_VIEWER sees a submitted version whose period disagrees with its hashed snapshot. The snapshot itself is intact, so nothing is lost.
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — The finding holds, and it is broader than the finder's repro shows. I wrote my own probe with no session PATCH: an ongoing session (seed SES_A, ends_at=None) and an approved and submitted v1, then a 1.2 s wait, then POST /versions. GET ?version=1 (still SUBMITTED) and the PROGRAM_VIEWER's GET both changed period_to: "OBS v1 period_to before: 2026-11-12T13:21:05Z after: 2026-11-12T13:21:07Z status: SUBMITTED | v2: 2026-11-12T13:21:07Z | viewer v1 before=2026-11-12T13:21:05Z after=2026-11-12T13:21:07Z". So any new version of a SESSION report rewrites the period shown for every earlier version, including the frozen approved or submitted one, not only after a session end time is set. Code: backend/app/api/reports.py:471-473 assigns rep.period_to on the single shared Report row. backend/app/services/reports.py:155-158 report_view reads period_from/period_to from that Report row, not from the ReportVersion. ReportVersion has no period columns. approve() (services/reports.py:215-218) hashes report_view() into the EVIDENCE snapshot, so the stored snapshot keeps the old period while the API shows the new one. The contract summary for createReportVersion (openapi.yaml:780) says the earlier version is kept ("이전 버전은 보존"). Not already known: docs/review-2026-11-05/repro/contract-sweep/reports-records-contracts-uploads/test_sweep_rrcu2.py (section I) holds an old probe of the same behaviour. contract-sweep-findings.md:297-300 says that bundle was never reported, and its probes were only "참고". It is not in README, HANDOFF section 5 or the ADRs. The finder's probe is sound: right roles, ictx (needed because FakeStorage has no put_object), and it compares with the real snapshot. Severity medium and side fix_impl are right.
- **고칠 쪽:** 구현 — Keep the period per version: add period_from/period_to to report_versions (migration with up/down and a test) set in generate(), and have report_view read them from the version. Alternatively compute the new period into a local Scope and do not mutate the Report row. Add a regression test showing GET ?version=1 equals the snapshot's period_to after POST /versions.

### S35. [medium] GET /v1/reports/{report_id}/export — missing-behavior

**A HWPX export of a DRAFT has no "초안" mark unless the template happens to contain {{report.status}}**

- **계약:** openapi.yaml:885 exportReport summary: "초안은 \"초안\" 표시". requirements 10-4.4: "문서에 \"초안\" 표시, 생성 시각, 데이터 기준 시각을 넣는다". ADR-0018 결과: "초안은 모든 형식에 \"초안\"·생성 시각·데이터 기준 시각을 넣는다".
- **구현:** render_hwpx only replaces {{...}} placeholders that the operator's template contains. The draft label reaches the file only through {{report.status}}. A template without that placeholder produces a DRAFT HWPX with 200 that looks like a final document. XLSX and PDF always add the label. Nothing at template upload (check_hwpx) or at export refuses or warns.
- **근거:** backend/app/adapters/report_renderers.py:196-224 (hwpx_values/render_hwpx: substitution only), :180-193 (check_hwpx does not require report.status); backend/app/api/reports.py:541-544
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_rpb_a.py::test_hwpx_draft_has_no_draft_mark — OBS status: DRAFT HWPX: 200 ['<sec><p>실증일지 (예시 양식) — A-2026-11-01 / v1 / 회차 A-2026-11-01 실증 결과 요약</p></sec>'] 초안 in HWPX: False | XLSX: 200 초안 in XLSX: True
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I could not refute it; the code supports the finding. The contract promises the mark without conditions: openapi.yaml:885 exportReport summary says '초안은 "초안" 표시'. requirements 10-4.4 says the same, and so does ADR-0018 결과: '초안은 모든 형식에 "초안"·생성 시각·데이터 기준 시각을 넣는다'. 10-evidence-reports/design.md:17 repeats it ('모든 형식에 상태(초안 등)·생성 시각·데이터 기준 시각'). In the implementation, the PDF (report_renderers.py:68,75) and the XLSX (:126) always write doc['status_label']. The HWPX path never does. hwpx_values (:196-207) only builds a dictionary, and render_hwpx (:210-224) replaces only the PLACEHOLDER matches that the uploaded template contains; design.md:29-31 adds '모르는 자리표시자는 그대로 둔다'. A template without {{report.status}} therefore gives a DRAFT HWPX with no draft label. check_hwpx (:180-193) checks only the zip, XML entries, paths and size. export_report (api/reports.py:541-544) renders and returns 200. No document lists this as known or decided. HANDOFF.md:61 has only 'HWPX 양식 인코딩·ZIP 경로' as unfinished. docs/review-2026-11-12/README.md V6/V7 cover control characters, quotes, encoding and paths. The 11-05 sweep left an unfinished probe for exactly this case (docs/review-2026-11-05/repro/contract-sweep/reports-records-contracts-uploads/test_sweep_rrcu.py:60 'C. HWPX export of a DRAFT without {{report.status}}'), but its bundle was never reported ('점검하지 못한 묶음'), so the finding is not a re-report. The side is fix_impl: the contract text is clear and needs no domain.md change, and the existing 409 Conflict response of exportReport can carry a refusal. Medium severity is right. Real agency forms (T11-f) will only have the placeholders an operator adds, so a final-looking draft file is a realistic result. I did not write or run a probe of my own (reading lens); the finder's probe uploads a template without the placeholder and compares HWPX with XLSX, which is what it claims to test.
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I reproduced it on origin/main with my own probe. PUT /v1/report-templates/EXAMPLE_SESSION_LOG/versions/1/hwpx (user 106) with a template that has {{report.title}} {{report.generated_at}} {{report.data_as_of}} but no {{report.status}} returns 200. A new report is created (DRAFT). GET .../export?format=HWPX (user 102) returns 200 application/hwp+zip, and the XML is '<sec><p>실증일지 (예시 양식) — A-2026-11-01 2026-11-12 22:20 KST 2026-11-12 22:20 KST</p></sec>', with no "초안" anywhere. As a control I re-uploaded the same template with {{report.status}} added. The export then contains '초안 실증일지 ...'. So the draft mark depends only on that placeholder.

Code: report_renderers.py:209-224 (render_hwpx) only substitutes PLACEHOLDER matches, and the label reaches the file only through values['report.status'] (:198). check_hwpx (:180-193) checks only zip / XML entry / paths / size. reports.py export_report (HWPX branch) neither checks nor adds anything. PDF (:68,:75) and XLSX (:126) always write status_label.

Contract: openapi.yaml exportReport summary '초안은 "초안" 표시'. Requirements 10-4.4 say the same. ADR-0018 결과 says '초안은 모든 형식에 "초안"·생성 시각·데이터 기준 시각을 넣는다'. design.md 'HWPX 변수' only lists report.status as an available variable. It does not say the mark is optional for HWPX. None of the known lists cover this: HANDOFF 5, review-2026-11-12 V6/V7 (those are about control characters, quotes, encoding and ZIP paths) and the T11-f '확인 필요' note (that is about placeholder splitting in real forms).

I read the finder's probe test_zz_rpb_a.py::test_hwpx_draft_has_no_draft_mark critically. Its roles (106 operator for PUT, 102 for export), its fixture (ictx with REPORT_FORMATS including HWPX) and its DRAFT state are all correct, and it fails for the stated reason. Severity medium is right. A draft HWPX looks like a final document to a reader, but approval and submission state is still tracked in the API, so this is not data loss. Side fix_impl: the contract text is consistent and explicit.
- **고칠 쪽:** 구현 — Guarantee the mark for non-final HWPX exports. Either refuse (409 CONFLICT with a detail) a DRAFT HWPX export when no XML entry contains {{report.status}}, or require that placeholder at PUT .../hwpx (that is a contract change for the PUT). Both must work without knowing the real agency form (확인 필요, T11-f), so do not inject into the HWPX structure.

### S36. [low] GET /v1/reports/{report_id}/export — wrong-status

**Format gate runs before the scope check: a cross-org or missing report gives 409 instead of 404, with no CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT audit**

- **계약:** openapi.yaml NotFound: "없음 또는 다른 기관 리소스 (둘을 구분하지 않는다)"; security.md:10 says a request for another organization's resource gets 404 and is written to the audit log.
- **구현:** export_report checks `format not in enabled_formats(...)` and raises 409 CONFLICT before _load_report. For a disabled format, another organization's report_id and a nonexistent report_id both get 409, and no CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT is recorded. The same caller gets 404 plus an audit row on GET /v1/reports/{id}.
- **근거:** backend/app/api/reports.py:529-531 (format gate) before :531 _load_report; backend/app/api/deps.py:111-123
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_rpb_a.py::test_export_disabled_format_cross_org_and_missing — OBS cross-org export (format off): 409 CONFLICT audit + 0 | cross-org GET: 404 audit + 1 | missing report export: 409
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I couldn't refute this by reading. The code does what the finding says. In backend/app/api/reports.py:529-530, export_report raises ProblemError("CONFLICT") when `format not in rr.enabled_formats(c.settings.report_formats)`. Only after that does line 531 call _load_report. _load_report goes through load_scoped (deps.py:111-123), which is the only place that writes CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT and raises 404. So when the format is turned off, a report from another organization and a report_id that does not exist both get 409, and no audit row is written.

The rule that is broken is real. security.md:10 says a request for another organization's resource gets 404 and is written to the audit log, and openapi.yaml NotFound (line 4741) says the same. The export operation lists 409, but its text gives no order of checks that would put the format gate ahead of the scope check. I found nothing that records this order as intended or already known: no ADR (ADR-0018 only says when PDF is on), nothing in docs/review-2026-11-05 or docs/review-2026-11-12, nothing in HANDOFF. api-conventions.md sets a check order only for UNSUPPORTED_SCHEMA_VERSION.

The finder's probe tests what it claims. User 103 is ORG_B ORG_ADMIN (seed.py:52), so the report:read check passes and the request reaches the format gate. ctx runs with REPORT_FORMATS empty. The same user gets 404 plus one audit row on GET /v1/reports/{id}.

Low severity is right. The 409 is the same for any report_id, so nothing about whether a report exists leaks. What an auditor loses is the CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT row while a format is off. fix_impl is the right side, because the contract and security.md are clear.
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — My own probe confirms it. backend/app/api/reports.py:529-530 checks the format against `rr.enabled_formats(c.settings.report_formats)` and raises 409 CONFLICT before `_load_report` at :531, which is where `load_scoped` (deps.py:111-123) writes CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT and returns 404. The `ctx` fixture has report_formats=[] (printed by the probe). With the format off, a cross-org export by user 103 (ORG_B) of an ORG_A report got 409 CONFLICT and 0 audit rows. GET /v1/reports/{id} from the same caller got 404 and 1 audit row. When I turned XLSX on (settings.report_formats=["XLSX"]), the same cross-org export got 404 and 1 audit row, so the check order is the only cause. The finder's probe tests what it claims: user 103 is ORG_ADMIN of ORG_B, it uses the right fixture, and MFA is not needed for report:read. This goes against security.md:10, which says a cross-org request gets 404 and an audit record, and against the contract's NotFound response ("없음 또는 다른 기관 리소스"). Low severity is right. The answer is 409 for an own report, a cross-org report and a missing report alike, so it does not leak whether a report exists. The real effect is a missing cross-org audit row while a format is off. This is the only route that runs a settings check before the scope load (grep over app/api found no other), so this is not a deliberate pattern. I found no ADR, review document or HANDOFF item that records this. fix_impl is the right side.
- **고칠 쪽:** 구현 — Call _load_report (and _report_version) first, then the format check. Existing test_reports_api.py asserts 409 only for an own-org report, so it stays valid.

### S37. [low] GET /v1/reports/{report_id}/export — wrong-status

**/v1/system/capabilities lists PDF while export refuses PDF (renderer unavailable)**

- **계약:** openapi.yaml:885: "켜진 형식만, /v1/capabilities의 report_formats". This makes capabilities.report_formats the list of formats export accepts. ADR-0018 결정 1: PDF is on only when REPORT_FORMATS has PDF and WeasyPrint can be imported.
- **구현:** capabilities returns settings.report_formats filtered only by name. Export uses rr.enabled_formats(), which also drops PDF when pdf_available() is false. With PDF configured and WeasyPrint/pango missing, capabilities advertises PDF and export returns 409 "PDF 내보내기가 켜져 있지 않습니다 (REPORT_FORMATS)". That message is also misleading, because REPORT_FORMATS does contain PDF.
- **근거:** backend/app/api/system.py:86; backend/app/adapters/report_renderers.py:49-58; backend/app/api/reports.py:529-530
