- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_rpb_a.py::test_capabilities_vs_export_when_pdf_unavailable — OBS capabilities.report_formats: ['XLSX', 'HWPX', 'PDF'] export PDF: 409 {... 'code': 'CONFLICT', 'detail': 'PDF 내보내기가 켜져 있지 않습니다 (REPORT_FORMATS)'}
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — Reading the code and docs confirms the finding. (1) The contract ties export to capabilities: openapi.yaml:885 has exportReport summary "켜진 형식만, /v1/capabilities의 report_formats". ADR-0018 결정 1 (docs/adr/0018-report-rendering.md:20-21) says PDF is on only when REPORT_FORMATS contains PDF and WeasyPrint can be imported, and that a failed import "기능만 꺼지고 서버는 뜬다". So when the import fails, PDF is a feature that is off, and getCapabilities ("켜진 기능", openapi.yaml:92) should not list it. (2) The implementation disagrees. backend/app/api/system.py:86 builds report_formats from settings.report_formats filtered only by name. backend/app/api/reports.py:529-530 checks rr.enabled_formats(), which drops PDF when pdf_available() is false (backend/app/adapters/report_renderers.py:49-58), and then returns 409 CONFLICT with detail "PDF 내보내기가 켜져 있지 않습니다 (REPORT_FORMATS)". That detail is misleading, because REPORT_FORMATS does contain PDF. (3) The finder's probe is sound. enabled_formats looks up pdf_available as a module global at call time, so the monkeypatch of rr.pdf_available reaches export, and capabilities never calls it. User 102 is the same-org user used for the other export probes. (4) A client really observes this: web/src/pages/Reports.tsx:138 draws one download button per caps.report_formats entry, so users get a PDF button that always returns 409. (5) It is not already recorded. The 2026-11-05 sweep only left an unreported repro (docs/review-2026-11-05/repro/contract-sweep/reports-records-contracts-uploads/test_sweep_rrcu.py, block D). contract-sweep-findings.md lists that bundle under "점검하지 못한 묶음". HANDOFF section 5 only has the Pillow/Pyphen license approval, which is a different issue. Severity stays low: backend/Dockerfile installs pango and fonts, so production normally has pdf_available() true, and this only happens when WeasyPrint or pango fails to load. The side is fix_impl, because the contract and ADR text are right and the code is inconsistent.
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — My own probe reproduces the finding. The finder's probe tests what it claims: it monkeypatches rr.pdf_available, the same module global that reports.py:529 reaches through rr.enabled_formats. It runs on ictx, where report_formats=['XLSX','HWPX','PDF'] (backend/tests/conftest.py:190), with role 102, which can read and export, so the 409 is not caused by permissions.

My probe test_zz_rpbv3x_a.py sets report_formats=['XLSX','PDF'] on ctx and runs once with the renderer present and once without it:
- Renderer present (pdf_ok=True): capabilities lists ['XLSX','PDF'], and XLSX and PDF both export with 200.
- Renderer missing (pdf_ok=False): capabilities still lists ['XLSX','PDF']. XLSX exports with 200, but PDF returns 409 CONFLICT with detail 'PDF 내보내기가 켜져 있지 않습니다 (REPORT_FORMATS)', although REPORT_FORMATS does contain PDF.

Where the two sides differ in the code:
- backend/app/api/system.py:86 filters report_formats by name only.
- backend/app/adapters/report_renderers.py:57-58 (enabled_formats) also drops PDF when WeasyPrint cannot be imported.

What the documents say:
- The contract (openapi.yaml:885) ties export to capabilities.report_formats.
- ADR-0018 decision 1 says PDF is on only when REPORT_FORMATS has PDF and WeasyPrint can be imported, so the implementation is the side to change.
- A client sees the effect: web/src/pages/Reports.tsx:138 draws one download button per entry in caps.report_formats, so a PDF button appears and then fails.

Not already recorded: the 2026-11-05 repro file test_sweep_rrcu.py (block D) probed the same thing, but no review .md file, HANDOFF section 5 or ADR records it as fixed, known or waiting for a decision.

Severity stays low. The shipped backend/Dockerfile:22 installs pango, so this only happens in deployments without the PDF system libraries. The real container has WeasyPrint (real_pdf_available=True), so the missing-renderer case was simulated by monkeypatch, not by removing the library.
- **고칠 쪽:** 구현 — In capabilities, use rr.enabled_formats(s.report_formats) so it matches export, and make the export 409 detail say when the PDF renderer is unavailable.

### S38. [low] GET /v1/reports/{report_id} — scope-or-permission

**PROGRAM_VIEWER sees latest_version that counts unsubmitted drafts**

- **계약:** domain.md 2장: PROGRAM_VIEWER sees only 제출(SUBMITTED)된 보고서·지표. openapi.yaml:691/702: the viewer gets the submitted reports, each at its "보이는 최신 버전". The implementation's own intent (reports.py:369): "제출본이 없으면 존재를 드러내지 않는다".
- **구현:** For a viewer, _report_version limits the version to submitted ones, but report_view always returns latest_version = rep.latest_version. So the viewer learns that newer non-submitted versions exist, and how many. The web page shows it as "버전 1 / 2". The same happens in GET /v1/reports (list) and in trace's metric view, which uses the same _view.
- **근거:** backend/app/services/reports.py:159 (latest_version: rep.latest_version); backend/app/api/reports.py:373-381, 391-397, 409; web/src/pages/Reports.tsx:145
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_rpb_a.py::test_new_version_rewrites_previous_period_to (OBS line) — OBS viewer sees submitted v1 ... latest_version before=1 after=2 (v2 is a DRAFT the viewer cannot open: GET ?version=2 -> 404)
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I read the code and could not refute the core claim. backend/app/services/reports.py:159 always returns "latest_version": rep.latest_version, and Report.latest_version goes up for every new draft (reports.py:122, `rep.latest_version += 1`). For a viewer, backend/app/api/reports.py:373-381 (`_report_version`) limits the returned version to submitted ones, but `_view` (391-397) never adjusts latest_version. So a PROGRAM_VIEWER calling GET /v1/reports/{id} (453) or GET /v1/reports (409) learns that unsubmitted drafts exist and how many. The same applies to the other `_view` callers at 445/475/499/511/522, but those are write routes a viewer cannot reach. The finder's probe really uses the viewer (user 105) and prints latest_version before and after POST /versions; nothing in it makes the result come from another cause. On the contract side, the Report schema's latest_version (contracts/openapi.yaml:5786) has no description, so the contract does not spell out the field's meaning. But domain.md:27/39 says PROGRAM_VIEWER sees only 제출(SUBMITTED) reports, listReports says 'each report's 보이는 최신 버전' (openapi.yaml:702), and the code's own intent at reports.py:369 is not to reveal a report that has no submitted version. Revealing newer drafts goes against that. The web page shows '버전 1 / 2' (web/src/pages/Reports.tsx:145), and the viewer gets 404 on ?version=2. This is not recorded as known or decided: ADR-0047 and HANDOFF 52/92 (report-scope-leaks) cover asset IDs, damage and trace, not latest_version. HANDOFF 5장/274 is about operators reading drafts, not viewers. One part of the finding is wrong: the trace endpoint (api/reports.py:552-559) uses reports.trace, not `_view`, and does not return latest_version, so trace is not affected. Severity low is right: only a count of drafts leaks, inside the viewer's assigned project, with no content. fix_impl is right.
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I reproduced this with my own probe, which does not rely on the finder's probe. The provider manager (102) creates a report. The provider admin (101) approves and submits it. The PROGRAM_VIEWER (105, ORG_AGENCY) first sees version=1 latest=1. The manager then makes two new draft versions. Output: "OBS4X viewer after: get version=1 latest=3 status=SUBMITTED | list version=1 latest=3 | ?version=3 -> 404 | owner v3 status=DRAFT". So the viewer is shown v1 and cannot open v3 (404), yet latest_version=3 tells it that two newer versions exist that were never submitted, and how many. The viewer is in another organisation, so this is a small leak across organisations: it reveals whether drafts exist and how many. Code: backend/app/services/reports.py:159 always returns rep.latest_version. backend/app/api/reports.py:373-381 (_report_version) limits only `version` to submitted versions for _viewer(p). _view at 391-397 does not override latest_version. The list (409) and getReport (453) both go through _view. Contract: openapi.yaml:702 promises "각 보고서의 보이는 최신 버전". domain.md:39 says PROGRAM_VIEWER sees only 제출(SUBMITTED)된 보고서. The code's own rule at reports.py:369 is "제출본이 없으면 존재를 드러내지 않는다". The Report schema at openapi.yaml:5786 does not define latest_version, so the text is silent rather than contradictory. Even so, read with domain.md, the viewer should only see what was submitted, so fix_impl is correct. I found no mention of latest_version in docs/review-2026-11-12, docs/adr or HANDOFF. The finder's probe is sound: it uses ictx because approve needs the real S3 storage, and the FakeStorage in ctx has no put_object (I hit this myself). Severity low is right: only a count and existence are leaked, not content. I did not check the finder's side claim about trace's metric view.
- **고칠 쪽:** 구현 — In _view, set latest_version for _viewer(p) to the highest submitted version, using the same query as _report_version. Add a viewer case to the regression test.

**일치를 확인한 작업**

- PATCH /v1/reports/{report_id}/fields: 200 shape matches; unknown key 400; missing value 400; viewer 403; other org 404 (load_scoped audits CROSS_ORG); non-DRAFT latest 409 INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION; edits keep auto_value, write history (previous/value/edited_by/edited_at); null value resets to the auto value. No mandatory audit is listed for this action.
- POST /v1/reports/{report_id}/approve: ORG_MEMBER 403; ORG_ADMIN 200 with snapshot_sha256, snapshot stored in the EVIDENCE bucket, cited assets PROTECTED, older APPROVED/SUBMITTED versions moved to SUPERSEDED; second approve 409 INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION; 200/409 bodies match the contract; REPORT_APPROVED audit written
- POST /v1/reports/{report_id}/submit: DRAFT 409 INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION; damaged APPROVED version 422 RULE_VIOLATION with unmet_conditions (matches the contract); clean 200 SUBMITTED; sharing_scope untouched; REPORT_SUBMITTED audit written
- GET /v1/metric/trace (GET /v1/reports/{report_id}/metrics/{metric_code}/trace): viewer 200 on a submitted version, shape matches MetricTrace; unknown metric 404; version=2^40 gives 400; hidden asset IDs filtered from sources/events/originals (ADR-0047)
- GET /v1/reports/{report_id}: version=0 and 2^40 give 400; missing version 404; viewer gets 404 for a draft-only report; viewer gets 200 with the shape matching once submitted; cross-org 404 plus CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT
- POST /v1/reports/{report_id}/versions: 201 shape matches; operator/viewer 403; 409 only when the template is missing (documented). Mismatch is only the period_to finding above
- GET /v1/reports/{report_id}/export: XLSX of a DRAFT carries 초안; version=2^40 gives 400; REPORT_EXPORTED audit written. Mismatches are only the three export findings above

**발견 담당의 메모 (발견으로 올리지 않은 것)**

> Probe file: C:/Users/user/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/69809d01a353b176/ef4ed640-70bd-41cd-a22c-ed40e2ba8d1d/scratchpad/night/wt/rv/backend/tests/integration/test_zz_rpb_a.py (5 tests). They are expected to fail on the current code, except test_flow_contract_shapes, which passes and is an observation run. Run them with: bash $N/bin/odrun.sh rv "python -m pytest -q -p no:cacheprovider -s tests/integration/test_zz_rpb_a.py". Approve tests need ictx, because FakeStorage in ctx has no put_object and approve then returns 500 there. That is a fixture limitation, not a finding.
> 
> Not reported, with reasons:
> (1) Operator reads other organizations' report drafts without break-glass or audit: already awaiting an owner decision (HANDOFF 5장).
> (2) REPORT_APPROVED, REPORT_SUBMITTED and REPORT_EXPORTED audits use a separate connection and can survive a failed commit: accepted cost in ADR-0053.
> (3) POST /versions and PATCH /fields write no audit row: security.md's mandatory list does not include them, and the field history is kept in the version.
> (4) _owned's 403 for a different-org writer is unreachable, because ORG_ADMIN/ORG_MEMBER scope is the own org, so 404 comes first.
> (5) A path metric_code that breaks the TemplateCode pattern gives 404, not 400. This path-pattern behaviour is the same API-wide and was treated as a nit.
> (6) When a newer DRAFT exists, submit acts on the latest version, so an older APPROVED version can no longer be submitted (409). The contract does not say which version submit uses, so this is left as a possible product question, not a finding.
> (7) trace hidden_count also counts deleted (not just out-of-scope) cited assets. Minor wording, not reported.
> (8) Replacing a template version's HWPX (PUT .../hwpx) changes the HWPX exports of already-approved reports. That belongs to the PUT operation, which is outside this group; worth a look by whoever owns report-templates.
> 
> All findings modify only behaviour, so no contract change is needed, except if the HWPX fix chooses to require {{report.status}} at PUT .../hwpx. That would be contract_changed for the PUT.

## records (회차 기록) — 발견 4건

### S39. [medium] PATCH /v1/records/{record_id} — wrong-status

**PATCH with evidence_refs: null returns 500 instead of 400**

- **계약:** contracts/openapi.yaml:5600-5611 SessionRecordUpdate: evidence_refs is {type: array, maxItems: 500} and is not nullable (unlike test_item/conditions/result/note, which are [string,'null']). updateSessionRecord (openapi.yaml:502-523) documents 400 BadRequest 'input format error (VALIDATION_ERROR)'. Bad but parseable input must not give 500.
- **구현:** RecordUpdate.evidence_refs is `list[Ref] | None` (backend/app/api/reports.py:94), so an explicit null passes validation. update_record copies `fields = body.model_dump(exclude_unset=True)` into values (reports.py:167,175), so values['evidence_refs'] becomes None. The `if body.evidence_refs is not None` guard (reports.py:176) skips the replacement, and `_check(db, org, None)` then runs `enumerate(None)` in evidence.check_refs (backend/app/services/evidence.py:47). The TypeError goes to the generic handler and comes back as 500 INTERNAL.
- **근거:** backend/app/api/reports.py:94, 167-178; backend/app/services/evidence.py:47; backend/app/errors.py:162-166 (unhandled -> INTERNAL)
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_rec_a.py::test_patch_evidence_refs_null — PATCH_NULL_REFS 500 {"code":"INTERNAL",...}; server log: TypeError: 'NoneType' object is not iterable; assert 500 == 400
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I could not refute this finding. The contract (contracts/openapi.yaml, SessionRecordUpdate) declares evidence_refs as {type: array, maxItems: 500} with no 'null', while the text fields beside it are [string,'null']. updateSessionRecord documents 400 BadRequest. The implementation accepts the null: RecordUpdate.evidence_refs is `list[Ref] | None` (backend/app/api/reports.py:94). update_record runs values.update(fields) with fields = model_dump(exclude_unset=True), which puts None into values['evidence_refs']. The `if body.evidence_refs is not None` guard skips the replacement, and _check (reports.py:178 -> 103) calls evidence.check_refs, where `enumerate(refs)` at backend/app/services/evidence.py:47 raises TypeError. The generic Exception handler (backend/app/errors.py:162-166) turns that into 500 INTERNAL. The null check at reports.py:170 only covers title and kind. I re-ran the finder's probe tests/integration/test_zz_rec_a.py::test_patch_evidence_refs_null in od-rv-py on the current checkout. It fails, and the traceback ends in `TypeError: 'NoneType' object is not iterable` at evidence.py:47. The probe is valid: user 102 is a writer in the same org as SES_A, the POST creating the record succeeds, and the only failure is the PATCH. This is not recorded as fixed, known or awaiting a decision. The 2026-11-05 sweep lists this bundle under '점검하지 못한 묶음'. Its repro file docs/review-2026-11-05/repro/contract-sweep/reports-records-contracts-uploads/test_sweep_rrcu.py (case A) probed this exact case but never recorded a result. The same 2026-11-05 doc fixed sibling explicit-null cases with 400 VALIDATION_ERROR (#7 event ended_at, #4 external source), so fix_impl is consistent with precedent. Medium severity is correct: a client gets a 500 for bad-but-parseable input. Nothing is lost or leaked, because the exception rolls back the request before add_version.
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I reproduced it on my own and the finding holds. The contract (contracts/openapi.yaml, SessionRecordUpdate) declares evidence_refs as `{type: array, maxItems: 500}` with no null allowed, unlike test_item, conditions, result and note, which are [string,'null']. updateSessionRecord documents 400 for format errors.

In the code, RecordUpdate.evidence_refs is `list[Ref] | None` (backend/app/api/reports.py:94), so an explicit null passes validation. `values.update(fields)` (reports.py:175) then sets values['evidence_refs'] to None. The guard `if body.evidence_refs is not None` (reports.py:176) skips the replacement, and `_check` reaches `enumerate(refs)` with None (backend/app/services/evidence.py:47). The TypeError goes to the generic handler (backend/app/errors.py:162-166), which returns INTERNAL.

My probe ran with controls on the same user (102, who can create and patch) and the same record:
- control PATCH {note} → 200
- PATCH {evidence_refs: "x"} → 400 VALIDATION_ERROR
- PATCH {evidence_refs: null} → 500 INTERNAL, server log `TypeError: 'NoneType' object is not iterable`
- versions afterwards = 2 (create + note patch), so the failed request added no version

So the 500 comes from the null itself, not from the wrong role, a missing record or stale state. The transaction rolls back, so no data is lost or corrupted. That fits medium: bad but parseable input gives 500, which a client can hit.

This is not already recorded. docs/review-2026-11-05/contract-sweep-findings.md lists the reports·records·contracts·uploads bundle under "점검하지 못한 묶음". An old scratch probe for this exact case exists in docs/review-2026-11-05/repro/contract-sweep/reports-records-contracts-uploads/test_sweep_rrcu.py (section A), but no finding came of it. The contract is correct, so the side is fix_impl.
- **고칠 쪽:** 구현 — In update_record, reject an explicit null: `if "evidence_refs" in fields and fields["evidence_refs"] is None: raise _bad("/evidence_refs", "근거 목록은 null일 수 없습니다", "type")`. Alternatively, add a field_validator on RecordUpdate.evidence_refs that rejects None while the field stays optional. Regression test: PATCH {evidence_refs: null} -> 400 with pointer /evidence_refs.

### S40. [medium] POST /v1/sessions/{session_id}/records (same code path: PATCH /v1/records/{record_id}) — wrong-status

**OBSERVATION_RANGE evidence ref with a well-formed but impossible timestamp gives 500**

- **계약:** EvidenceRef OBSERVATION_RANGE from/to reference common.schema.json#/$defs/utc_datetime (openapi.yaml:5584-5585; contracts/schemas/common.schema.json:136-141), which has 'format: date-time' and the RFC 3339 UTC pattern, so '2026-02-30T00:00:00Z' or '2026-10-01T25:00:00Z' is invalid input. createSessionRecord and updateSessionRecord document 400 VALIDATION_ERROR for invalid input (openapi.yaml:472, 519).
- **구현:** ObsRef only checks the regex pattern (backend/app/api/reports.py:68-69). evidence.check_refs then calls parse_rfc3339(r['from']) and parse_rfc3339(r['to']) (backend/app/services/evidence.py:66) outside any try. parse_rfc3339 raises TimeRuleError, a ValueError subclass (backend/app/domain/normalize.py:39-42). No handler maps it, so the request fails with 500 INTERNAL. POST and PATCH both reach this through _check (reports.py:101-105), which catches only BadEvidence.
- **근거:** backend/app/api/reports.py:68-69, 101-105, 140, 178; backend/app/services/evidence.py:66; backend/app/domain/normalize.py:39-42; backend/app/errors.py:162-166
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_rec_a.py::test_post_obs_range_invalid_calendar_date and ::test_patch_obs_range_invalid_calendar_date — POST_BAD_DATE 500 INTERNAL (TimeRuleError: 시각 형식이 올바르지 않습니다: '2026-02-30T00:00:00Z'); PATCH_BAD_DATE 500 INTERNAL (hour must be in 0..23, '2026-10-01T25:00:00Z'); assert 500 == 400
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I could not refute this. The contract and the current code both say what the finding says, and the finder's probe fails for the reason it claims. Contract: the EvidenceRef OBSERVATION_RANGE fields from/to point to common.schema.json#/$defs/utc_datetime (openapi.yaml:5584-5585), and that schema has format: date-time plus the UTC pattern (common.schema.json:136-141). Both operations document 400 BadRequest (openapi.yaml:472 and 519). Implementation: ObsRef only checks the pattern (backend/app/api/reports.py:68-69, UTC_TS at :39). check_refs calls parse_rfc3339(r["from"]) and parse_rfc3339(r["to"]) with no try (backend/app/services/evidence.py:66). parse_rfc3339 raises TimeRuleError, a ValueError subclass (backend/app/domain/normalize.py:25, 39-42). _check catches only BadEvidence (reports.py:101-105). errors.py has handlers only for ProblemError, RequestValidationError, RuleViolation, InvalidStateTransition, HTTPException and DBAPIError, then the catch-all Exception handler, which returns INTERNAL (errors.py:163-166). I re-ran the finder's two tests in od-rv-py and both failed with 500. The traceback ends in evidence.py:66 -> normalize.py:42 TimeRuleError for '2026-10-01T25:00:00Z'. The probe tests what it claims: it uses user 102 on their own organisation's session SES_A and the device DEV_A_SENSOR, so a wrong role or scope is not the cause. This is not a known or fixed item. The 2026-11-12 review row V3 and ADR-0056 fixed the same symptom only in the jsonschema contract validator (contracts.validate_document). The records routes use pydantic models instead, so that fix does not cover them. Earlier sweep findings #7 and #8 (docs/review-2026-11-05/contract-sweep-findings.md) were the same 500 on other routes (PATCH /v1/events, POST /v1/observations/corrections), and corrections now uses _validate_utc_timestamp (backend/app/api/observations.py:42-49, 229). The records bundle was never checked in that sweep. The code itself shows this is an oversight: POST /v1/reports in the same file wraps parse_rfc3339 in try/except ValueError and returns 400 (reports.py:428-431). Nothing in domain.md or the ADRs makes a 500 intended. Severity medium matches the earlier findings #7 and #8. fix_impl is the right side because the contract text is correct.
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I reproduced it with my own probe. As user 102 (record:write, same org as SES_A), a control POST with a valid OBSERVATION_RANGE ref on DEV_A_SENSOR returns 201. That rules out the role, fixture and device as causes. Then every value that matches the pattern but is not a real time returns 500 INTERNAL: '2026-02-30T00:00:00Z' in from, '2026-10-01T25:00:00Z' in to, and '2026-13-01T00:00:00Z' all fail on POST, and PATCH /v1/records/{id} with hour 25 fails the same way. The server log shows TimeRuleError ('day is out of range for month', 'hour must be in 0..23', 'month must be in 1..12'), raised by parse_rfc3339 (backend/app/domain/normalize.py:39-46). The cause is in three places. ObsRef checks only the UTC_TS regex (backend/app/api/reports.py:39, 68-69). check_refs calls parse_rfc3339(r['from']) and parse_rfc3339(r['to']) outside any try (backend/app/services/evidence.py:66). _check catches only BadEvidence (reports.py:101-105). backend/app/errors.py:108-166 has no handler for TimeRuleError/ValueError, so the catch-all Exception handler returns INTERNAL. The parse also runs before the device check, so an unknown device id with a bad date also gives 500, not the 400 not_found that check_refs would otherwise give. The contract treats these values as invalid input. It uses common.schema.json utc_datetime (format date-time; ADR-0056 says date-time means a value parse_rfc3339 accepts) and documents 400 VALIDATION_ERROR for createSessionRecord and updateSessionRecord. This was not recorded before: the reports·records·contracts·uploads bundle is listed as unchecked in docs/review-2026-11-05/contract-sweep-findings.md:297-299. Its findings at lines 97 and 104 are the same pattern on other routes (EventPatch.ended_at, Correction.observed_at), filed as wrong-status, which supports medium and fix_impl. The finder's probe tests what it claims (right user, seeded device, only the timestamp changed).
- **고칠 쪽:** 구현 — In check_refs, wrap parse_rfc3339 in try/except TimeRuleError and add a 400 error {pointer: f"{ptr}/from" or "/to", code: "format"}. Alternatively, add a field_validator on ObsRef.from_/to that calls parse_rfc3339 and raises ValueError, so FastAPI returns 400 with the exact pointer. Add regression tests for POST and PATCH.

### S41. [medium] POST /v1/sessions/{session_id}/records (same code path: PATCH /v1/records/{record_id}) — missing-audit

**Evidence refs to another organisation's event/device are refused without a CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT audit**

- **계약:** security.md:26 lists '교차 기관 접근 시도' (cross-org access attempts) among audit entries that must always be recorded. security.md:10 says another organisation's resource is hidden (404) and recorded in the audit log. The project rule in SWEEP.md is 404 plus a CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT audit. 10 design.md:6 says other-org evidence answers with the same error as 'not found' (existence hidden). It does not exempt this path from the audit. Comparable body-referenced IDs do write the audit, e.g. POST /v1/events linked_asset_ids via load_scoped (backend/app/api/observations.py:347-349) and observation batch source IDs (2026-11-05 sweep #10).
- **구현:** evidence.check_refs compares organization_id itself (backend/app/services/evidence.py:51, 60, 67) and returns a 400 not_found error for a foreign asset, event or device. It writes no audit row. The record path itself (session of org B) correctly gives 404 with one CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT. A member can therefore probe other organisations' event_id/device_id/asset_id values through evidence_refs and leave no audit trail.
- **근거:** backend/app/services/evidence.py:41-79 (no audit), backend/app/api/reports.py:101-105, 140, 178; compare backend/app/api/deps.py:111-123 and backend/app/api/observations.py:347-349
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_rec_a.py::test_cross_org_evidence_ref_audit — CROSS_ORG {"obs": [400, [{pointer:/evidence_refs/0, code:not_found, 장비를 찾을 수 없습니다}], 0, 0], "event": [400, [{... 이벤트를 찾을 수 없습니다}], 0, 0], "path_session_b": [404, 0, 1]}; assert 0 == (0 + 1)
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I could not refute it, and the side (fix_impl) and severity (medium) are right. (1) The rule is clear. .kiro/steering/security.md:10 says another organisation's resource gets a 404 and is written to the audit log. security.md:27 (the finding cites :26, which is the section heading) puts '교차 기관 접근 시도' under '반드시 기록'. .kiro/specs/02-identity-tenancy/requirements.md:35 (req 2.2) and design.md:31 ask for 404 plus CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT. (2) Nothing exempts this path. 10-evidence-reports/design.md:6 only fixes the response: other-org evidence gets the same error as 'not found', and it says nothing about the audit. 10 requirements 1.2 only says to check that evidence is from the same org. The contract (openapi.yaml:431-518, createSessionRecord / updateSessionRecord) is silent. HANDOFF, docs/review-2026-11-05/contract-sweep-findings.md and docs/review-2026-11-12/README.md do not record this as known, fixed or decided. The 2026-11-05 sweep wrote a probe for exactly this case (repro/contract-sweep/reports-records-contracts-uploads/test_sweep_rrcu.py:138-146, 'H_cross_org_evidence'), but that bundle stopped before any finding was written (contract-sweep-findings.md:297-300). (3) The code does what the finding says. backend/app/services/evidence.py:50-52, 59-61 and 65-68 compare organization_id directly and append a not_found error. No audit is called. backend/app/api/reports.py:101-105 turns that into a 400 VALIDATION_ERROR, used at :140 for create and :178 for patch. (4) The project already treats IDs given in a request body as access attempts. POST /v1/events linked_asset_ids goes through load_scoped (backend/app/api/observations.py:347-349, which audits at deps.py:116-119). Observation batch source IDs were fixed to audit (sweep #10, contract-sweep-findings.md:31). Existing tests pin the 400 not_found (tests/acceptance/test_t11_reports.py:189-191) and the path-level audit (tests/integration/test_reports_api.py:68-75), but no test pins the missing audit. (5) I read the finder's probe (test_zz_rec_a.py::test_cross_org_evidence_ref_audit) and it is sound: user 102 (org A member, holds record:write) sends refs to DEV_B_CAMERA and to an event created by 104 in SES_B. It counts CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT before and after, and compares with the path-level 404, which does audit.
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — My own probe reproduced the finding, and nothing documents it as known or decided. backend/app/services/evidence.py:41-79 (check_refs) compares organization_id itself at lines 51, 60 and 67 and raises BadEvidence, which reports.py `_check` turns into a 400 VALIDATION_ERROR. Neither function writes an audit row. My probe tests/integration/test_zz_recv2x_a.py::test_cross_org_ref_unaudited ran as ORG_MEMBER 102 of org A against org A's own session SES_A, so role, scope and session are all valid; the control with the member's own device DEV_A_SENSOR returned 201. Observed: POST with org B's DEV_B_CAMERA gave 400 not_found '장비를 찾을 수 없습니다' and 0 new CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT rows. PATCH /v1/records/{id} with the same ref gave 400 and 0 rows. A device ID that does not exist (dev 999) gave the identical 400 body, so existence hiding works, but the audit is missing. As a control, GET /v1/devices/DEV_B_CAMERA by the same user gave 404 and 1 CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT. On the contract side, security.md:10 says a request for another organisation's resource is answered 404 to hide existence and recorded in the audit log, and security.md:26 lists '교차 기관 접근 시도' (cross-org access attempts) among audit entries that must always be recorded. 10 design.md:6 only requires the response to be the same as 'not found'; it does not exempt this path from the audit. Nothing in review-2026-11-05/contract-sweep-findings.md, review-2026-11-12/README.md, HANDOFF.md or the ADRs records it. The 2026-11-05 repro file docs/review-2026-11-05/repro/contract-sweep/reports-records-contracts-uploads/test_sweep_rrcu.py (case H) probed the same thing, but that bundle was never reported ('점검하지 못한 묶음', the bundles that were not checked). Severity medium: a missing audit an auditor would hit. Side fix_impl.
- **고칠 쪽:** 구현 — Keep the existence-hiding 400 not_found response. When the referenced row exists but belongs to another organisation, also record CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT (result DENIED, organization_id = owner org, target_type Asset/Event/Device, target_id = referenced id, details.path). For example, have check_refs return the foreign hits, or pass c/principal/request into _check and audit there, the same way load_scoped does. If the owner wants evidence refs exempt, record that decision in design.md instead.

### S42. [low] PATCH /v1/records/{record_id} — data-semantics

**PATCH {kind: null} reports the error at pointer /title**

- **계약:** SessionRecordUpdate.kind is {enum: [LOG, TEST, NOTE]} and not nullable (openapi.yaml:5605). The 400 BadRequest problem carries errors[].pointer for the offending field (api-conventions VALIDATION_ERROR with field pointers; openapi.yaml:4716-4725).
- **구현:** `if fields.get("title", "") is None or fields.get("kind", "") is None: raise _bad("/title", ...)` (backend/app/api/reports.py:170-171). A null kind is rejected with status 400, but the pointer names /title, so a client highlights the wrong field.
- **근거:** backend/app/api/reports.py:170-171
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_rec_a.py::test_misc_semantics (prints MISC.patch_kind_null) — "patch_kind_null": [400, [{"pointer": "/title", "code": "invalid", "message": "제목·종류는 비울 수 없습니다"}]]
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I could not refute it. Contract: SessionRecordUpdate.kind is `{enum: [LOG, TEST, NOTE]}` with no 'null' type (contracts/openapi.yaml:5605), while the nullable fields say `type: [string, 'null']` (5607-5610). So `kind: null` is invalid input and should give 400 VALIDATION_ERROR. api-conventions.md:34 says VALIDATION_ERROR carries `errors` with a reason for each JSON Pointer, and problem.schema.json:20 defines `pointer` as an RFC 6901 JSON Pointer. Implementation: the pydantic model accepts None for kind (`kind: Literal[...] | None = None`, backend/app/api/reports.py:88), so validation passes and the request reaches the combined check `if fields.get("title", "") is None or fields.get("kind", "") is None: raise _bad("/title", ...)` (reports.py:170-171). A null kind therefore comes back with pointer /title. Status 400 is right; only the pointer is wrong. The finder's repro output matches this code path exactly. I found no mention in any .md file (review docs, HANDOFF, ADRs, steering), so it is not already known or decided. The neighbouring PATCH /v1/sessions/{session_id} returns a pointer for each field (tests/integration/test_session_edit.py:53, e.g. `{"starts_at": None}` gives /starts_at), so per-field pointers are the established convention and the contract side is right. Low severity fits: the status is correct and only the field shown to the client is wrong.
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I reproduced it with my own probe. On current origin/main (wt/rv), PATCH /v1/records/{id} with body {"kind": null} returns 400 VALIDATION_ERROR with errors[].pointer ["/title"]. A wrong kind value goes through pydantic, so {"kind": "XXX"} correctly returns pointer ["/kind"]. That makes the null case inconsistent within the same endpoint. The cause is backend/app/api/reports.py:170-171, where `if fields.get("title", "") is None or fields.get("kind", "") is None: raise _bad("/title", "제목·종류는 비울 수 없습니다")` always uses the title pointer. The null passes pydantic because RecordUpdate.kind is typed `Literal[...] | None` (reports.py:88). In the contract, SessionRecordUpdate.kind is `{enum: [LOG, TEST, NOTE]}` and not nullable (openapi.yaml:5605), and VALIDATION_ERROR carries field pointers. The status is right (400). Only the pointer is wrong, and the record is not changed (it stays at version 1, kind LOG). The finder's probe tests what it claims: role 102 (member, record:write), a fresh record, and a direct PATCH. This is not listed in docs/review-2026-11-05, docs/review-2026-11-12 or HANDOFF. No existing test pins the "/title" pointer for kind null (grep found only unrelated /title pointer tests in test_session_edit.py and test_system.py). The severity (low) and side (fix_impl) are correct.
- **고칠 쪽:** 구현 — Check the two fields separately and use the matching pointer, '/kind' for kind and '/title' for title, for example by looping over ("title", "kind").

**일치를 확인한 작업**

- GET /v1/sessions/{session_id}/records — latest version per record, record:read roles (PROVIDER+operator, viewer 403), unknown/other-org session 404 + CROSS_ORG audit via load_scoped, response matches contract
- GET /v1/records/{record_id} — latest by default, ?version=N returns that version, version=0/abc -> 400 VALIDATION_ERROR, nonexistent version -> 404, viewer 403, operator 200, other-org 404 (existing test), response matches contract
- GET /v1/records/{record_id}/versions — all versions oldest first, latest_version filled, response matches contract
- POST /v1/records/{record_id}/approve — ORG_ADMIN only (member/operator 403), other-org admin 404 + 1 CROSS_ORG audit, approves latest version with approved_by/approved_at, second approve 409 CONFLICT, one EVIDENCE_APPROVED audit, cited assets protected via evidence_citations of that version, response matches contract
- POST /v1/sessions/{session_id}/records — apart from the two findings above: Idempotency-Key replay (201 + Idempotent-Replayed: true, same record), same key different body 409 IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT, key shorter than 8 -> 400, other-org session 404 + audit, response matches contract
- PATCH /v1/records/{record_id} — apart from the findings above: always a new DRAFT version (also after approval; approved v2 kept APPROVED), explicit null clears nullable fields while other fields carry over, {} -> 400, unknown field -> 400, response matches contract

**발견 담당의 메모 (발견으로 올리지 않은 것)**

> The probe file is at C:/Users/user/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/69809d01a353b176/ef4ed640-70bd-41cd-a22c-ed40e2ba8d1d/scratchpad/night/wt/rv/backend/tests/integration/test_zz_rec_a.py. To run it: bash "$N/bin/odrun.sh" rv "python -m pytest -q -p no:cacheprovider -s tests/integration/test_zz_rec_a.py". Four tests fail on purpose, one per reproduced finding; test_misc_semantics passes and only prints the observed values. Notes are in var/sweep/rec/notes.txt. The 2026-11-05 repro file docs/review-2026-11-05/repro/contract-sweep/reports-records-contracts-uploads/test_sweep_rrcu.py already probed cases A (PATCH evidence_refs null) and H (cross-org evidence ref audit), but its results were never recorded in any review document. So these are not already-known items. Not reported on purpose: (1) EVIDENCE_APPROVED is written through a separate audit connection, not connection=db.connection(). ADR-0053 accepts this pattern project-wide, and approve_report does the same. (2) Operator reads of other organisations' records are not audited. The 2026-11-05 sweep already noted this, and HANDOFF 5 has the operator-read-scope decision pending. (3) PATCH and approve take no Idempotency-Key, although api-conventions.md:55 says write requests accept one. The contract and the code agree, so it is not a contract-vs-implementation mismatch; it is a possible conventions gap for the owner. (4) SessionRecordCreate optional strings (test_item etc.) are typed `string` in the contract, but the implementation accepts explicit null on POST. This is lenient acceptance that nobody can observe as harm, so it is left out. (5) A PATCH that changes only note re-validates the existing evidence_refs. If a cited asset was deleted since, the PATCH fails 400 not_found at /evidence_refs/i. This matches the damage notification text ('근거를 바꾼 새 버전을 만드세요'), so it is not reported. Record approve uses CONFLICT, not INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION. That is consistent, because domain.md has no record-status state table (its DRAFT->APPROVED row is report_status).

## contracts (스키마·코드표·사전 검증) — 발견 2건

### S43. [medium] POST /v1/contracts/validate — missing-behavior

**사전 검증이 코드표 검사(이벤트 상위분류·기관 하위코드·관측 metric)를 하지 않아 운영 경로가 거부하는 문서를 valid:true로 답한다**

- **계약:** openapi.yaml:1535-1555 validateContract summary: "저장 없이 매니페스트·관측값·이벤트를 계약으로 검사". 200: "검사 결과 (문서가 틀려도 200, 결과에 문제 목록)". The contract is the code tables as well as the schemas (contracts/codes/*). Spec 03 requirement 13.1 says the validator checks "스키마·코드표·도메인 규칙", and 13.2 says it uses the same validation code as the production path. ACCEPTANCE T05-f expects "운영 경로와 같은 결과". The event schema description (event.schema.json:4) says event_code is a common top-level category plus an optional org subcode.
- **구현:** validate_contract calls only contracts.validate_document. For the event contract that checks only the code_ref pattern; for observation batches it checks only the unit dictionary (as a WARNING). The production paths do more. POST /v1/events runs _check_code: an unknown top-level category or an unregistered org subcode gets 422 RULE_VIOLATION. POST /v1/observations passes the metrics table to normalize, so an unknown metric is REJECTED with UNKNOWN_METRIC. So the validator answers valid:true with problems:[] for documents the real endpoint rejects. This includes the published contract example event.manual-safety.json (event_code SAFETY_INCIDENT.near_miss). The validator also ignores the principal, so it cannot apply the caller's own org subcodes.
- **근거:** backend/app/api/catalog.py:96-104 (validate_contract; principal unused); backend/app/services/contracts.py:304-331 (validate_document: schema + location + _observation_problems, no event-category or metric check), 287-289 (only unit lookup); backend/app/api/observations.py:336-344 (_check_code), 368; backend/app/services/observations.py:51-52, 87-99 and backend/app/domain/observations.py:83 (UNKNOWN_METRIC); .kiro/specs/03-metadata-standard/requirements.md:123-124; docs/ACCEPTANCE.md:45
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_ctr_a.py::test_event_unknown_category, ::test_event_unregistered_subcode, ::test_observation_unknown_metric — event NOPE_CATEGORY: validate -> {'valid': True, 'problems': []}; POST /v1/events -> 422 RULE_VIOLATION "공통 상위분류에 없는 이벤트 코드입니다". event SAFETY_INCIDENT.near_miss: validate -> valid True, []; POST /v1/events -> 422 RULE_VIOLATION "기관 하위코드가 등록되어 있지 않습니다". observation metric no_such_metric (DEV_A_SENSOR, SES_A): validate -> valid True, []; POST /v1/observations -> 200 results [{index 0, REJECTED, UNKNOWN_METRIC}].
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I could not refute this. The contract and spec do promise code-table checks. openapi.yaml:1539 summary is "계약으로 검사". Spec 03 requirement 13.1 (.kiro/specs/03-metadata-standard/requirements.md:123) names "스키마·코드표·도메인 규칙", and 13.2 says the validator uses the same validation code as the production path. ACCEPTANCE T05-f expects "운영 경로와 같은 결과", and event.schema.json:4 ties event_code to contracts/codes/event-categories.json. On the code side, backend/app/api/catalog.py:90-98 calls only contracts.validate_document, and the principal is discarded as `_`. validate_document (services/contracts.py:304-331) runs the schema check (event_code is only common code_ref), the location check and _observation_problems. _observation_problems only looks up units, as a WARNING, and never checks the metric. The production paths do check codes: api/observations.py:336-344 _check_code returns 422 RULE_VIOLATION for an unknown top-level category or an unregistered org subcode, and services/observations.py:51-52/87-99 plus domain/observations.py:83 reject an unknown metric with UNKNOWN_METRIC. I ran the finder's three probes in od-rv-py and all three passed, which confirms the mismatch. NOPE_CATEGORY and SAFETY_INCIDENT.near_miss both get validate {'valid': True, 'problems': []}, while POST /v1/events returns 422 RULE_VIOLATION. Metric no_such_metric gets validate valid:true, while POST /v1/observations returns 200 with [{index 0, REJECTED, UNKNOWN_METRIC}]. Nothing in docs/review-2026-11-05, docs/review-2026-11-12/README.md, HANDOFF.md or ADR-0056 records this as known or decided. ADR-0056:9/47 actually claims the validator predicts real results. Severity medium and side fix_impl are right. One caveat: the finding uses the published example event.manual-safety.json (SAFETY_INCIDENT.near_miss) as evidence, but that is the weakest part. The example is meant for an org that has registered that subcode, so it being rejected for the seed org is expected. The unknown top-level category and unknown metric cases are stateless code-table gaps and settle the finding on their own.
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I reproduced it with my own probe, using control cases, user 102 and the ctx fixture. Event VESSEL is the control: validate gives valid:true and POST /v1/events gives 201, so the role, session and fixture are fine. Event NOPE_CATEGORY: validate gives 200 {'valid': True, 'problems': []}, but POST /v1/events gives 422 RULE_VIOLATION "공통 상위분류에 없는 이벤트 코드입니다". Event SAFETY_INCIDENT.near_miss, the code in the published example contracts/examples/event.manual-safety.json: validate gives valid:true with [], but POST /v1/events gives 422 RULE_VIOLATION "기관 하위코드가 등록되어 있지 않습니다". Observation sea_water_temperature/degC is the control: validate is valid and ingest gives ACCEPTED. Observation no_such_metric: validate gives valid:true with [], but POST /v1/observations gives 200 with results [{index 0, REJECTED, UNKNOWN_METRIC}].

The code matches this. backend/app/api/catalog.py:90-98: validate_contract calls only contracts.validate_document and ignores the principal. backend/app/services/contracts.py:304-331: validate_document checks the schema, the location and _observation_problems. The only code-table lookup is the unit check at 287-289, as a WARNING. There is no event-category, subcode or metric check. The production paths add more: api/observations.py:336-344 (_check_code, called at 368) and services/observations.py:51-52, 87-99 (metrics_table, then normalize, then UNKNOWN_METRIC).

The contract and spec say the validator should do this. Spec 03 requirement 13.1 says it checks "스키마·코드표·도메인 규칙", and 13.2 says it uses the same validation code as production. ACCEPTANCE T05-f expects "운영 경로와 같은 결과". ADR-0056 line 47 says the validator predicts the real result.

It is not already recorded. Neither HANDOFF, review-2026-11-05/12 nor ADR-0056 records this gap. ADR-0056 covers hardening, not code-table checks. No existing test pins valid:true for unknown codes: test_contract_validator_api.py only uses a known metric. Severity medium and side fix_impl both look right.
- **고칠 쪽:** 구현 — Move the code-table checks into a shared function and call it from both paths. Event: check the top-level category, and check the org subcode against OrgEventCode for the principal's organization; validate_contract would need db and the principal. Observation items: check the metric against the metrics table. Return each failure as a Problem with severity ERROR, pointer /event_code or /observations/i/metric, and the same code names the production path uses. POST /v1/events and normalize keep their current statuses. Optionally make the contract summary say that code tables are checked too, e.g. "계약(스키마·코드표·도메인 규칙)으로 검사"; that is a wording-only contract change.

### S44. [medium] POST /v1/contracts/validate — missing-behavior

**사전 검증이 시각 순서 도메인 규칙(이벤트 ended_at<started_at, 매니페스트 capture_end_at<capture_start_at)을 검사하지 않는다**

- **계약:** openapi.yaml:1535-1555: "저장 없이 매니페스트·관측값·이벤트를 계약으로 검사", and the 200 result lists the document's problems. Spec 03 requirement 13.1 says the validator checks domain rules ("도메인 규칙(위경도 순서, 시간대, 결측 표기 등)"), and 13.2 says the validation code is the same as the production path's. ACCEPTANCE T05-f: "운영 경로와 같은 결과".
- **구현:** Both rules are pure document checks with no DB state, but they live only in the production handlers, outside validate_document. POST /v1/events returns 400 VALIDATION_ERROR (/ended_at, before_start) when ended_at < started_at. POST /v1/uploads returns 400 VALIDATION_ERROR (/capture_start_at, time_rule) through resolve_capture_time when capture_end_at < capture_start_at. For the same documents /v1/contracts/validate returns valid:true with problems:[].
- **근거:** backend/app/api/observations.py:369-373 (before_start check after validate_document); backend/app/services/ingest.py:211-218 and backend/app/domain/normalize.py:65-68 (time_rule); backend/app/services/contracts.py:304-331 (no time-order check); backend/app/api/catalog.py:96-104
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_ctr_a.py::test_event_end_before_start, ::test_manifest_end_before_start — event ended_at 1 day before started_at: validate -> {'valid': True, 'problems': []}; POST /v1/events -> 400 VALIDATION_ERROR errors [{pointer /ended_at, code before_start}]. upload-manifest.vessel-video.json with capture_end_at=2020-01-01T00:00:00Z: validate -> valid True, []; POST /v1/uploads -> 400 VALIDATION_ERROR errors [{pointer /capture_start_at, code time_rule, message capture_end_at이 capture_start_at보다 빠릅니다}].
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I could not refute it. The contract promises this, the code behaves as described, and nothing records it as known.
- Contract: openapi.yaml:1535-1555 says validate checks the document "저장 없이" and lists its problems. Spec 03 requirements 13.1 and 13.2 (.kiro/specs/03-metadata-standard/requirements.md:119-124) say the validator checks domain rules including time ("시간대"), using the same code as the production path. ACCEPTANCE T05-f expects "운영 경로와 같은 결과". ADR-0056 line 9 and line 47 say the same.
- Code: catalog.py:91-99 returns only what contracts.validate_document gives. validate_document (services/contracts.py:297-331) runs the schema checks, _location_problems and _observation_problems, and has no time-order check. The event schema (event.schema.json:23-24) only checks the format of started_at and ended_at.
- Production paths: create_event (api/observations.py:367-371) rejects ended_at < started_at with 400 /ended_at before_start, after validate_document has run. IngestService.create_upload (services/ingest.py:211-218) calls resolve_capture_time (domain/normalize.py:52-68), which raises TimeRuleError when end < start and the source is an evidence source. That becomes 400 /capture_start_at time_rule.
- Both rules need no database state, so validate could check them.
- Not already known: I searched HANDOFF section 5, docs/review-2026-11-05 (README and contract-sweep-findings), docs/review-2026-11-12/README.md and ADR-0056. The open items ADR-0056 lists are unevaluatedProperties and observations[].location. None mentions time order in validate.
- Probe: the finder's probe tests what it claims. It uses user 102, the seeded SES_A, and the contract example manifest, which has an evidence timestamp source, since production returned time_rule. I re-ran test_zz_ctr_a.py with -k end_before_start: 2 passed. Validate returned {'valid': True, 'problems': []}, while /v1/uploads returned 400 VALIDATION_ERROR [{pointer /capture_start_at, code time_rule}], and the event case returned 400 before_start.
- Side and severity: fix_impl is right, because the contract and spec text are correct. Medium is right: a client is told the document is valid and then gets a 400 from the production endpoint.
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — My own probe reproduced the finding. The finder's probe was sound: it used user 102, which has event:write, and the production 400 comes from the time-order check itself, not from scope or permission.

Probe: backend/tests/integration/test_zz_ctrv1x_a.py.
- Control: an event whose ended_at is after started_at gets 201 on POST /v1/events, so the role, session (SES_A) and code VESSEL are all accepted.
- The same event with ended_at 1 hour before started_at:
  - /v1/contracts/validate -> 200 {'valid': True, 'problems': []}
  - POST /v1/events -> 400 VALIDATION_ERROR, errors [{pointer /ended_at, code before_start}]
- Example manifest upload-manifest.vessel-video.json (timestamp_quality.source GPS, so an evidence source):
  - unchanged: validate gives valid:true
  - with capture_end_at=2020-01-01T00:00:00Z: validate -> valid:true, []; POST /v1/uploads -> 400 VALIDATION_ERROR, errors [{pointer /capture_start_at, code time_rule}]

Code:
- backend/app/services/contracts.py:304-331: validate_document has no time-order check.
- backend/app/api/observations.py:369-373: the before_start check runs after validate_document.
- backend/app/services/ingest.py:213-218 and backend/app/domain/normalize.py:65-68: the time_rule check.

Both checks look only at the document, with no DB state. Spec 03 requirement 13.1 lists domain rules including timezone/time checks; 13.2 says the validation code is the same; ACCEPTANCE T05-f says "운영 경로와 같은 결과". The review documents, ADRs and HANDOFF do not record this as known, decided or intended. The severity (medium) and side (fix_impl) are right.
- **고칠 쪽:** 구현 — Add the time-order checks to contracts.validate_document as Problems, only when both times parse: event ended_at < started_at gives /ended_at before_start; manifest capture_end_at < capture_start_at gives /capture_start_at time_rule, applied only when timestamp_quality.source is an evidence source, as in resolve_capture_time. The production handlers then get these from validate_document and keep returning the same 400 bodies. Add unit tests that compare the validate result with the production result for each rule.

**일치를 확인한 작업**

- GET /v1/schemas - schema:read (all user roles, API keys with status:read; devices 403); lists every contracts/schemas file with the single supported version; matches SchemaIndex
- GET /v1/schemas/{name}/{version} - unknown name, unsupported version or a value that breaks the path pattern gives 404 NOT_FOUND (documented); media type application/schema+json; probe test_bundles_validate_examples_standalone: every bundle passes Draft202012Validator.check_schema and validates all valid contract examples standalone with 0 errors (no unresolved external refs), and every invalid example fails
- GET /v1/codes/{table} - the 5 enum tables return {table, version, items}; anything else 404; event-categories appends only the caller org's subcodes with organization_specific:true (existing test_org_event_subcodes covers isolation); the lru_cache copy is not mutated
- POST /v1/codes/event-categories/subcodes - human ORG_ADMIN only (member/viewer/operator/device/API key 403), no MFA required as documented, org comes from the principal (body/header/query org rejected 400), 422 RULE_VIOLATION for an unknown parent, 409 CONFLICT for a duplicate, 409 IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT, replay 201 with Idempotent-Replayed, Idempotency-Key length 8..128 enforced with 400, audit ORG_EVENT_CODE_CREATED written inside the request transaction and not on failure or replay (code reading plus existing tests/integration/test_org_event_subcodes.py)
- POST /v1/contracts/validate - status codes: a contract outside the enum gives 400 VALIDATION_ERROR /contract, extra fields 400, an invalid document gives 200 with problems, unsupported schema_version gives 200 with UNSUPPORTED_SCHEMA_VERSION; result shape matches ValidateResult (the two findings above are about missing checks only)

**발견 담당의 메모 (발견으로 올리지 않은 것)**

> I left the probe file in place for the fixers: C:/Users/user/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/69809d01a353b176/ef4ed640-70bd-41cd-a22c-ed40e2ba8d1d/scratchpad/night/wt/rv/backend/tests/integration/test_zz_ctr_a.py. All 7 probes pass on current origin/main, and each one asserts the mismatching behaviour.
> 
> Not reported:
> - The subcode audit row has target_id None. The contract does not promise a target_id, and the existing test asserts None on purpose.
> - The units and metrics code tables report version "1.0.0-draft". CodeTable.version is a free string, so this does not break the contract.
> - validate does not run the production checks that need state (session/device scope, quota, the human_verified-by-machine 403). Those are not document rules.
> 
> Both findings rest on two sources: the contract summary says the endpoint checks documents against the contract, which includes the code tables in contracts/codes, and spec 03 requirements 13.1 and 13.2 plus ACCEPTANCE T05-f say it checks code tables and domain rules the same way production does.
> 
> A verifier could argue that "계약으로 검사" means JSON Schema only. In that case finding 1 becomes fix_contract (state that code tables and org subcodes are not checked), but that would contradict requirement 13.1.
> 
> Fixing the org-subcode part of finding 1 means validate has to use the DB session and the principal.
> 
> I found nothing in docs/review-2026-11-05, docs/review-2026-11-12/README.md, ADR-0056 or HANDOFF section 5 that already records either finding.

## uploads (업로드 세션) — 발견 3건

### S45. [low] GET /v1/uploads/{upload_id}/parts — wrong-status

**listUploadParts returns an undocumented 503 STORAGE_UNAVAILABLE when the storage part listing fails**

- **계약:** contracts/openapi.yaml:1676-1692 (listUploadParts) lists only 200, 401, 403, 404, 409, 410, 429. Neither the summary ('완료된 파트와 남은 파트의 새 URL (재개용)') nor any response mentions 503. Sibling operations createUpload (1644) and completeUpload (1739) do document 503 ServiceUnavailable.
- **구현:** IngestService.list_parts catches any exception from storage.list_parts and raises ProblemError('STORAGE_UNAVAILABLE', Retry-After: 30), so the client gets 503 with code STORAGE_UNAVAILABLE. The behaviour is reasonable and matches api-conventions (STORAGE_UNAVAILABLE 503 + Retry-After). Only the contract leaves it out.
- **근거:** backend/app/services/ingest.py:456-459; contracts/openapi.yaml:1676-1692; .kiro/steering/api-conventions.md:49
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_upl_a.py::test_list_parts_storage_outage_status — list parts during storage outage -> 503 30 STORAGE_UNAVAILABLE documented: ['200', '401', '403', '404', '409', '410', '429'] (AssertionError: '503' not in documented). The 2026-11-05 sweep probe docs/review-2026-11-05/repro/contract-sweep/reports-records-contracts-uploads/test_sweep_rrcu.py section F tried the same case, but the result was never recorded.
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I could not refute it. Contract: contracts/openapi.yaml:1676-1692 (listUploadParts) lists only 200, 401, 403, 404, 409, 410 and 429, and the operation has no description. The global note at openapi.yaml:13 does not cover this case. It says any operation may return 503 SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE, but only for overload, when the server cannot start DB work. It says nothing about STORAGE_UNAVAILABLE. Sibling operations do document storage 503 in both their responses and their descriptions: createUpload (1605, 1643), completeUpload (1713, 1736) and the deletion operations (3593/3617, 3629/3650). Implementation: route backend/app/api/uploads.py:44-48 calls IngestService.list_parts. At backend/app/services/ingest.py:456-459, any exception from storage.list_parts becomes ProblemError('STORAGE_UNAVAILABLE', Retry-After: 30). By contrast, upload_status (getUpload) swallows the same error and uses an empty list. I re-ran the finder's probe in od-rv-py and got 503, Retry-After 30, code STORAGE_UNAVAILABLE, with documented statuses ['200','401','403','404','409','410','429'] (AssertionError at test_zz_upl_a.py:75). The probe tests what it claims: role 102 on its own org's fresh UPLOADING session, and only list_parts is monkeypatched. The case is not recorded as fixed, known or waiting for a decision. contract-sweep-findings.md:297-300 says the uploads bundle was never checked. Section F of the 2026-11-05 probe (test_sweep_rrcu.py:101-111) was never written up. HANDOFF has no entry for it. Side fix_contract is correct. A 503 with Retry-After matches api-conventions.md:49 and the sibling operations, and a resuming client should retry rather than get an empty pending list. Severity low is correct: it only happens during a storage outage, and the ServiceUnavailable response already exists.
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I reproduced it with my own probe. The probe makes a real moto-S3 upload session (ictx) and first gets a 200 from GET /v1/uploads/{id}/parts. It then makes storage.list_parts raise OSError, and the same GET returns 503, Retry-After: 30, application/problem+json, code STORAGE_UNAVAILABLE. The documented responses are ['200','401','403','404','409','410','429'].

The code path is backend/app/services/ingest.py:452-459 (IngestService.list_parts catches any exception and raises ProblemError('STORAGE_UNAVAILABLE', Retry-After 30)). The route is backend/app/api/uploads.py:45-49 and adds no handling of its own.

The global note at contracts/openapi.yaml:13 does not cover this. It only says any operation may return 503 SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE under DB overload (ADR-0062), and this is a different code. Siblings that touch storage do document 503 with a text note: createUpload 1643, completeUpload 1713/1736, and 3593/3617 and 3629/3650. I found no review doc or ADR that already records this gap. ADR-0035 added 409 to listUploadParts but not 503.

The finder's probe tests the right thing (role 102, ictx, fresh UPLOADING session, monkeypatched list_parts). The behaviour matches api-conventions.md:49, so the contract is the side to change. Severity low is right: the 503 only appears during a storage outage, and the client gets a standard problem response with Retry-After.
- **고칠 쪽:** 계약 문구 — Add '503': {$ref: '#/components/responses/ServiceUnavailable'} to listUploadParts, with a one-line note that the storage could not list the parts and the client should retry after Retry-After. Then run validate_contracts and tests/contract (contract_changed: true).

### S46. [low] POST /v1/uploads/{upload_id}/complete — wrong-status

**Storage outage during complete_multipart_upload is reported as 409 'part list mismatch' instead of 503 STORAGE_UNAVAILABLE**

- **계약:** contracts/openapi.yaml:1704-1714 (completeUpload description): 409 CONFLICT is for '파트 목록(ETag·누락 파트)이 저장소와 맞지 않으면 … GET /v1/uploads/{upload_id}/parts로 확인해 이어 올린 뒤 다시 완료한다'. Storage failure is 503 STORAGE_UNAVAILABLE with Retry-After (1712-1713, and api-conventions.md:49 / requirement 04-1.6: storage failure → 503 + Retry-After).
- **구현:** After the part sizes and ETags have been checked against the storage listing, every exception from storage.complete_multipart_upload is caught by a bare 'except Exception' and becomes ProblemError('CONFLICT', detail='파트 목록이 저장소와 맞지 않습니다 (GET .../parts로 확인)') with no Retry-After. A connection error or timeout therefore tells the client that its part list is wrong, although the list was just verified against storage.
- **근거:** backend/app/services/ingest.py:513-525 (the comment on line 523 says '잘못된 ETag·누락 파트' but the except catches everything); backend/app/adapters/s3_storage.py:203-209 (no exception classification)
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_upl_a.py::test_complete_storage_outage_status — complete during storage outage -> 409 None {"code":"CONFLICT","detail":"파트 목록이 저장소와 맞지 않습니다 (GET .../parts로 확인)"} (assert 409 == 503). Monkeypatched complete_multipart_upload raises ConnectionError; moto ictx fixture, user 102.
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I read the contract and the current code and could not refute the finding. Contract: completeUpload (contracts/openapi.yaml:1704-1714) limits 409 CONFLICT to a part list that does not match storage (ETag or missing part), where the client should check GET .../parts and resume. It also lists 503 ServiceUnavailable for this operation (line 1736). Requirement 04-ingestion-api/requirements.md:34 says a storage failure gives 503 STORAGE_UNAVAILABLE with Retry-After. Code: backend/app/services/ingest.py:513-519 has just checked the part sizes and ETags against a fresh list_parts, so the part list is known to be correct. Lines 520-524 still wrap storage.complete_multipart_upload in a bare `except Exception` (the comment says '잘못된 ETag·누락 파트') and raise ProblemError('CONFLICT', detail=mismatch) with no Retry-After. backend/app/adapters/s3_storage.py:203-209 passes botocore exceptions through unchanged. So a connection error, timeout or S3 5xx at this step becomes a 409 that says the part list is wrong. The rest of the same service maps storage faults to 503 + Retry-After: list_parts (453-458) and _check_part_sizes (480-485). Nothing marks this as known or decided: no item about it in HANDOFF, docs/review-2026-11-05, docs/review-2026-11-12 (K3 / ADR-0053 only covers the retry after a commit failure, via _completed_in_storage) or the ADRs. The client impact is observable: examples/python/marine_client.py:45-51 retries only 429, 503, or 409 with Retry-After. A 409 with no Retry-After is treated as final, so a short storage outage at completion stops the upload for good. The session itself stays UPLOADING (no _release on this path), so data is not lost, and the outage window is narrow (just after a successful list_parts). Severity low and side fix_impl are right. The contract text is correct and the general convention supports 503.
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I reproduced it independently, one layer lower than the finder's probe. The finder monkeypatched the adapter method to raise a plain ConnectionError. My probe patches the real boto3 client inside the S3 adapter (ictx, moto, user 102 with upload:write; parts are uploaded through the presigned URLs and pass the size and ETag checks). It raises genuine botocore EndpointConnectionError and ReadTimeoutError. Both cases give `409 CONFLICT detail='파트 목록이 저장소와 맞지 않습니다 (GET .../parts로 확인)'`, with no Retry-After header and the session still UPLOADING. When the client is restored, the same request gives 202 RECEIVED. Cause: backend/app/services/ingest.py:520-525 wraps storage.complete_multipart_upload in a bare `except Exception` that always raises CONFLICT, and backend/app/adapters/s3_storage.py:203-209 does not sort exceptions by kind. Lines 513-518 have already checked every ETag and part number against ListParts before this call, so a real part-list mismatch almost never reaches this except. In practice it catches storage failures. Contract: contracts/openapi.yaml:1704-1705 ties 409 to a mismatch between the part list and storage, and says to fix it via GET .../parts. 503 is a listed response for this operation (line 1736). api-conventions.md maps storage failure to STORAGE_UNAVAILABLE 503 + Retry-After. One caveat: the contract's 503 sentence (1712-1713) names only "cannot confirm sizes" and "cannot clean up". It does not name a failure of the complete call itself, so the text does not spell this exact case out. Its 409 wording still clearly does not cover it. Impact is small, so severity stays low. The error rolls back the transaction, so run_idempotent stores no record. The agent's fixed key 'complete-<upload_id>' therefore gets no replayed 409. Also, agent handleProblem (agent/internal/core/agent.go:728) retries CONFLICT the same way as STORAGE_UNAVAILABLE (it just uses its own backoff when Retry-After is missing). A client or operator who believes the 409 detail would look for a part-list problem that does not exist, and no Retry-After is sent. The side is right as fix_impl.
- **고칠 쪽:** 구현 — In complete(): map botocore ClientError codes InvalidPart / InvalidPartOrder / NoSuchUpload (part-list problems) to 409 CONFLICT, and map every other exception (connection, timeout, 5xx) to ProblemError('STORAGE_UNAVAILABLE', headers={'Retry-After': '30'}) with the session kept. A retry after a lost response is already handled by _completed_in_storage. Regression test: monkeypatch complete_multipart_upload to raise ConnectionError, expect 503 + Retry-After and the upload still UPLOADING.

### S47. [low] POST /v1/uploads — data-semantics

**Part URL lists (parts in createUpload, pending_parts in listUploadParts) are silently cut to 1000 entries; the contract does not say so**

- **계약:** contracts/openapi.yaml:1598-1600 '수집기 범위·원천 장비 정책·쿼터 검사 후 파트 URL(기본 15분)을 준다'. UploadCreated (6287-6300) has part_count (1..10000) and parts: array of PartUrl, with no description of a cap. listUploadParts summary (1681) '완료된 파트와 남은 파트의 새 URL (재개용)'. PartList.pending_parts (6317) is also undocumented. Requirement 04-1.1 says '파트별 Part_URL'.
- **구현:** _part_urls returns only numbers[:settings.max_part_urls_per_response] (default 1000) for both createUpload.parts and listUploadParts.pending_parts. With the default 64 MiB part size, any file above about 62.5 GiB gets part_count > 1000 but only 1000 URLs, and nothing in the response says the list is partial. The Go agent, the Python example client and the web Uppy client all re-fetch /parts when the list is short (agent/internal/core/agent.go:534, examples/python/marine_client.py:302, web/src/pages/Upload.tsx signPart refresh), so this is intended behaviour. Only the contract is silent, so a client written from the contract alone would upload 1000 parts and then get 400 incomplete_parts at complete.
- **근거:** backend/app/services/ingest.py:364-371,373-376,467; backend/app/config.py:91; contracts/openapi.yaml:1598,1681,6287-6319
- **재현:** backend/tests/integration/test_zz_upl_a.py::test_part_url_cap — part_count 1500 len(parts) 1000; pending_parts 1000 completed 0 (ictx with upload_part_size 5 MiB, byte_size 1500*5 MiB). AssertionError on len(parts) == part_count.
- **읽기 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I read the code and the contract myself and the finding holds. backend/app/services/ingest.py:364-371 `_part_urls` returns `numbers[:self.c.settings.max_part_urls_per_response]`, and backend/app/config.py:91 sets that to 1000. Both createUpload (`_created_view`, ingest.py:373-376) and listUploadParts (`list_parts`, ingest.py:461-467, `pending_parts`) go through it. ingest.py:265-266 sets part_size = max(upload_part_size (64 MiB, config.py:89), ceil(size/10000)), so part_count can be up to 10000. Any file above about 62.5 GiB, or any deployment with a smaller part size, gets part_count > 1000 and only the first 1000 URLs. The response carries no flag or text saying the list is partial.

The contract says nothing about this cap. createUpload's description (openapi.yaml:1598-1609), UploadCreated (6287-6300), PartList (6302-6319, pending_parts is a bare array of PartUrl) and the listUploadParts summary (1681) never mention it. A grep of openapi.yaml for a part-URL cap or for "다시 받" finds nothing; the only 1000 limits there belong to unrelated fields. No ADR, steering file, review document or HANDOFF entry records it either: a grep of docs/.kiro/HANDOFF for max_part_urls or for the clients' wording "URL 수가 한도" finds nothing. Requirement 04-1.1 (.kiro/specs/04-ingestion-api/requirements.md:29) says "파트별 Part_URL", which reads as one URL for every part.

The cap is intended. agent/internal/core/agent.go:534 (`if len(pending) < created.PartCount { // 응답의 URL 수가 한도로 잘렸으면 목록을 다시 받는다`) and examples/python/marine_client.py:302 both handle the short list on purpose. So fix_contract is the right side.

I also checked a possible second problem in the resume path, and there is none. backend/app/adapters/s3_storage.py:190-201 pages through S3 ListParts with PartNumberMarker, so completed_parts is not cut at 1000. Each /parts call after uploading a batch returns the next 1000 pending URLs, so the loop does finish.

Severity stays low: the shipped clients handle the cap, and only a client written from the contract alone would hit 400 incomplete_parts on files over about 62.5 GiB. The finder's probe sets byte_size to 1500×5 MiB with a 5 MiB part size in ictx and asserts len(parts) == part_count. That tests exactly what it claims, and its output (part_count 1500, parts 1000, pending_parts 1000) matches the code.
- **실행 검증:** 반증 못 함 — I reproduced it with my own probe, separate from the finder's. With max_part_urls_per_response monkeypatched to 3 on ictx (5 MiB parts) and a 5-part file, POST /v1/uploads returned 201 with part_count 5 and parts [1, 2, 3]. The response has no field (only asset_id, expires_at, part_count, part_size, parts, status, upload_id) and no text saying the list is partial. Completing with only those 3 parts gave 400 VALIDATION_ERROR (errors[].code incomplete_parts). GET /parts then returned completed [1, 2, 3] and pending [4, 5]. Completing with all 5 parts gave 202 RECEIVED.

The code matches: backend/app/services/ingest.py:364-371 (_part_urls slices numbers[:limit]), used by _created_view at :373-376 and list_parts at :467; the default is backend/app/config.py:91 (1000).

The finder's probe is sound. It used the right role (user 102) and fixture (ictx, 5 MiB parts), the 1500-part declaration stays under the 10000-part ceiling, and the only assertion that failed was len(parts) == part_count.

The contract is silent about the cap: openapi.yaml createUpload description (1599-1610), listUploadParts summary (1681), and UploadCreated.parts and PartList.pending_parts (6287-6319). Nothing in HANDOFF, the 2026-11-05 and 2026-11-12 review docs, the ADRs or the 04-ingestion design records it as known.

The behaviour is intended: agent.go:534 has the comment "응답의 URL 수가 한도로 잘렸으면 목록을 다시 받는다", and the Python example client refreshes /parts when its list runs out. So fix_contract and low severity are right. The default only bites above about 62.5 GiB, and nothing is lost: the session stays usable and the client gets a 4xx.
- **고칠 쪽:** 계약 문구 — Document the cap in createUpload's description and in the UploadCreated.parts / PartList.pending_parts descriptions: 'parts holds at most max_part_urls_per_response (default 1000) URLs, starting from the lowest part numbers. If there are fewer URLs than part_count, get the rest with GET /v1/uploads/{upload_id}/parts after uploading these.' Optionally also wire MAX_PART_URLS_PER_RESPONSE into .env.example and compose if it is meant to be tunable (COMMON rule 8). contract_changed: true (description text only).

**일치를 확인한 작업**

- POST /v1/uploads — consistent apart from the part-URL cap finding. Checked: device scope (unmapped source gives 404 + DEVICE_SCOPE_VIOLATION, cross-org device/project/session gives 404 + CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT), revoked device 422, variant 422 RULE_VIOLATION with allowed_variants, C0 METADATA_ONLY 201 without parts, C0 without session 422, same file with binary 200 ALREADY_REGISTERED, binary-less C0 asset reopened with the same asset_id, valid in-progress session returned, expired session released then a new 201 (409 when locked, 503 when cleanup fails), quota 409 QUOTA_EXCEEDED, storage 503 + Retry-After, schema-version 422, source_location transform 400, Idempotency-Key replay/conflict, huge byte_size (2^63) gives 400 out_of_range on both the normal and the C0 path (not 500)
- GET /v1/uploads/{upload_id} — PROVIDER + device via upload:read; other org gets 404 + CROSS_ORG_ACCESS_ATTEMPT; PROGRAM_VIEWER and PLATFORM_OPERATOR get 403; RECEIVED and REGISTERED are told apart (asset ingest_status); response matches UploadStatus
- DELETE /v1/uploads/{upload_id} — 204 releases the reservation and deletes the binary-less asset (C0 asset reverts); a second DELETE and a DELETE after complete give 409; other org gets 404 + audit; roles 105/106 get 403; afterwards /parts gives 410

**발견 담당의 메모 (발견으로 올리지 않은 것)**

> The probes are left in place for fixers: C:/Users/user/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/69809d01a353b176/ef4ed640-70bd-41cd-a22c-ed40e2ba8d1d/scratchpad/night/wt/rv/backend/tests/integration/test_zz_upl_a.py (findings) and C:/Users/user/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/69809d01a353b176/ef4ed640-70bd-41cd-a22c-ed40e2ba8d1d/scratchpad/night/wt/rv/backend/tests/integration/test_zz_upl_b.py (consistency checks: cross-org on all 4 id operations gave 404 with 4 CROSS_ORG audits, roles, complete retry 202, missing parts 400, unknown ETag 409, abort/abort-again/parts-after-abort). Run them with odrun.sh rv "python -m pytest -q -p no:cacheprovider -p no:logging -s tests/integration/test_zz_upl_a.py".
> 
> Not reported because they are known or ambiguous:
> (1) Abort/expiry swallowing abort_multipart errors is review-2026-11-12 C4 (open).
> (2) The C0 reverse transition on cancel is in HANDOFF '확인 필요'.
> (3) ALREADY_REGISTERED for quarantined checksum-mismatch assets is review-2 #5 (awaiting a decision).
> (4) Lineage lost when a parent upload expires is C9.
> (5) Orphan multipart when the commit fails after POST /v1/uploads is a cost already accepted in ADR-0053.
> 
> Observations, not findings:
> (a) GET /v1/uploads/{id} after abort or expiry of a non-C0 upload reports ingest_status 'FAILED' with asset_id gone. Upload.error_code is never set anywhere (always null), although requirement 04-7.2 asks for '오류 코드'. The contract gives the field no meaning, so this is a possible owner or product question, not a contract mismatch.
> (b) A complete request whose part list omits planned parts gets 400 VALIDATION_ERROR incomplete_parts. The completeUpload text '파트 목록(ETag·누락 파트)이 저장소와 맞지 않으면 409' could be read as covering this, but 400 is documented and the wording is ambiguous.
> (c) PartList.completed_parts[].size reports the planned size, not the stored size (ingest.py:465). Because the presigned URL signs Content-Length, the two cannot differ in a real flow, so there is nothing observable.
> (d) The source_system_id of a COLLECTOR is accepted; domain.md 1장 allows a collector to be its own source.
> 
> Coverage limit: GET /v1/uploads/{id} silently reports parts_completed 0 when the storage listing fails. No 503 is documented for that operation, and the behaviour is consistent with that.
